[PAGE 002]
p. 2 Preface
Preface
In Mage Knight Board Game, players earn powerful abilities that're richly diverse and ingeniously
stackable as they face ever-stronger warriors and wizards and monsters. This fine-tuned combination of
abilities and challenges has given the game much variety and many, many fans. But whenever this much
variety and this many fans come together, you inevitably get questions, lots and lots of questions. It may
seem contradictory, but that's the prize complex games often receive for their popularity. Peruse Board-
GameGeek1
and you'll see that MKBG has certainly achieved this honor, for across its fifteen years of
stardom it has racked up many hundreds of rule questions.
Yet these questions, though myriad, are no cause for dismay. Many come from new players and the
replies help other new players. Other questions cover situations you may soon encounter yourself, or
give insight into the underlying principles of the game. Some even elicit clarifications from the designers
themselves, always a win.2
And in those rare instances where there're no definitive answers, you can at
least be guided by the consensus opinion of your fellow gamers.3
So quite arguably, these questions and
replies are a valuable database of information.
Although that value is undeniable, the database itself can be difficult to mine. Misspellings and
loose terminology often frustrate searches. Digressions, wordiness, and errors prolong reading time.
And clashing opinions can cast doubt on the conclusions that're ultimately drawn. That's where a Com-
pendium like this one comes in handy. Concise, orderly, and using standard terminology, it summarizes
questions, gives reasoned answers,4
and links you to discussions for attribution and further reading.5
With it, you can hopefully resolve your problems quickly and get back to what really matters: playing
the game.
• Credits: "Thank you!" to the designers and developers who've provided many clarifications on BGG:
Vlaada Chvátil, Paul Grogan, Filip Murmak, and Phil Pettifer.6
And to the prolific posters on BGG.7
• Lost Legion: These Q&A presuppose the clarifications and component changes made in the Lost Legion
expansion. If you don't own Lost Legion or the Ultimate Edition or the recent updated version of the
MKBG base game,8
then some of the Q&A in this Compendium may seem wrong or odd to you.9
• Apocalypse Dragon: At present, this Compendium does not cover the Apocalypse Dragon expansion.
• Latest Version of this File: https://boardgamegeek.com/filepage/218596/q-compendium.
• Page Numbers: Cited page numbers are to the original releases of the rulebooks.10

[PAGE 003]
Setup p. 3
Clarifications by Mechanic
Setup
# S1:—The Dummy Is Not a Player—The dummy player does not count as a player: in solo games, the
number of Units in the Offer and the number of mana dice in the SOURCE are normally three. But if you're play-
ing a blitz scenario or playing against VOLKARE (see VOLKARE Q20), you add an extra Unit and an extra die.12
Q2:—Number of Map Tiles—Do the map tiles that you place on the map during setup count toward the
number of map tiles specified for use during that scenario? And are they revealed?
A2: Yes. Yes. For example, the Solo Conquest scenario reads . . .
• Map Shape: Wedge
• Countryside Tiles: 7
For this scenario we use the Wedge layout (rulebook p. 12), so first you place the PORTAL tile on side A.
With the Wedge map, there's one additional tile in each succeeding tier of the map: the PORTAL tile creates the
first tier; then you expose and place two green countryside tiles to fully populate the second tier. So at the start
of Solo Conquest you have three tiles in your map—the PORTAL plus two countryside tiles—all of them already
revealed. The two countryside tiles you placed count against the seven countryside tiles in the scenario descrip-
tion, leaving you with five green countryside tiles that have yet to be explored.
If a scenario setup specifies that 11 countryside tiles are to be used with a Fully Open map shape, you
use side B of the PORTAL tile which directly connects to 3 countryside tiles: these 4 tiles—the PORTAL plus 3
countryside tiles—are placed on the map already revealed before the game begins, leaving 8 countryside tiles
left to be explored.13
S3:—Ordering Tiles—You don't always start with tile number 1 and work your way up to the higher
numbered tiles: that rule is only for the First Reconnaissance scenario (Walkthrough, p. 2). In all other scenari-
os, you shuffle the countryside tiles together and stack them on top of the (separately shuffled) core tiles.14
S4:—Choosing Map Tiles—When a scenario setup says "Countryside tiles: 7", it means you shuffle all
of the countryside tiles together (1 through 11 if playing without the Lost Legion expansion; 1 through 14 if
playing with it), and blindly draw 7 of them for use in the scenario, and in the scenario they'll be randomly or-
dered. You're not restricted to using only tile numbers 1 through 7.15
S5:—Placing Initial Map Tiles—When setting up a scenario and laying out the already revealed tiles,
you choose where the tiles go before revealing them, not the other way around.16


[PAGE 004]
p. 4 Dummy Player, Turn Start
Dummy Player
S1:—Skills—The dummy player adds Skills to the Common Skill Offer only during solo games.17
S2:—Starting Mana Tokens—The dummy player begins the game with mana tokens matching the col-
ored dots on the bottom of his hero card, one for each dot. An additional mana token gets added to these at the
end of every Round. These tokens are not true "mana", but are simply markers indicating how many additional
Deed cards the dummy player will flip when, on his turn, he flips over his third card and it matches one of the
colors of the marker crystals.
The only other purpose these colored dots serve is to show which crystals a player begins with when us-
ing the "Starting at a Higher Level" variant: see Variants S2.18
Player-controlled Mage Knights do not begin the game with mana crystals matching these dots.
S3:—End of Round—The dummy player announces the end of the Round only if, when he begins his
turn, his Deed deck is empty. If, as he's flipping cards, he empties his Deed deck, he does not announce the end
of the Round: he'll do that on his next turn. If the dummy player is the one who announces the end of the Round,
all non-dummy players get one more turn.19
Cross References: Setup S1; CITIES S16; Variants S2; Scenarios Q8.
Turn Start
# S1:—Options between Turns—There are two different types of activity that can happen between turns:20
I. The "before your turn" options and the "on another player's turn" options:†
o Play a Motivation Skill.
o Plunder a VILLAGE.
o Optionally advance the shield token on Master of Chaos if it wasn't used in the previous turn.
o Use the ability of the Tactic Midnight Meditation or Sparing Power.
II. The "at the start of your turn" steps:‡
o Forced: Take back any competitive (black helmet) or cooperative (white helmet) Skill token
of yours that's in the center of the play area. See Skill Tokens S4.
o Forced: If your round order token is face-down, flip it face-up—your turn is now over.
o Forced: If your deed deck is empty and you have no cards in your hand, you must announce
the end of the round (assuming it hasn't already been announced yet) and you must forfeit
your turn (take no further actions). See End of Round S1.
o Optional: If your deed deck is empty but you do have cards in your hand, you may forfeit
your turn and announce the end of the round (per the above bullet), but only if you wish to.
o Forced: If you haven't skipped or forfeited your turn and are on a MAGICAL GLADE, gain a
gold (day)/black (night) mana token.
†
- You can't take any of these options during your End-of-Turn sequence, or before Tactic selection at
the start of a new Round, or after your final turn of a Round. But you can take these options when Fully or Par-
tially Attending a PvP combat. Note also that the terms "before your turn" and "on another player's turn" are
interchangeable terms for describing this group of options.21
See Miscellaneous S5.
‡
- This set of steps do not occur when a player only Partially Attends a PvP combat: see the partially
attending entry—these steps will only happen when that player finally does take her full turn.


[PAGE 005]
Mana p. 5
Mana
# Q1:—The Free Mana Die—Does "taking a mana die from the SOURCE", as stated in the strong effect of
the card Mana Draw, count as your free one-allowed die from the SOURCE?
A1: No. Any effect that tells you to "take a die" from the SOURCE is a separate effect from you taking
your one free die from the SOURCE. But do note that the phrase “pay a mana” is not the game instructing you to
“take a free mana die from the SOURCE”.22
Q2:—Taking Dice from the SOURCE—If an effect allows you to use "additional dice" from the SOURCE,
can you use those dice even if you don't use your one free die from the SOURCE?
A2: Yes. So for the basic effect of Mana Pull, you could use a black mana die from the SOURCE as any
color even if you haven't used your standard free SOURCE die.23
See also Mana Draw S1.
S3:—Day and Night Restrictions—You cannot use black mana during the day and you cannot use gold
mana at night—unless explicitly permitted otherwise, like when in a DUNGEON/TOMB or with the basic effect of
Mana Pull. And it doesn't matter what form the mana is in—mana dice or mana tokens. (Remember, there can
never be black or gold crystals.) You may find it easier to think of black mana as losing its potency during the
day when the sun is hitting it, and of gold mana as losing its potency when it's not bathed in sunlight.24
S4:—Gold Mana—During the day you can use gold mana in place of a basic color, including when pay-
ing the mana cost of an ALTAR, or when buying a Spell at a MAGE TOWER, etc.25
S5:—Gold Mana—If you take a gold mana die from the SOURCE during the day, you must immediately
choose what color it is and immediately use it. You can't take a gold mana die from the SOURCE and bring it
down into the darkness of a DUNGEON and decide what color it'll be while there. Nor can you take a gold mana
die from the SOURCE, decide it's red mana, and bring that red mana down into a DUNGEON and use it there. Tak-
ing a die from the SOURCE is only allowed if you immediately use the mana it provides.
You can, though, take a gold mana die from the SOURCE during the day, immediately use Crystallize to
make it a red mana crystal, and then bring that red mana crystal down into a DUNGEON and use it there.26
S6:—Wild Mana—You cannot use gold mana as black mana: gold mana can only assume one of the
four basic colors. Black mana, contrarily, is not wild: it cannot be used as any other color unless an effect ex-
plicitly says so, such as Mana Pull.27
Q7:—Black Mana in DUNGEONS—If you enter a DUNGEON during the day, can you use a black mana die
from the SOURCE?
A7: When in a DUNGEON/TOMB it is always night, so you can use black mana but not gold mana. And
furthermore, you can only play effects that're usable during Combat: see Combat Q2 and Energy Steal Q1.28
Q8:—Mana Tokens When It's not your Turn—What happens to a mana token that you gain while it isn't
your turn, such as the mana token you can gain from Motivation?
A8: You keep it until you use it or until the end of your upcoming turn (even if you don't execute an
End-of-Turn Sequence during that turn), whichever happens first.29
Also note that Mana crystals, even excess
ones, can't be turned into mana tokens when it isn't your player turn. See S9 and Q10 below.30
S9:—Surplus Crystals—Unused mana tokens are lost during step 3a of your End-of-Turn sequence. If
after this point you gain a fourth crystal of a basic color from a MINE or as a combat reward, you don't gain any-
thing from it, not even a mana token. But any mana tokens you gain after step 3a (noting the restrictions in the
previous sentence) will stay in your possession until you use them or until step 3a at the end of your next turn,
whichever comes first. You cannot turn crystals into usable mana outside of your turn, even during your End-
of-Turn sequence, but you can do so while engaging in PvP combat, even while only partially attending.31


[PAGE 006]
p. 6 Mana
Q10:—Mana Tokens vs. Mana Crystals—I can't figure out what a mana token is, whether it's a die that
gets returned to the SOURCE at the end of the turn, a crystal that goes into your inventory, or something else.
A10: "Pure Mana" is what you use to power the strong effect of action cards, to power spells, etc.32
A mana die represents pure mana.
A mana token represents pure mana.
A mana crystal can be, instantly and freely, turned into pure mana at any time during your turn.
When playing Solo Conquest, you never need to turn mana crystals into pure mana outside of your turn.
(Well, practically never: see S9 just above.) Most of the time you can dispense with the idea of "pure mana" and
envision that "a red mana die" = "a red mana token" = "a red mana crystal", unless some effect specifically
mentions mana dice, or mana tokens, or mana crystals. And there're various restrictions as to when you can ac-
cess mana dice in the SOURCE (but you probably already know those).
Or perhaps your question is more basic. If so, then know that you use the colored-plastic-mana-thingies
that come with the game to represent both mana crystals and mana tokens. If a colored-plastic-mana-thingy is
sitting on the table in your play area, then it's a mana token. If it's sitting on your hero card in the inventory box,
then it's a mana crystal. Mana tokens—whether you use them during your turn or not—get returned to the bank
during your End-of-Turn sequence; mana crystals stay in your inventory until you use them.
You gain mana tokens only when a specific effect says so, such as when you're on a MAGICAL GLADE or
when you're using the basic effect of Concentration. You gain mana crystals only when a specific effect says so,
such as with either effect of Crystallize or when you're on a CRYSTAL MINES.
The way most people play is to set the mana die, mana token, or mana crystal on the deed card or Unit
that they're powering with it. If you have trouble remembering whether a specific colored-plastic-mana-thingy
represents a mana token or a mana crystal (important if you wish to take actions back) then tip all of the mana
tokens on their sides and keep all of the mana crystals standing upright—or position mana tokens toward the left
side of the card and mana crystals toward the right.33
S11:—Mana-Powered Effects—If you expend red mana or blue mana to power an effect that gives you
Attack points or Block points, those Attack points and Block points are not automatically Fire points or Ice
points: for the points to be Fire or Ice, the text describing the effect has to explicitly say that they are.34
S12:—Using SOURCE Dice—Whenever you're allowed to use a mana die from the SOURCE—no matter
what rule or effect gives you that permission (c.f., your one free mana die every turn, or the basic effect of
Mana Draw)—you may use the mana die any way you wish (assuming the effect text doesn't explicitly say oth-
erwise), such as to power the strong effect of an Action Card, or to use with the basic effect of Crystallize, or to
enable a specific ability of a Unit that requires mana, or to select a specific effect of Mana Bolt, or when paying
mana at an ALTAR, etc. See S4 and Q10 above.35
All mana dice that you took and used from the SOURCE are re-rolled and returned to the SOURCE during
your End-of-Turn sequence (unless some rule or effect explicitly states otherwise). When you re-roll a used
mana die before returning it to the SOURCE, it doesn't matter what color you roll: it's placed in the SOURCE with
its new color showing, even if that means more than half of the dice in the SOURCE are now black and/or gold.36
S13:—Setting up the SOURCE—When rolling mana dice for the SOURCE at the start of a Round, at least
half of the mana dice in the SOURCE must be basic colors: re-roll all black and all gold dice if this isn’t the case.
If there still aren't enough dice of basic colors in the SOURCE, re-roll all of the dice that are currently black and
currently gold, again and again, as necessary. This re-rolling occurs only at the start of Rounds—you never re-
roll dice in the SOURCE simply for this reason during the middle of a Round.37
S14:—DUNGEON Reward—The DUNGEON site card says "roll a mana die to determine your reward". It
means a mana die that's in the Bank (see the base game rulebook p. 3), not a die from the SOURCE.38


[PAGE 007]
Movement p. 7
Movement
# S1:—Unspent Points—Movement points gained during the Movement Phase will be lost when you be-
gin your Action Phase. And Movement points played during your Action Phase will be wasted unless some ef-
fect explicitly allows you to use them, such as Blocking Cumbersome enemies, or entering a MAZE/LABYRINTH,
or buying Attack points with the card Agility. That's because once you've begun your Action Phase, you cannot
move your figure to a new space or explore new tiles (exception: Temporal Portal).
Example:
You play Move 4 during the Movement Phase to move into a hills space that contains a MAZE, ex-
pending 3 Move points to do so. Then, to initiate your Action Phase, you declare you're entering the
MAZE. The moment you do that, the 1 remaining unspent Move point is lost. So in order to enter the
MAZE, you play the strong effect of Agility and use 2 of its 4 Move points to move into the MAZE.
Having taken a FORESTERS Unit with you, you expend it for 2 Move points to move even deeper into
the MAZE. At this moment—since you're not yet in combat—you could Refresh the FORESTERS if you
have a means of doing so (but only if the FORESTERS became spent while moving into the MAZE, for
the FORESTERS must have been unspent for you to have taken them with you into the MAZE). Then
you begin Combat. At this point, your 2 leftover Move points from Agility do carry forward because
you played them during your Action Phase. With Agility, you can convert the points into Ranged
Attack 1 or into a (non-Ranged) Attack 2.
You can play Move points during Combat for use with Agility. If you're about to get knocked out, you
can even play every card you hold as a sideways Move 1 and then later convert these Move points into Attack
points during your Attack Phase for use with a previously-played Agility card. Under normal circumstances you
likely wouldn't be able to Attack if you were knocked out; however, Move cards can be played even during the
Block Phase and those Move points will linger through the remainder of the Action Phase.39
See S11 below and Miscellaneous S9.
S2:—During Combat—You can use Wings of Wind, Underground Travel, and the move effect of Druid-
ic Staff to move into new spaces, but only during the Movement Phase. During the Action Phase (inclusive of
the Combat Phase) you can use Move points from, say, the SCOUTS Unit, but you can't enter a new space with
them or use the tile-revealing effect of that SCOUTS Unit. The only way to move your figure onto a new space
outside of the Movement Phase (except for withdrawals or retreats) is with Temporal Portal, as that card explic-
itly states that you move "as your Action". See Miscellaneous S9.40
S3:—Out-of-Order Turns—When playing your turn out of order (when you're attacked by another play-
er or by VOLKARE, or during a cooperative assault or defense), you're not allowed to move to a new space, re-
veal a map tile, or perform an Action (except for movements and actions explicitly dictated by your situation).41
S4:—Entering Fortified Sites—It always costs two movement points to enter a CITY: ignore any other
terrain showing in the CITY space. Entering an unconquered CITY is an Assault and immediately ends your
Movement Phase. As you enter an unconquered CITY, you lose one point of Reputation and must immediately
fight. If you don’t kill every enemy token in the CITY, you retreat back to the space you entered it from. This,
though, is not Forced Withdrawal—see Forced Withdrawal S1 and S2, and CITIES S3 and S10.42
With other Fortified Sites—KEEPS, MAGE TOWERS—the movement cost of entry is based on the underly-
ing terrain, but the rest of the remarks in the above paragraph also apply to those Assaults.43
S5:—Altered Terrain Costs—Some effects let you move spaces at no cost (like Underground Travel), or
at a Movement point cost that's unrelated to the terrain moved over (like Wings of Wind). In both cases you're
not paying the terrain cost of the spaces nor the cost of WALLS, and you can move over inaccessible spaces.
With effects like these, as long as you move over revealed tiles and stop on a safe space, you can pass through
mountains, lakes, Rampaging Enemies, KEEPS, MAGE TOWERS, CITIES (even unconquered), and VILLAGES. See
O9 below.44


[PAGE 008]
p. 8 Movement
Q6:—Rampaging Enemies—If you start your turn in a space adjacent to a Rampaging Enemy and spend
two Move points to explore, does this provoke the enemy?
A6: No. Only moving directly from one adjacent space to another adjacent space can provoke a Ram-
paging Enemy. See Lost Legion, p. 4, for how Walls affect this.45
S7:—The PORTAL—On the PORTAL tile (assigning N as the direction toward the top of the page, e.g., the
edge with the Forest space), on Side A the spaces W, SW, and SE of the portal space are inaccessible; on Side
B, the spaces W and SW of the portal space are inaccessible.46
S8:—Revealing Information—As you move, information is often revealed. If, during the Movement
Phase, you travel past an unconquered KEEP or MAGE TOWER during the day, or past an unconquered CITY during
either day or night, you reveal the enemy token(s) that're there. And at night, once you move onto a RUINS to-
ken, you flip the RUINS token face-up: you can keep moving farther or you can stay there, but either way the RU-
INS token gets revealed (but no enemy token(s) within that ADVENTURE SITE get revealed).47
See O9 just below, Combat S14, and End of Round S6.
O9:—Revealing Information—With some move effects, you don't pay the terrain cost of the spaces you
cross, per S5 above. Do you reveal enemy tokens and RUINS tokens (see S8 above) as though you had moved
into those spaces via the standard movement process?
A9: Definitely not for these effects:
—Space Bending: it says "ignore any spaces you leap over".
—Temporal Portal (strong effect): you "teleport" there.
—Underground Travel: thematically you're moving underground.
But a player could argue "yes" for these effects:
—Druidic Staff: A druid is "one with the land", so conceivably he learns all there is to know about
the spaces he moves through. (But Lost Legion p. 4 seems to treat this effect as a teleport.)
—Wings of Wind, Flight: You're flying over spaces, so you likely can see what's below you, perhaps
even better than if you had walked. Then again, maybe clouds or darkness limit visibility.48
As you see, making judgment calls for individual cards is fraught with ambiguities: a simpler solution is
to rule "no" for all types of movements for which you don't pay (adjusted) terrain costs. See S5 above.
Effects that simply "change the terrain cost" should reveal information in the usual way, allowing you as
you move to reveal enemy tokens and RUINS per the usual rules. These latter effects include Song of Wind, Frost
Bridge, Path Finding, Mountain Lore, Mist Form, Secret Ways, and FORESTERS. And note that with effects that
simply change the terrain costs, you do have to pay to cross WALLS.49
S10:—Applying Multiple Effects—Note this easily overlooked rule on p. 6 of the rulebook: If a player
plays more than one [effect that changes the Move costs for different types of terrain], he can apply [these ef-
fects] in any order.50
For an exception to this, see Prayer of Weather S2.
S11:—Movement Phase vs. Action Phase—You get one Movement Phase and one Action Phase every
turn. The Movement Phase always precedes the Action Phase. You can move as much as you want and explore
as many tiles as you want during the Movement Phase, but once the Action Phase begins, the Movement Phase
is over: you cannot move to a new space again during this turn, nor can you explore new tiles.
During the Movement Phase you can, as spelled out in point 6 on p. 6 of the base game rulebook, alter-
nate between moving to new spaces, exploring new tiles, and playing more movement points, in whatever order
you wish and as many times as you wish—just so long as the Action Phase hasn't begun yet.
And Combat, anytime it occurs, always take place in the Action Phase.
• If your movement triggers an automatic combat (say, by provoking Rampaging Enemies to attack
you), your Movement Phase instantly ends and your Action Phase (now a Combat Phase) begins.


[PAGE 009]
Movement p. 9
• If you (a) choose to enter an ADVENTURE SITE (which isn't the same thing as being on the same space
as an ADVENTURE SITE), or (b) challenge or provoke a Rampaging Enemy, or (c) begin to Interact
with the Locals, or (d) activate an ANCIENT ALTAR, or (e) attack another mage knight, or (f) assault a
fortified site, or (g) attempt to burn down a MONASTERY, etc., then your Movement Phase has just
ended and you're now in your Action Phase.
• There is never more than one Combat per player turn. If multiple enemies are involved in a Combat
(say, if you're Assaulting a fortified site and fighting adjacent Rampaging Enemies that were pro-
voked by your entrance), there is still only one (combined) Combat.
After the Action Phase ends, you can play healing effects and special effects, but you can't engage in
movement nor in any other Action. For instance if you Assaulted a KEEP and won, you cannot Interact with the
Locals there during the same turn: your Action Phase (which was Combat) is now over. To Interact with the Lo-
cals at a newly captured Keep, you must wait until a future turn and choose "Interact with the Locals" as your
Action during that turn.
See Combat Q1, Q2, S4, S18, and S40, Influence S7, and End of Turn S1.51
Q12:—Revealing Information—When you spend Move points to enter and Assault a Fortified Site, do
you immediately reveal any enemy tokens adjacent to that Fortified Site (say, in another adjacent CITY or KEEP),
or must you succeed in actually conquering the Fortified Site (and not being forced back) in order to reveal
those adjacent enemy tokens?
A12: Since you've paid the Movement points to enter the site, and since your Movement Phase has end-
ed and a Combat Phase has begun, it seems clear that you're "in" that space and so meet the Site Reference card
requirement for revealing adjacent tokens.52
S13:—After Revealing Information—If you play the basic effect of March for two Movement Points,
reveal a map Tile, and then wish to add a green mana to change from the basic effect to the strong effect of the
March card, it is too late. You have revealed new information and therefore cannot change earlier decisions.53
Cross References: Map Revealing S2; Combat Q1, S31, S32, S40; CITIES S10; Resting S3; Miscellane-
ous S9; Silver Units S10; FORESTERS S1.


[PAGE 010]
p. 10 Map Revealing
Map Revealing
# S1:—Initial MONASTERIES—If there're any MONASTERIES on the initially revealed tiles of a scenario, deal
an Advanced Action card for each MONASTERY to the Unit Offer before beginning Round one.54
S2:—Revealing MONASTERIES—If you have one MONASTERY on the map and there's a corresponding Ad-
vanced Action card in the Unit Offer, and someone burns that MONASTERY before anyone buys the Advanced
Action card, and then someone reveals a new tile with a MONASTERY on it, you will still add a new Advanced
Action card to the Unit Offer, giving you two there—and both of them could be bought at that single MONAS-
TERY. If nothing else changes before a new Round begins, you would then deal only one Advanced Action card
to the Unit Offer as there's now only one unburnt MONASTERY present.55
S3:—Core Tiles on Coastlines—If the map shape is "Wedge", you're not allowed to place
core tiles on the coastlines. Forgetting this rule is a common beginner mistake.56
S4:—Running out of Tiles—If there're no tiles in the tile deck when a player attempts to explore, the
player may use a random countryside tile that was set aside as "unused" during setup. If all of these countryside
tiles have been placed, the player may use any set aside non-CITY core tile instead. Tiles explored in this way
(countryside or core) must be placed adjacent to at least three other tiles.57
S5:—Running out of Tiles—You cannot use the "running out of tiles" option that was discussed in the
previous entry in order to place a countryside tile when you're playing a "Wedge" map and are exploring along
a coastline and there're only core tiles left in the prepared draw stack.58
S6:—Length of the Map—Unless a scenario states otherwise, there's no limit to the length that the map
can extend outward, just so long as the map shape is maintained and the tile placement rules are followed.59
Q7:—Being Cut Off from CITIES—When you place core tiles, you'll often reveal mountains and lakes,
especially on their southern spaces. If enough inaccessible spaces like this end up close together, an impenetra-
ble barrier can potentially be created, rendering the CITIES beyond it unreachable. How, then, do you reach those
CITIES?
A7: This can happen and it is part of the game. The key is to make different choices earlier in the game:
(1) collect better Movement effects—Spells or Action cards or Skills—that let you fly over, burrow
under, travel through, or teleport past mountains and lakes,
(2) manage your tile placement better so that when the core tiles come out and a barrier does appear,
you can place an alternate route to get around that barrier, or
(3) use the additional tile placing option discussed in S4 above to skirt the inaccessible terrain.
If you don't plan ahead, then I guarantee that at some point you will face this predicament.60


[PAGE 011]
Combat p. 11
Combat
# Q1:—When Combat Begins—:61
A1: Combat is usually initiated by a player's intentional decision, a decision that's often indicated by re-
vealing an enemy token. Examples:
a. A player expends movement points to enter an unconquered KEEP/MAGE TOWER.62
The moment those
Movement points are used, the Movement Phase ends and the Combat Phase begins. Healing effects can no
longer be played and if the enemy garrisoning that site hasn't yet been revealed, it's revealed now. See Move-
ment S8, and Combat S3 and S14.
b. A player decides to challenge an adjacent Rampaging Enemy. Combat begins immediately.
c. A player moves to a space that provokes an adjacent Rampaging Enemy. Combat begins immediately.
Anything he does in the new space is done while he is in Combat. See Movement S1, Q6, and S11.
d. While standing on an ADVENTURE SITE a player declares he is entering that ADVENTURE SITE. Enemy
token(s) are revealed and combat begins immediately. See Q2 and S18 below.
e. A player standing on a MAZE/LABYRINTH chooses to enter it. His Movement Phase ends and his Action
Phase begins—but he is not yet in Combat. Any unspent Movement points vanish, for the player has begun his
Action Phase. He must now play and pay 2/4/6 new Movement points to walk into the MAZE/LABYRINTH. If he
plays more Movement points than he uses, those points will carry over into the Combat Phase.63
At this point
the player is still not in Combat: Combat begins only when he draws an enemy token.64
Any Heal points generated during the Movement Phase do carry over into the Action Phase, and are
available even while walking through a MAZE/LABYRINTH. However any unspent Heal points are immediately
lost when a player begins Combat. See Miscellaneous S9.
Q2:—When Combat Begins—Is there a moment between entering a DUNGEON (revealing a MONSTER to-
ken) and the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase of Combat, a moment during which you can play Healing effects?
A2: No. As soon as the MONSTER token is revealed, you are in Combat. See Q1 just above and Energy
Steal Q1. And at the moment that the Combat Phase ends, you are no longer in the DUNGEON.65
S3:—Healing during Combat—The Rejuvenate card has a hand symbol in the upper left corner (and on-
ly a hand symbol), so even its "non-healing" effects are prohibited during Combat. See Rejuvenate Q1.
The readying effect of Inspiration is a hand-symbol effect (as indicated by the hand icon on the Skill
description card), and so cannot be played during Combat.66
S4:—Combat Overview—:
The Combat Phase has four sub-phases: 67
Ranged and Siege Attack Phase
Block Phase
Assign Damage Phase
Attack Phase
Once Combat begins, you cannot withdraw from it—you must complete it. You will execute the four-
phase sequence exactly once, no matter how many enemy tokens you're facing.68
All sub-phases are voluntary
except the Assign Damage Phase, which is mandatory.
First you do the Ranged and Siege Attack Phase: you may attack none, any, or all of the enemy tokens
with Ranged Attacks (if they're unfortified) and/or Siege Attacks (if they aren't fortified twice). Group the ene-
my tokens however you wish. Any enemy tokens that you kill are immediately removed from the Combat.
Next is the Block Phase: Block as many or as few enemy Attacks as you wish, but each separate Attack
must be handled one at a time—even if those Attacks all come from a single enemy token.
After you've made all of your Blocks, next comes the Assign Damage Phase: any enemy Attacks that
were not fully Blocked (ignore the Attacks of enemies that were killed during earlier phases) are now assigned
to your Units and/or Mage Knight. You choose the order in which to process these unblocked enemy Attacks.


[PAGE 012]
p. 12 Combat
Last is the Attack Phase: you may Attack as many enemy tokens as you wish, grouping them however
you wish, without regard for how you may have grouped them during the Ranged and Siege Attack Phase.
You go through this four-phase sequence exactly once, and all (unkilled) enemy tokens are present dur-
ing each phase. Attack them or don't attack them, it's up to you, but all of those that weren't killed or that
weren't fully Blocked during the Block Phase will assign damage to you during the Assign Damage Phase (see
ORC SUMMONERS S1). If any enemy tokens survive the entire Combat Phase, see S18 below and CITIES S23.
S4a:—Blocking—If an enemy has a physical Attack of 4, you will need 4 Block points to block it. That
Block can be of any type, for any type of Block is efficient against a physical attack.
If you're facing a Fire Attack, only Ice or Cold-Fire Blocks are efficient against it; physical Blocks and
Fire Blocks are only half effective against it. So if an enemy attacks you with a 4 Fire Attack, you'll need a 4 Ice
Block or a 4 Cold-Fire Block, or a physical Block 8 or a Fire Block 8, or some combination that totals 4 (with
Ice and Cold-Fire Blocks counting fully, and others counting half).
Armor values have nothing to do with blocking. All Blocks are done before any Damage is assigned.69
S4b:—Assigning Damage—If a Cold-Fire Attack of 9 hits you, you do not take 18 points of damage—
you take only 9 points of damage, for at this point an elemental Attack is no different than a physical Attack.
The only exception in this case is if you assign the Cold-Fire damage to a Unit that has both Ice and Fire
Resistance: in that case the Unit deducts its Armor value from the damage without taking a Wound. If any Dam-
age remains, the Unit deducts its Armor value a second time and then takes a Wound—see S34 below.
S4c:—Attacking—If an enemy token has no resistances, you only need to do damage equal to its Armor:
any type of damage will do. Against 5 Armor, an attack of Attack 2 + Fire Attack 3 will kill it. And a 2 Ice At-
tack delivers the same amount of damage as a plain (physical) 2 Attack.70
If an enemy has 5 Armor and Physical Resistance, any non-elemental attack will be halved. Against this
enemy, your Fire Attack 3 is still 3 damage, but the physical Attack 2 is halved to 1 damage, making your total
damage now 4—not enough to kill it.
Other resistances work similarly: if an enemy is Resistant to Fire, then all Fire Attacks are halved, but
physical or Ice or Cold-Fire Attacks are at full strength.
Example: You use a 6 Ranged Fire Attack (from AMOTEP GUNNERS) and a 4 Ice Siege Attack (from ICE
MAGES) against an unfortified enemy. If the enemy has no Resistances, this will be a combined Attack of 10
(6+4). If the enemy has Ice Resistance, it'll be an Attack of 8 (6+2); if the enemy has Fire Resistance, it'll be an
Attack of 7 (3+4); if the enemy has both Fire and Ice Resistance, it'll be an Attack of 5 (3+2).
S5:—Types of Attack—MKBG can mean varying things by "types of attack/block", including . . .
(a) the "modes of attack" that appear on the left-side of enemy tokens (physical, fire, ice, cold-
fire, summons, multiple attacks);
(b) the "elements of the attack" (fire, ice, cold-fire); and
(c) the "distance of the attack" (siege, ranged, or regular "close-ranged" attacks).
Sometimes a phrase encompasses multiple meanings. Concentration, Will Focus, Deadly Aim, and Am-
bush include both the element and the distance meanings, while Spirit Guides and Shapeshift are referring only
to the elements of the attack. When an effect says "reduce one enemy attack by 1", it's referring to all kinds of
attack—ice attack, cold-fire siege attack, physical ranged attack, etc. Context is your guide.
When an effect provides "Attack 2" or "Block 5", that indicates physical Attacks and physical Blocks.
When the Action Card Swiftness says "Ranged Attack 3", it means physical Ranged Attack 3. Even if an Attack
or Block requires mana to generate it, the Attack or Block it provides is physical unless the effect text explicitly
states something like "Fire Attack 3" or "Ice Block 3" or "Cold-Fire Siege Attack 3". See Mana S11.71
At times you'll come across abilities that affect only non-elemental Attacks and Blocks. These abilities
will use the word "physical" in their phrasing—see, for example, the strong effect of the Sword of Justice.
See Cold Toughness S1 where enemy abilities are differentiated from enemy attack types.72


[PAGE 013]
Combat p. 13
S6:—Attack Types during the Attack Phase—During the Attack Phase there is no difference between
Attacks, Ranged Attacks, and Siege Attacks: they're all treated as plain Attacks. So if you use Leadership dur-
ing the Attack Phase, you gain +2 regardless of what type of Attack is shown on the Unit. And if you use Vial of
Toxin during the Attack Phase, you gain +3 to the Attack regardless of what "range" of Attack it is applied to—
Ranged, Siege, or just a plain Attack—just so long as it's a physical Attack. See S5 above.73
Q7:—Resistances versus Multi-Card Plays—When you play Concentration, Will Focus, or Maximal
Effect and then a follow-on card, which of the played cards decides "the color of the card" when facing enemy
tokens with Fire or Ice Resistance?
A7: The second card played is what matters: the first card is simply enabling the second card or making
it better. So using the strong effect of (red) Maximal Effect to play (blue) Ice Shield against an enemy with Fire
Resistance lets the Ice Shield armor reduction apply—but not if the enemy has Ice Resistance. See S33 below.74
Q8:—Spell Colors—Is a Spell that's powered by red mana considered "a red card" for purposes of Re-
sistance and Special Effects?
A8: Yes. For example if a Spell powered by red mana says “Fire Attack 4, and the target melts into
goo”, then an enemy with Fire Resistance halves the Fire Attack to 2 but it won't turn gooey.75
S9:—Fortified Sites—MONASTERIES are not fortified. MAGE TOWERS, KEEPS, and CITIES are fortified.76
S10:—Mage Knights in Fortified Sites—Mage Knights and their Units gain no benefit from being in a
fortified spot when they're attacked by enemy tokens.
There's an exception, though, for PvP combat.77
And, by extension, a DEFENDER'S Units should be pro-
tected against attacks by an AGGRESSOR'S ILLUSIONISTS Unit that might otherwise prevent the DEFENDER'S Units
from "attacking during this combat". However fortification does not apply when VOLKARE attacks a player.78
S11:—WALLS—If you engage a Rampaging Enemy across a WALL and the enemy has the Unfortified
ability, you can attack it with Ranged Attacks. Thematically, some enemies take advantage of a WALL; others
cross it to fight you. An enemy token inside a Fortified Site (like a KEEP) that's attacked across a WALL hexside
is doubly fortified: but if it has the Unfortified ability, both site Fortifications are canceled for that enemy token.
See also the next two entries.79
S12:—Grouping Enemies—When you group several enemy tokens together for an attack, those enemy
tokens don't share abilities or resistances with each other. Rather . . .
(1) if any member of the group has fortification, that restricts the types of attacks that can be played
against the group during the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase; and
(2) if any member of the group has Fire, Ice, or Physical Resistance, the strength of any Fire, Ice, or
Physical Attacks (respectively) played against the group is halved. Cold-Fire Attacks are halved
only when at least one enemy token has both Fire and Ice Resistance.80
These are the only two qualities—fortifications and resistances—which extend from one enemy token to
affect the entire group.81
So remember . . .
• If fortification prohibits an attack against a member token, that attack is prohibited against the group.
• If resistance halves an attack against a member token, that attack is halved against the group.
If an enemy token in an attacked group has the Elusive ability, the armor values of the other enemy to-
kens remain as they were. Similarly, the Defend ability doesn't propagate to other enemy tokens, nor does Ar-
cane Immunity, nor the Unfortified ability (see S13 below), nor cumbersome, etc. Immunity against non-Attack
/non-Block effects (which enemy tokens with Ice Resistance have against blue cards and against Unit abilities
powered by blue mana) does not spread to other enemy tokens in the same group, nor does the corresponding
immunity of Fire Resistance. Only effects (1) and (2) listed above will apply to the entire group.82


[PAGE 014]
p. 14 Combat
S13:—Grouping Fortified Enemies—During the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase, if you attack a forti-
fied site that has two enemies, one with a fortified symbol on its counter and the other with an unfortified
symbol on its counter, and you group them together, you won't be able to attack them during the Ranged-
and-Siege-Attack Phase—not with Ranged Attacks and not with Siege Attacks—because there's at least one
enemy token in the group that's doubly Fortified. [The rulebook (p. 8) says it's not that these attacks are ineffec-
tive, it's that you cannot play any attacks during the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase if all enemy tokens are
doubly Fortified. Lost Legion relaxed some of the restrictions on when you can play effects: in general, cards
and Skills and Units can be played so long as you respect the sequence of play, even when you don't make use
of their beneficial effects—but it didn't remove the attack prohibition for doubly Fortified targets. See Miscella-
neous S1 and Vial of Toxin S2.] Similarly, if any enemy token in a group is singly fortified (after all site and
ability effects are applied), you can't play Ranged Attacks against that group. See S11 above, and CITIES Q2 for
an example with CRYPT WORMS.83
If you group two enemy tokens for a combined attack, one of which is fortified from being inside a forti-
fied site, and the other of which is fortified from being behind a WALL or from having the fortified symbol on its
counter (but not both), then you can attack this group with a Siege Attack. Their having single fortification from
two different sources does not make the group doubly fortified.84
If you group a fortified Armor 3 enemy with an unfortified Armor 5 enemy, and play Siege Attack 4
against them during the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase, you are not then allowed to play a Ranged Attack 4
effect to finish killing off the unfortified enemy token. The group is killed—or not killed—as a whole: as long
as they are grouped, only Siege Attacks are permitted against them during the entire Ranged-and-Siege-Attack
Phase. Groups cannot, in general, be killed piecemeal by Attacks.85
S14:—Rampaging Enemies during Assaults—If at night you Assault a KEEP/MAGE TOWER with Rampag-
ing Enemies adjacent to it that won't be provoked by your entry, you must declare whether you're challenging
them before you reveal the defending enemy token in the KEEP/MAGE TOWER. See CITIES S3.86
S15:—Reputation—Killing DRACONUM in LABYRINTHS/TOMBS gains you Fame and possibly site re-
wards, but it doesn't gain you a Reputation bonus.87
Q16:—Reputation—When do you adjust your Reputation for Assaults on fortified sites, for killing Ram-
paging Enemies, and for killing HEROES and THUGS?
A16: When you make an Assault, you lose one point of Reputation at the moment you enter the fortified
space (unless you're already on the "X" space of the Reputation track). You apply Reputation adjustments for
killing enemy tokens whenever you defeat them—at the same time that you gain the Fame for doing so.88
See
Influence O3, Enemy Tokens: HEROES S1, and Silver Units: THUGS O3.89
S17:—Burning MONASTERIES—If you attempt to burn a MONASTERY, you cannot combine that attempt
with combat against Rampaging Enemies, either by challenging them yourself or by provoking them by your
move. If your move provoked them, you cannot attempt to burn the MONASTERY this turn.90
If someone burns a MONASTERY and marks it with their shield token, treat that spot as if it's an empty
space of the indicated terrain type: anyone can enter it, but no one can Interact with the Locals there.91
S18:—Remaining on ADVENTURE SITES—If you explore an ADVENTURE SITE (even those with two enemy
tokens like SPAWNING GROUNDS or some RUINS tokens) and don't defeat all of the enemy tokens, you neverthe-
less remain on that space at the end of Combat. During your following turn, you can explore the ADVENTURE
SITE again, or even remain on the space and choose not to explore it. See Miscellaneous S14.92
S19:—Forced Withdrawal—A failed Assault of a fortified site forces you back to the hex you came
from. This is not Forced Withdrawal. Forced Withdrawal only happens during the End-of-Turn sequence: it can
apply after a failed Assault, but only if the space you retreat to isn't a safe space. Retreating from a fortified site


[PAGE 015]
Combat p. 15
after a failed combat doesn't use Movement points or provoke Rampaging Enemies; the same is true for Forced
Withdrawals from unsafe spaces. See Forced Withdrawal S1 and S2.93
S20:—Poison and Knockout—The extra Poison damage that goes into your discard pile does not count
toward the knockout rule. See Blood Ritual S3.94
S21:—Knockout—If you play Blood Rage and the Wound you get from it knocks you out, you still get
to play the Attack 5 or 9 that the card gives you—you can't, though, add any more Attack card effects after this
point (unless you somehow manage to draw more cards after you get knocked out). If you played Blood Rage
by means of Maximal Effect, you could take up to three Wounds while resolving the effect. If so, you might
take more Wounds than your hand size limit before the knockout takes effect.95
Q22:—Knockout/Paralyze—If you get knocked out or paralyzed, can you use Motivation to draw cards?
A22: Yes. I Feel No Pain also works. Being knocked out or paralyzed causes you to discard non-wound
Deed cards—but you can still use Skills/Units/mana, and any enduring effects of cards played earlier continue
to apply. Between turns, you can also plunder VILLAGES.96
Note: you can get knocked out only once a turn.97
S23:—SUMMONERS—To cancel a summoning attack by ORC SUMMONERS, ILLUSIONISTS, DRAGON SUM-
MONER, or SHROUDED NECROMANCER, you must do so at the start of the Block Phase before the SUMMONER draws
the MONSTER/ORC token(s). After that point new information has been revealed, so you can't change a previous
decision (i.e., your decision not to cancel the SUMMONERS'S summoning attack). See ORC SUMMONERS S1.98
Official Clarification: The attack (from a SUMMONER) itself isn't an enemy. If you want to cancel a SUM-
MONER'S attack, you have to do so before you draw the summoned token. If you do draw the summoned token,
then you have to block/assign damage. (In retrospect, I would say "Summon Attack" isn't the best name for it. It
gives an impression that you are facing two enemy tokens, which isn't true.)99
Implication: the summoned MONSTER/ORC is the SUMMONER'S Attack, or perhaps even more precisely,
"the Attack of the summoned MONSTER/ORC" is the SUMMONER'S Attack. Once the MONSTER/ORC token has been
revealed, its Attack cannot be canceled: you can Block the Attack or reduce the Attack, but you cannot "cancel"
the Attack or "prevent that MONSTER/ORC from Attacking". You've already had one bite at the apple. You can,
though, modify the Attack of the summoned MONSTER/ORC or even directly "kill" the MONSTER/ORC (say, with
Exploding Shield), although you gain no Fame from doing so. See Banner of Fear Q6.100
[Effects that kill a summoned MONSTER/ORC or modify its characteristics have no further effect once the
MONSTER/ORC disappears—but effects that have an action that targets "the same enemy" during the ensuing At-
tack Phase, in my opinion, should be usable against the SUMMONER itself. See Dueling S2, Taunt O2, Ice Shield
S3, and Shield Bash Q6. The vampiric ability, though, should not affect the armor of the SUMMONER.]101
Q24:—Arcane Immunity—Since you can affect the Attacks of an enemy with Arcane Immunity, can
you also affect the Attacks of an enemy with Ice Resistance by using a blue card or a blue-mana ability?
A24: No. Arcane Immunity works differently than Ice Resistance. Enemies with Arcane Immunity are
immune to magic, yet their attacks are not. See Arcane Immunity S6.102
S25:—Cumbersome—ZOMBIE HORDE (from Shades of TEZLA) has three physical Cumbersome Attacks of
1. If you use Movement points to Block all three, you need 3 Movement points. But—unlike with Block cards
—you don't have to get the points from three different Movement cards. See Miscellaneous S9.
With Cumbersome, any Movement points you pay will reduce the attack. Even if you don't reduce it to
zero, the damage from the attack will still be lessened. And note that Movement points reduce Cumbersome At-
tack points before they're doubled for Swiftness, a consideration when Nature's Vengeance is used.103
S26:—The Defend Symbol—The Elusive symbol (which exactly doubles the armor value) and the De-
fend symbol (always either 1 or 2) are similar. Entry CRYSTAL SPRITES S1 demonstrates both symbols.104


[PAGE 016]
p. 16 Combat
S27:—The Defend Ability—Only one Defend effect can be applied to a given enemy token. Although all
three enemies in the third Defend example in Shades of TEZLA (p. 2) have Defend, only two of them can apply
Defend during the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase because only two enemy tokens are being attacked during
that phase. You get to choose which enemy tokens use their Defend abilities, so be smart! In the example, the
Defend abilities of both CRYSTAL SPRITES are used—one on a CRYSTAL SPRITES and the other on the ELVEN PRO-
TECTORS. The ELVEN PROTECTORS are killed during the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase, and so will not be pres-
ent during the Attack Phase to apply their Defend ability to any newly attacked enemy token. During the Attack
Phase, the lone remaining enemy token—the second CRYSTAL SPRITES (which has already applied its Defend
ability to protect the now-dead ELVEN PROTECTORS)—receives no Defend boost to its armor. See S29 below.105
S28:—The Defend Ability—
Q: Is an actual attack of Attack points needed to trigger the Defend ability? A: Yes.
Q: When does the armor-increase-given-by-the-Defend-ability get assigned to an enemy token? A: After
at least one Attack point (even if it's inefficient and halved) is irrevocably assigned to a specific enemy token or
group of tokens. You are permitted to attack an enemy token or group of enemy tokens with an attack that's in-
sufficient to defeat them, and doing so is enough to justify attaching a Defend bonus to the attacked tokens.
Q: Does Tremor (which reduces the armor of enemy tokens) constitute an Attack that forces Defend to
be assigned to particular targets? A: No: Attack points are needed.
O: Can I assign some Attack points to an enemy token, thereby forcing Defend to react, then play Trem-
or to lower its armor, and then play enough additional Attack points so that, together with the points I played
previously, I have enough Attack points to kill that enemy token, all during the same Attack Phase? A: Not
quite. In general, Armor reductions and similar effects are applied first (that is, immediately106
), and only after-
ward do you apply any Attack points. Defend kicks in when Attack points are applied (that is, after Tremor ar-
mor reductions). See O46 and S49 below, Basic Action Cards S2, and Sacrifice S4.
O: Can I assign one Attack point against an enemy token, apply Defend to it, then use Tremor on it, then
assign a second completely separate Attack against that same enemy token, all in the same Attack Phase? A:
No. Opinions differ, but I believe you're allowed only one consolidated attack against an enemy token during
any sub-phase of Combat.107
If you skirt this restriction, you open the door to gamey tactics that might neuter
the Defend ability altogether, which clearly isn't what is wanted.
Q: Could I play an unsuccessful Ranged-and-Siege-Attack-Phase attack (one too weak to kill the target,
but which affixes Defend points to this target), then follow that up with the Tremor Spell, and then play an At-
tack-Phase attack against the reduced armor of that enemy (due to the effects of Tremor)? A: Yes, so long as the
Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase attack could potentially harm the enemy token: e.g., Ranged Attack points
played against a Fortified enemy token would not work.108
S29:—The Defend Ability—If one ELVEN PROTECTORS token (Armor 4, Defend 2) and one SHADOW to-
ken (Armor 4, Elusive 8) are in Combat with you, and you attack the PROTECTORS first, then the PROTECTORS
will have Armor 6 for the whole Combat phase.
Conversely, if you attack the SHADOW first during the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase, their Elusive Ar-
mor will be increased to 10. If you then kill the PROTECTORS in the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase but not the
SHADOW, and then successfully block the SHADOW Attack in the Block Phase, the SHADOW will have Armor 6
during the Attack Phase, as the Defend boost persists even after the enemy providing that Defend is killed.109
Q30:—Enemies with the Ambush Trait—Under Ambush (TEZLA p. 2), what does the last sentence mean?
A30: Any movement effects that don't trigger attacks by Rampaging Enemies also don't trigger Ambush
attacks. In other words, if a movement ignores "provoke", it also ignores "ambush". See Movement S5.110
An enemy with the Ambush trait "may not cross a WALL". If a Hero ends up two hexes away from an
Ambusher, and more than one route to him exists, and if at least one route is free of WALLS (and mountains and
lakes), then the Ambusher will attack. If a Hero ends up adjacent to the Ambusher across a single WALL hexside,
but there's a valid two-hex route around the WALL, then the Ambusher will attack.111


[PAGE 017]
Combat p. 17
S31:—Pursuing Enemies—Pursuit, like Ambush, is an ability that some scenarios assign to enemy to-
kens, and a variant that players can add to any scenario.112
Enemies in pursuit will cross WALLS to get closer to their targeted hero, but they won't attack across
WALLS. You can, though, challenge them across WALLS.
Pursuing Enemies attack their targeted hero only if they're already adjacent to the targeted hero and only
if their movement would otherwise cause them to enter the same location as the targeted hero: if they do attack
in this way, the attack counts as that hero's Action for the turn just as if the hero had provoked a Rampaging En-
emy. Also note that pursuing enemies are Rampaging Enemies, so no one can move into their space, and if pur-
suing enemies are adjacent to a hero and the hero moves directly from an adjacent space to another adjacent
space, that movement will provoke an attack from them the same as usual, no matter which hero those pursuing
enemies are chasing (assuming no WALLS interfere of course). If you are the target and you assault a fortified
location adjacent to their location, they'll automatically join in that combat even if your assault move doesn't
provoke them (assuming again that no WALLS impede them from doing so).
The paragraph describing when a hero can be attacked is grammatically complicated, using multiple
negatives. What it's saying is that a hero is safe from attack if he's in a location where Interaction is possible,
(this includes if he's in a fortified location that he's not currently Assaulting). He's also safe if he's behind a
WALL. Accordingly, pursing enemies can participate in attacks on the NECROPOLIS and the HIDDEN VALLEY.
If you Rest, if you fully participate in a PvP combat as a DEFENDER, if you announce the end of the
Round, or if you accept an offer to participate in a cooperative assault on a CITY/VOLKARE/faction leader, then
you won't have a Movement Phase this turn and consequently any pursuing enemies won't move toward you or
attack you this turn (except of course in the case of cooperative assaults where pursuing enemies could be pro-
voked into attacking you as you enter the assault space via the normal rules that cover provoking Rampaging
Enemies). However if you take a normal turn and simply decide not to move, then you still have had a Move-
ment Phase and pursuing enemy tokens will move at the end of your (unused) Movement Phase.113
Multiple pursuing enemy tokens can move into a space with each other if their movement rules other-
wise allow it, even if those pursuing enemy tokens are pursuing different players. Similarly, if pursuing enemy
tokens are in the same space with each other, you can split them apart in the future if their movement rules oth-
erwise allow it. And if multiple pursuing enemies are in the same space and you move adjacent to them, you can
challenge them in combat—it's even permissible to challenge just one of them (unless they're both pursuing you
—and even in that case you can still decide to attack just one of them if they're across a WALL from you).
If, as a Mage Knight moves to Assault a fortified site, a pursuing enemy moves into the space the Mage
Knight entered the site from, the Mage Knight is permitted to challenge that pursuing enemy, just as he is per-
mitted to challenge any other adjacent Rampaging Enemy. However, should the Mage Knight fail to defeat the
fortified site defenders, he moves back into the now unsafe space (assuming he didn't defeat the Pursing enemy
token that's now there) and must backtrack from there via the Forced Withdrawal rules (see F.W. entry S1).114
When a pursuing enemy token participates in Combat as a rampaging enemy, if it survives, it is never-
theless still flipped. [Some players disagree with this and don't flip them.] While inverted, pursuing enemies can
be challenged in Combat.
In scenarios, pursuit movement doesn't begin until the turn after the pursuing enemy token is placed.115
S32:—Entering another Character's Space—If you end your Movement Phase in another character's
space (except for CITIES/PORTAL where multiple players can be), then one of the following things happen:
(a) If you provoked a Rampaging Enemy while entering the space, your movement is disallowed.116
(b) If PvP is permitted, you must start a PvP combat.117
You can't take a different Action: not Interacting
with the Locals, not entering an ADVENTURE SITE, not using a card effect (Peaceful Moment) or Skill (flipping
back Shamanic Ritual) as your Action, nor challenging Rampaging Enemies. See Temporal Portal O7.
(c) If PvP isn't permitted, you have to do a Forced Withdrawal and may not take an Action Phase.118


[PAGE 018]
p. 18 Combat
S33:—Fire & Ice Resistance—The non-Attack/non-Block effects of red/blue Deed cards (and of Unit
abilities that require red/blue mana) do not affect enemies with Fire/Ice Resistance. This rule affects nine cards:
Chilling Stare (Ultimate Edition) Chill & Lethal Chill
Explosive Bolt (Ultimate Ed., strong effect) Demolish & Disintegrate
Ice Shield (strong effect) AMOTEP FREEZERS (blue-mana effect)
Shield Bash (strong effect) DELPHANA MASTERS (red- and blue-mana effects)
Exploding Shield
You might wish to add reminder marks to these cards—maybe small red/blue pentagons.119
If you face several enemies, some with Fire/Ice Resistance and some without, you can still play the
above cards (including Demolish/Disintegrate that affects multiple enemies at once), but only those enemies
without Fire/Ice Resistances will be affected by the non-Attack/non-Block effects.120
S34:—Assigning Damage to a Unit with Resistance—An IRONCLADS token (4 physical attack, Brutal)
goes unblocked when facing a Mage Knight (armor 3) and an ILLUSIONISTS Unit (armor 2, physical resistance).
Since the IRONCLADS are Brutal, the 4 attack delivers 8 damage.
If any Units are to be assigned damage from this attack, it must be done before any damage from this
attack is assigned to the Mage Knight himself. Choosing the ILLUSIONISTS first will reduce the IRONCLADS attack
by the ILLUSIONISTS' armor value: 8 damage - 2 armor = 6 damage remaining. No Wound is taken for this first
reduction because the ILLUSIONISTS are resistant to physical attacks. As there is still damage remaining, the ILLU-
SIONISTS now take one Wound (this is mandatory), but in doing so it reduces the remaining damage by its armor
a second time: 6 damage - 2 armor = 4 damage remaining. This completes the ILLUSIONISTS' contribution. A Unit
can only be assigned damage once during a Combat Phase, even if due to Resistances it doesn't take a Wound.
At this point the player assigns all remaining damage to his Mage Knight. (Once this decision is made,
no more damage from the current attack can be assigned to Units.) So the Knight takes a Wound and decreases
the damage by his armor: 4 damage - 3 armor = 1 damage remaining. Since there is still damage left, the Knight
takes a second Wound and then decreases the damage again: 1 damage - 3 armor ≤ 0 damage remaining.
The ILLUSIONISTS have taken one Wound, the Mage Knight two Wounds (in his hand). See S38 below.121
S35:—Assigning Damage to a Unit with Resistance—If you assign damage to an unspent Unit that's Re-
sistant to the type of attack being made, and if the armor of the Unit is high enough to absorb all of the damage
from that attack without the Unit becoming wounded, then that Unit can still use any of its abilities later during
this Combat. It may not, however, be assigned damage from another attack during the same Combat Phase. See
Silver Units S9.
A wounded Unit can never be assigned damage, even if its Armor-plus-Resistance would be enough to
prevent it from taking a Wound from that damage.122
S36:—Reward for Killing Enemies—When you defeat enemy tokens, you always get their full Fame
points, even when burning MONASTERIES (but not when attacking player-controlled KEEPS: S39 below).
If you face several enemies and defeat only some of them, you receive Fame for those you defeat and
place shield tokens on a CITY card for those that were inside the CITY. And you gain Reputation for killing Ram-
paging Enemies: S40 below. But you gain "the reward for clearing a site" only if you defeat all of the enemy
tokens in that site. E.g., if you kill the sole defender of a MAGE TOWER but not a Rampaging Enemy that was
provoked by your move into that MAGE TOWER, you don't retreat and you do gain the reward Spell card for con-
quering the TOWER. See CITIES S3.123
S37:—Resistances during the Ranged and Siege Attack Phase—The protective benefits of the Physical/
Fire/Ice/Cold-Fire Resistance icons that appear on enemy tokens are always active: they affect attacks made
during both the Attack Phase and during the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase.124


[PAGE 019]
Combat p. 19
S38:—Assign Damage Separately—If you assign damage directly to your Mage Knight from four ene-
my tokens in the amounts of 3, 4, 5, and 6 while your Mage Knight has an armor of 4, you will take 6 Wounds
(1 + 1 + 2 + 2 = 6). You do not add the damage together 3 + 4 + 5 + 6 = 18, divide by 4, then round up to 5.125
If you assign all of the damage from an enemy token with an Attack 5 to your Mage Knight with Armor
2, you follow this process: take a wound (5 - 2 = 3); take a wound (3 - 2 = 1); take a wound (1 - 2 ≤ 0). All of
the damage having been assigned, you're done and your Mage Knight has taken three wounds.
The mechanics are easy: every time you assign a wound to a Unit or to the Mage Knight, a certain por-
tion of the enemy token's printed attack strength (doubled printed attack strength if the enemy has Brutal) is ab-
sorbed. The portion absorbed is equal to the armor value of the Unit or Mage Knight that takes the wound. The
amount of the attack that is "still remaining to be assigned after a wound is sustained" is equal to "the previously
unabsorbed attack strength" minus "the armor value of the Unit or Mage Knight that is taking the wound".126
S39:—Replacing Defeated Enemy Tokens—If you failed to defeat an enemy token in a TOMB/DUNGEON/
MONASTERY or when assaulting another player’s KEEP, the enemy token is discarded. If you failed to defeat an
enemy in a MONSTER DEN/SPAWNING GROUNDS/RUINS, the enemy token remains there.
When you assault another player’s KEEP, you fight a gray enemy token but you only earn half of the
Fame (rounded up) for defeating it. This enemy token is discarded if you fail to defeat it. The same applies to
MAGE TOWERS in the "Conquer and Hold" scenario.127
S40:—Rampaging Enemies and ADVENTURE SITES—If moving into a space provokes an attack by a Ram-
paging Enemy, your Movement Phase immediately ends and a Combat with that Rampaging Enemy begins. See
Combat Q1 and Influence S7. Exploring an ADVENTURE SITE, on the other hand, is a voluntary Action and can-
not happen on the same turn that a mandatory Combat occurs: if entry of a space provokes a Rampaging Ene-
my, you cannot explore an ADVENTURE SITE on the same turn. On the other hand if you choose to explore an AD-
VENTURE SITE, you cannot voluntarily challenge adjacent Rampaging Enemies as part of any Combat that en-
sues. There's no way to combine Combat with Rampaging Enemies and Combat that occurs while exploring an
ADVENTURE SITE. And also remember that ORC MARAUDERS and DRACONUM, when they appear in an ADVENTURE
SITE, are not "Rampaging Enemies", so you earn no Reputation bonus for defeating them there.128
S41:—Defeating Rampaging Enemies—If you defeat a Rampaging Enemy, whether you provoked them
or challenged them, you do not move onto their space after your successful combat.129
Q42:—Assigning Damage and Using Unit Abilities—Can a Unit use one of its abilities and also be as-
signed damage during the same Combat Phase?
A42: It depends. If a Unit has an ability that's usable during the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase or
Block Phase—such as a Ranged Attack 3, or a Block 2—then yes, because the Assign Damage Phase occurs
later in the Combat Phase. If a Unit has Resistance to an Attack and is assigned damage ≤ its Armor value (see
S35 above), then the Unit won't be wounded and—if it's still "ready"—it also can use an ability later on.130
S43:—Units Not Present—You cannot assign damage to Units that don't accompany you during Com-
bat, such as when burning a MONASTERY or when entering a DUNGEON/TOMB or if you didn't bring them with
you into a MAZE/LABYRINTH. You can, though, assign damage to HEROES during an Assault even if you didn't
pay them 2 Influence points. See Call to Arms S2.131
S44:—Excess Attack Points—Any excess Attack points (of any type) beyond the total Armor of the ene-
my group you are attacking are lost. Excess points can't be applied to other groups of enemy tokens nor saved at
the end of the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase for use during the Attack Phase. See S13 above.
Similarly, if an attack during the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase is insufficient to kill the attacked ene-
my token(s), any partial damage is voided as soon as the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase ends: the partial dam-
age does not linger on or make it any easier to kill those enemy tokens during the Attack Phase.132


[PAGE 020]
p. 20 Combat
S45:—After being Knocked Out—The base game rulebook, p.8, says "While knocked out . . . you contin-
ue taking Wounds if you have to assign further damage to your Hero." Don't be confused: assigning damage
continues to work the same way after you're knocked out—you can choose to assign damage to your Units or to
your Mage Knight at your discretion (with the usual proviso that after any damage is assigned to your Mage
Knight, the remainder of the damage from the current attack also has to be assigned to your Mage Knight).133
O46:—Apportioning Effects Among Multiple Enemy Tokens—At various times there has been discus-
sion in the BGG forums as to whether a player is allowed to . . .
(a) play Attack points from a Deed Card/Skill/Unit against one group of enemy tokens, then play Attack
points from another Deed Card/Skill/Unit against a different group of enemy tokens, then play At-
tack points from a third Deed Card/Skill/Unit against the first group of enemy tokens, and
(b) whether multiple effects from a single Deed Card/Skill/Unit (such as with the Spell Expose) can be
played against different groups of enemy tokens.†
I see no clear prohibition against either sequence, especially in light of the Skill Forked Lightning (see
Forked Lightning Q4).134
Combat S28 discusses a tangential issue of playing fewer Attack points than are need-
ed. See also S49 below and Sacrifice S4.135
†
- Of course multiple Attack points or Block points provided by a single Deed Card/Skill/Unit general-
ly have to be combined and used against a single group of enemy tokens/a single Attack.136
S47:—Paralyze Damage—An enemy token with the Paralyze ability (like MEDUSA) which inflicts a
Wound on a Unit will destroy that Unit. But although the Unit is destroyed, the Unit in dying nevertheless still
absorbs damage from the enemy attack in the usual way: see S38 above.
If, however, the Unit is Resistant to the type (physical, ice, fire, or cold-fire) of the Paralyze Attack, the
Unit will be destroyed only if the Attack is greater than the Unit's Armor. And if the Attack is greater than the
Unit's Armor, then the Unit in dying reduces the damage coming from the enemy Attack by twice the Unit's Ar-
mor value, just as it does in non-Paralyze attacks. See S34 and S35 above.137
S48:—Non-Attack/Non-Block Effects—Enemy tokens with Fire Resistance and/or Ice Resistance are
immune to non-Attack/non-Block effects from any Effect activated by a red/blue mana (see Q8, S12, and S33
above), but Attack points or Block points of any kind (Ice Block 3, Ranged Attack 2, Cold-Fire Siege Attack 4)
granted by those effects still apply in the usual way (though sometimes being halved for being inefficient). This
means that red/blue mana Effects that say "enemy is killed", "enemy armor is reduced", "enemy does not at-
tack", etc., will not affect enemies with Fire Resistance and/or Ice Resistance, as appropriate. See Cold Tough-
ness S3.138
S49:—Applying Multiple Effects—As you play Attack points, you assign them to a specific target, but
you don't apply them against that target until you're ready to do so. Before you release the waiting Attack
points, you can play other Attack points as well as non-Attack effects that (usually) take effect immediately,139
effects that, say, reduce Armor values and make a target easier to kill. See S28 and O46 above, and Sacrifice
S4.140
There is a debate as to whether Attack points, when assigned, need to be legal at that moment (see Bow of
Starsdawn O5, Ghostly Elixir S1, and S13 above), but the easier and more permissive interpretation is that At-
tack points only need to be legal when they're applied.141


[PAGE 021]
PvP p. 21
Player versus Player Combat
# Q1:—Sequence of Play in PvP—How do you handle PvP combat?142
A1: Here is an approach many players use:
1. When the AGGRESSOR ends his move in a hex with another player, PvP combat occurs.†
a. The AGGRESSOR is now restricted to the PvP sequence of play, which includes only
Ranged and Siege Attacks, and Melee Attacks.
2. The DEFENDER responds:
a. The DEFENDER takes "before your turn" and "on another player's turn" options like
pillaging and Motivation: see Turn Start S1.143
b. The DEFENDER declares whether he is fully or partially attending the PvP combat. See
S2 below.
c. The DEFENDER, if fully attending, takes his start-of-turn actions (MAGICAL GLADE): see
Turn Start S1.144
d. The DEFENDER plays Special Effects
such as Mana Draw and Crystallize (even if
only partially attending). Remember the restrictions on Skills and Mana dice that ap-
ply if a player is only partially attending.
3. Players conduct their Ranged and Siege Attacks.‡ PvP Combat consists of multiple Attack-
er/Blocker sequences:
a. Attack Step: The Attacker plays Special Effects and Attack effects, or passes (con-
cluding this sequence).
b. Block Step: The Blocker plays Special Effects and Block effects, or passes.
c. Assign Damage Step (Attacker): The Attacker plays Special Effects and other combat
effects (e.g. Tremor), and assigns unblocked Attack points.§
d. Assign Damage Step (Blocker): The Blocker plays Special Effects and other combat
effects (e.g. Veil of Mist, Banner of Fortitude, Battle Hardened).§
4. After all combat ends, the AGGRESSOR and a fully-attending DEFENDER play Special Effects
and Healing Effects, the AGGRESSOR going first.
5. End-of-Turn sequence (for both players, if the DEFENDER is fully attending). Turn order again
matters, the AGGRESSOR going first.
† - Some players believe the AGGRESSOR can play Into the Heat when he initiates PvP combat.145
‡ - Another view is that there should be a step in between steps (2) and (3) where the AGGRESSOR can
play start-of-combat effects, just before the DEFENDER plays his own start-of-combat effects. If so, this is where
the AGGRESSOR would first be allowed to play Into the Heat.
§ - Combat effects (Tremor, and wound- and Resistance-oriented effects, etc.) can be played in earlier
steps rather than waiting until step 3.c and 3.d if, say, a player is concerned about being able to use SOURCE dice
before the other player does. Effects persist for the entire combat.

- Some might argue that players attacked via PvP can play Healing Effects before the combat begins,
but the wording of the rule doesn't seem to support this. Perhaps it's better to view a PvP attack (and an attack
by VOLKARE) as immediately dropping a player into the thick of combat where no healing is allowed.146
S2:—Fully and Partially Attending Combat—147
o When Fully Attending a Combat, the player takes his next turn right then:
▪ He flips his Round Order Token face down.
 While it's facedown, he cannot be attacked again by PvP combat.
 When it would next be his turn, all he does is flip his Round Order token face up and
retrieve any expiring Interactive Skill in the center of the play area.
▪ He cannot move to new spaces or reveal tiles, and his Action this turn will be Combat.
▪ He can use a free mana die from the SOURCE, and play deed cards, and use Skills and Units.
▪ After Combat he can play Special Effect cards and Healing cards.
▪ He concludes his turn by taking all of the usual End-of-Turn steps.


[PAGE 022]
p. 22 PvP
o When Partially Attending a Combat, the player . . .
▪ Doesn't get a free mana die (but can use SOURCE dice if an effect allows him to do so).
▪ Cannot use Skill tokens (except those usable outside of his turn, like Motivation).
▪ Cannot play Healing Effects.
▪ Cannot play Special Effects outside of combat.
▪ Doesn't take End-of-Turn steps (not even drawing back up to his hand limit).
He can however play cards and activate Units, and he can turn crystals into mana.
S3:—Crystal Use—You can use crystals from your inventory as mana to power your cards even when
you only Partially Attend a PvP Combat.148
S4:—Declaring the End of the Round—You cannot declare the end of the Round during PvP combat.149
Q5:—Few Cards—If a player has no cards in her draw deck and only a couple of cards in her hand and
is attacked by PvP combat, what are her options? Can she declare the end of the Round?
A5: She has two options: (a) Fully attend the combat—she can get one free mana from the SOURCE, can
use Skills, can use Healing and Special Effects after the combat, and gets an End-of-Turn sequence—but she
must skip her next turn and therefore would not be able to declare the end of the Round until the following turn.
(b) Partially attend the combat—she gets no free mana from the SOURCE, cannot use Skills, cannot use Healing
and Special Effects after combat, and does not get an End-of-Turn sequence—but in this way she can declare
the end of the Round during her upcoming turn.150
S6:—PvP in a KEEP—If PvP combat occurs in a KEEP, and one player is forced out of it, the player who
remains takes possession of the KEEP, removing the opponent's shield token and placing his own there.151
S7:—Playing Cards outside your Turn—Cards like Agility, Ambush, or Wings of Night can also be used
when defending (against another player or VOLKARE) even when the combat isn't directly following the Move-
ment Phase of your own turn.152
O8:—Applying Effects to Mage Knights—How do you handle effects like those of SHOCKTROOPS that
say "Reduce . . . one attack of chosen enemy by 1" and "Reduce one enemy attack by 3"?153
A8: A strict reading of the effect text might prohibit the first effect (one attack of chosen enemy) from
being used against an opposing Mage Knight, yet allow the second effect (one enemy attack)—but such fine
parsing of the language may be beyond the designer's intent.154
See Know Your Prey O3.155
S9:—The Melee Phase—If using cards like Tornado and Disintegrate in PvP combat, when they say
"Play only in the Attack Phase," they mean "Play only in the Melee Attack Phase."156
O10:—Contradictory Effects—In some PvP combats, it's possible players will play competing effects,
such as one player removing Resistances from the opponent's Units (with effects like Expose, or the SORCERERS
third ability, or Know Your Prey), and the other player adding Resistances to his own Units (with effects like
Veil of Mist, or the ALTEM GUARDIANS third ability, or Force of Nature). In resolving such cases, apply the ef-
fects in the order they're played: if the first effect removes a Resistance from a Unit (whether or not the Unit had
that Resistance to begin with), and a later effect adds the same Resistance to that Unit, then the Unit now has
that Resistance. Or if the first effect adds Resistance to a Unit (whether or not the Unit had that Resistance to
begin with), and a later effect removes the Resistance, then the Unit now no longer has that Resistance.157


[PAGE 023]
PvP p. 23
S11:—Efficient Blocks—The rulebook (p. 10) says the efficient/inefficient Blocking rules apply normal-
ly. So if an opponent attacks you in a Melee Attack Phase with Fire Attack 4 + Ice Attack 3, and if you play Fire
Block 5, then all 5 Block points are fully effective: your opponent will be left with 2 points of damage to assign.
