If instead you play Ice Block 3 (fully effective vs. Fire Attacks) + Block 5 (not fully effective vs. either), the At-
tack is reduced by 3 + 5/2 = 5.5, again leaving him 2 points of damage to assign.158
O12:—Assigning Damage—The PvP rules don't say whether an Attacker is allowed to play an effect
while she's assigning damage, yet for the other phases of PvP combat the rules do say that she can play effects.
From this, some players infer that a player cannot play effects while assigning damage (and that this applies
both to the Attacker and to the Blocker). See, however, Q1 above and Battle Hardened S3. Further note that a
player can play a card or use mana from the SOURCE only when it's her turn to act.159
S13:—Forced Retreat—Damage points can be freely split between stealing Artifacts and inflicting
wounds and forcing a retreat.160
O14:—Skill Usage in PvP—An instructive example regarding Nature's Vengeance:
If the AGGRESSOR (or the DEFENDER when fully attending) plays Nature's Vengeance during PvP, then
only the first part of the Skill (giving an enemy Cumbersome and reducing an attack of that enemy) would be
applied to this PvP Combat. I would then interpret the interactive part of this Skill (the attack of all other ene-
mies being increased by one during the Block Phase) as coming into effect only after this entire PvP Combat is
concluded (because the interactive part of a Skill comes into play only after the owning player's turn ends).
If on the other hand the DEFENDER had played Nature's Vengeance during his previous turn, then the in-
teractive part would apply to this PvP Combat—and this would be true regardless of whether the DEFENDER ful-
ly or partially participates in the PvP Combat. See Skill Tokens S4 and S6.
In the former case (the AGGRESSOR playing Nature's Vengeance, or the DEFENDER playing it while fully
attending), the "enemy" affected has to be one of the BLOCKER'S unwounded Units (this is how a co-designer of
the TEZLA expected this Skill to work), and the effects (Cumbersome, -1 Attack) would apply to that Unit for the
entire PvP Combat. (See O8 above, and page 11 of the base game rulebook.) BRAEVALAR plays this Skill after
the attack is fully announced and specified. [If you do permit effects that use the word "enemy" to target the at-
tack of Mage Knights (see O8 above), then you should restrict the effect of the Cumbersome attribute and -1 at-
tack reduction to its target, which in this case would be the current attack. And once the current attack is re-
solved, the attack ceases to exist, so whether Cumbersome and the -1 Attack endures for the entire PvP Combat
is immaterial, because the attack—once resolved—is over with and gone.]
In the latter case (the DEFENDER having played Nature's Vengeance during his previous turn), the situa-
tion is more complicated because, in PvP Combat, players are permitted to partially block an opponent's attacks.
In this case, I would allow the BLOCKER to selectively block the non-Nature's Vengeance attack points first. If
that's allowed, then the only negative effect of blocking all attack points except the Nature's Vengeance point
would be that the attack would not count as being fully blocked, even though the attack would assign no dam-
age. (See Nature's Vengeance S1 and S2.) [An alternate approach is simply to point out that partial blocks in
PvP Combat are effective, and therefore any effect that raises the attack factor merely for the duration of the
Block Phase has practically no effect and consequently should be entirely ignored in PvP Combat.]161

p. 24 Arcane Immunity
Arcane Immunity
# S1:—Canceling an Attack—Enemies with Arcane Immunity are not affected by non-Attack/non-Block
effects from any source: magic does not touch them. As such, they can never be prevented†
from attacking; their
armor can never be reduced; they can never lose their defensive abilities; they can never lose their modes of at-
tack (like summons attacks—see Combat S5) or their long-range attacks (like Siege Attacks). Their attacks,
however, can be targeted by effects that reduce the strength of the attack, or by effects that remove elements
like cold-fire
or abilities like poison from the attack (as discussed in Chilling Stare S2). In these cases your
actions are targeting not the enemies themselves but their attacks. So a SHOCKTROOPS Unit can reduce the Attack
of a SORCERERS enemy token by 3, despite the SORCERERS having Arcane Immunity.162
†
- The phrase "prevent an enemy from attacking" and the phrase "cancel an enemy attack" appear to be
synonymous in game terms.‡
[Some players argue that these phrases mean different things, that the latter phras-
ing can be used against Arcane Immunity, but not the former (arguing that "canceling an attack" is different
than "preventing an attack"). I find this argument unconvincing. And note that Lost Legion p. 5 makes no dis-
tinction between "preventing attacks" and "canceling attacks" when it discusses the Elusive ability, using the
phrase "preventing in some way" to encompass both ideas. See Banner of Fear Q7.]163
‡
- One obvious difference between these phrases arises when facing an enemy with Multiple Attacks:
the former phrase cancels all of that enemy's attacks while the latter phrase cancels only one. Simply expressing
this distinction might be the entire reason for these two different phrases.

- Players might question whether an effect can remove an element of an attack from an enemy with
Arcane Immunity because at present no effect does so. (There are very few enemies in the first place that even
combine elemental attacks with Arcane Immunity: SHADOW MONSTERS are the only enemy token, and there's
also VOLKARE and Faction Leaders.) Lost Legion p. 6, however, implies that effects can do this (see S5 below).
S2:—Resolving Attacks and Blocks—Arcane Immunity does not affect any Attack points or Block
points played against an enemy token: simply look for any Resistances in the enemy tokens, same as usual. So
no Attack points directed against a SORCERERS token (Armor 6, Attack 6, Arcane Immunity, Assassination, Poi-
son, Fame 5) get halved. And the only Attack points that're halved against a DRAGON SUMMONER token (Armor
8, Physical Resistance, Arcane Immunity, Attack: summon 2 MONSTER tokens, Fame 9) are Physical Attacks.164
S3:—The Defend Ability—Defend can increase the Armor of an enemy with Arcane Immunity.165
Q4:—Grouping Enemies—If an enemy with Arcane Immunity is in the same group with other enemy
tokens, do those other enemy tokens gain Arcane Immunity protection for their Attacks and their armor?
A4: No: Arcane Immunity does not spread to other members of a group. See Combat S12.166
S5:—Shield Bash—The second example of Arcane Immunity on p. 6 of Lost Legion is incorrect—it was
written before a final change was made to the text of the Shield Bash card, and was overlooked in the final edit-
ing: If you use the strong effect of Shield Bash . . . the Block works, the attack even loses eventual Swiftness [and
even counts twice against an enemy with Swiftness] (as it targets the attack, not the enemy), but the Armor of
the enemy is not reduced. [Unfortunately, the error wasn't caught in the Ultimate Edition either.]167
S6:—Not a Resistance—Arcane Immunity is not a Resistance.168

[PAGE 025]
CITIES p. 25
CITIES
# S1:—Fight All Enemy Tokens—When a CITY is attacked, all enemy tokens in the CITY (that is, all of the
CITY Defenders) join in the Combat. Exception: Cooperative CITY Assaults—see S11 below.169
If you attack a CITY, kill an enemy token, and then retreat, that enemy does not re-spawn.170
Q2:—Fortifications—If you attack a CITY defended by three enemy tokens, one of which is a CRYPT
WORM (armor 6, Fortified, Attack 6, Fame 5), are all enemies doubly fortified?
A2: Not necessarily. When attacking CITY Defenders, you group them however you wish: singly, all to-
gether, or anything in between. Only those enemy tokens in the same group with the CRYPT WORM will face the
attack restrictions of double fortification. See Combat S12 and S13.171
S3:—Rampaging Enemies—If you kill every CITY Defender but not all provoked/challenged Rampaging
Enemies, you have defeated the CITY and you don't retreat to your starting space. Undefeated Rampaging Ene-
mies remain where they were.172
This is also true when Mage Knights assault KEEPS and MAGE TOWERS.
S4:—CITY Bonus: Summoned MONSTERS / Arcane Immunity—In CITY assaults, summoned MONSTERS (if
summoned by a SUMMONER inside the CITY and not by a nearby Rampaging Enemy) do benefit from the CITY
bonus and from the CITY fortifications, just as do all enemies inside the CITY.
The same goes for enemies who have Arcane Immunity: they too receive the CITY bonus and the CITY
fortifications.173
S5:—CITY Bonus: Brutal / Poison—Although Brutal can apply to any enemy token, the red CITY grants
the Brutal ability only to enemy tokens with a Physical Attack (any Fire, Ice, and Cold Fire attacks do not gain
the Brutal ability). Likewise for the green CITY, Poison is added only to those enemy tokens with a Physical At-
tack. See S6 just below and Know Your Prey O2.174
S6:—CITY Bonus: Brutal / Poison—A Brutal enemy token with a physical Attack defending inside the
red CITY gains no additional benefit (other than being fortified), for Attacks can't be "doubly" Brutal. Similarly,
Attacks from inside the green CITY can't be "doubly" Poisonous. See Know Your Prey O2.175
S7:—CITY Bonus: Armor Increase—Enemies in the white CITY get +1 to their Armor. This applies be-
fore any Armor reduction from other effects. So white-CITY defenders subject to the Spell Disease get their Ar-
mor reduced down to one, not to two. Enemy tokens with Elusive get +1 to both Armor values.176
S8:—CITY Bonus: Combat Example—To Block FREEZERS (Ice Attack 3, Swiftness, Paralyze, Fire Resis-
tance) in the blue CITY (+2 Ice Attack), you can play 10 Fire Block, 10 Cold-Fire Block, 20 Ice Block, 20 Phys-
ical Block, or some combination of those. See Possess O2.177
S9:—Cooperative Assaults: Restrictions—You cannot offer a Cooperative Assault from a space occu-
pied by another player. Once agreed to, a Cooperative CITY Assault is an Action (and requires a mandatory
move into the CITY). You cannot do any other Action, either before the Assault or afterward.178
S10:—Cooperative Assaults: Entering the CITY—To participate in a Cooperative CITY Assault, you have
to expend two Move points to enter the CITY. And you have to be adjacent to the CITY to be invited to join in:
you can't use a card like Space Bending or Underground Attack to reach the CITY—unless you're the player who
is initiating the Cooperative CITY Assault.179
S11:—Cooperative Assaults: Assigning Enemy Tokens—In Cooperative CITY Assaults, only the number
of enemy tokens are agreed to: the colors of those tokens are determined randomly. Cooperative Assaults on
VOLKARE are handled differently: Lost Legion, p. 8.180


[PAGE 026]
p. 26 CITIES
S12:—Cooperative Assaults: Rampaging Enemies—In Cooperative CITY Assaults, the first player (in
Round Order, see S15 below) to provoke or challenge a Rampaging Enemy as she moves into the CITY will be
the one to fight it. (Decisions to challenge Rampaging Enemies are made before revealing which CITY defenders
she'll be facing: see Combat S14.) If she fails to kill a Rampaging Enemy, other players do not then get a chance
to attack that Rampaging Enemy during the same CITY assault. Cooperative CITY Assaults are simultaneous
events: each CITY defender engages only one Mage Knight, and the same goes for Rampaging Enemies.181
S13:—Cooperative Assaults: Free Mana Dice—In Cooperative CITY Assaults, if the current player has
already used his free mana dice from the SOURCE, he is not granted an additional free mana dice.182
S14:—Cooperative Assaults: Empty SOURCE—During a Cooperative CITY Assault, if you play Mana
Storm and take all of the dice from the SOURCE, those who take their Cooperative Assault turns after you won't
be able to use those dice and will even lose out on their one free die. See Skill Tokens S6.183
S15:—Cooperative Assaults: Round Order—In Cooperative CITY Assaults, "in Round Order" means
play begins with the current player and continues with the involved player who would normally be next, etc.184
S16:—Cooperative Assaults: Turn Order—In a Cooperative CITY Assault, if Players C and A accept
Player B's offer, then play proceeds as follows. (Turn Order tokens are in this order: A, B, C, D, Dummy.)185
Player A has taken her normal turn.
Player B proposes a Cooperative CITY Assault, and Players C and A accept:
Player B does his CITY Assault.
Player C does his CITY Assault (and flips over his Round Order token).
Player A does her CITY Assault (and flips over her Round Order token).
Player B plays any Healing and Special Effects, then does his End-of-Turn sequence.
Player C plays any Healing and Special Effects, then does his End-of-Turn sequence.
Player A plays any Healing and Special Effects, then does her End-of-Turn sequence.
Player C takes no action during his turn order except to flip his Round Order token face-up.
Player D takes a normal turn.
Dummy Turn.
Player A takes no action except to flip her Round Order token face-up.
Player B takes a normal turn.
Player C takes a normal turn.
Player D takes a normal turn.
Dummy Turn.
S17:—Cooperative Assaults: Losing Next Turn—In Cooperative CITY Assaults, players who're invited
and accept will lose their next turn. In Full Conquest and Full Cooperation scenarios, when the final CITY is
conquered, the scenario rules say players get a last turn (if the Round doesn't end). Players who joined in the
Assault on the final CITY, though, will not get a final turn: they've already taken it in advance.186
S18:—Cooperative Assaults: Actions before Moving—In Cooperative CITY Assaults, players who accept
the invitation may play Special Effects and Healing Effects before moving into the CITY.187
S19:—Neutral / Friendly CITIES—Neutral CITIES (found in some scenarios) are already conquered: you
can enter them, Interact there (with +1 for your shield token), have a +1 hand limit if adjacent, but no one is the
CITY Leader.
With CITIES conquered during a game, any player—even one with no shield tokens on it—can Interact
with the Locals there.188
Team Rules, if in play, don't alter this. If a CITY hasn't yet been conquered—that is, if it
still has enemy tokens inside—then no Interaction is permitted there, even if the CITY is a MEGAPOLIS.189


[PAGE 027]
CITIES p. 27
S20:—Card Draws—A CITY must be conquered before a Mage Knight gains extra card draws from the
shield tokens he has earned there.190
S21:—Interacting with the Locals—After a CITY has been conquered (see S19 above, and Influence S7),
when Interacting with the Locals inside it, you receive one Influence point for every shield token you've placed
on the CITY card (in addition to whatever Influence adjustment you gain from your Reputation).
You can purchase the items below, as many of each as you can afford, but you receive the Influence bo-
nus for shields only once per turn. And remember that on the turn you conquer a CITY, no Interaction is allowed
there because your Action for that turn was Combat, not Interaction. And unlike with MONASTERIES and VILLAG-
ES, you cannot purchase healing inside CITIES.191
• Red CITY: you may purchase Artifacts for twelve Influence each. You receive them
during your End-of-Turn sequence, drawing one more Artifact than you purchased,
and choose one to discard: those you keep go on top of your Deed Deck.192
• Green CITY: you may purchase Advanced Action cards from the Advanced Ac-
tion Offer (not from the MONASTERY Unit Offer) or take the top card sight-unseen
from the Advanced Action deck, for six Influence each. When any card is purchased from the Offer,
it is instantly replenished. Purchased cards go on top of your Deed Deck.193
• Blue CITY: you may purchase Spells from the Spell Offer for seven Influence plus
a matching mana, each. After each Spell purchase, the Spell Offer is instantly re-
plenished. Purchased Spells go on top of your Deed Deck.194
• White CITY: you may recruit any Unit from the Unit Offer while here (no matter
what site icons are on the Unit card), for the standard price. You may also pay to
add elite (gold) Units to the Unit Offer, for two Influence each.195
S22:—Rewards for Conquering CITIES—When you conquer a CITY, these are the "rewards" you gain:196
(a) the Fame points for the enemy tokens you defeated while conquering it,
(b) the purchase and recruitment abilities discussed in S21 above that will apply when you're Inter-
acting with the Locals while inside that conquered CITY during future turns,
(c) the Influence bonus for shield tokens placed on the CITY card when you're Interacting with the
Locals while inside that conquered CITY during future turns,
(d) the hand limit increase you gain while you're within one space of the conquered CITY, and
(e) whatever scenario-specific rules the defeated CITY might trigger, including scoring bonuses and
satisfying end-of-game conditions.
S23:—Failing to Defeat All Enemy Tokens—If you fail to defeat all of the enemy tokens inside a CITY,
you withdraw back to the space from which you entered the CITY (however far away that space was); this,
though, is not Forced Withdrawal. See Combat S19 and Forced Withdrawal S1 through S3.
You do receive rewards for the enemy tokens that you did defeat (see Combat S36). Regarding Rampag-
ing Enemies that joined the Combat, see S3 above.
When you attack the CITY again, any enemy tokens killed during earlier attacks do not respawn: you on-
ly face those CITY Defenders that still remain (plus any Rampaging Enemies that're provoked by your CITY en-
try or that you optionally choose to challenge).197
Cross References: Movement S4, S8, Q12; Influence S1, S10; End of Turn S5; Demolish S4; Possess
O2; Know Your Prey O2.


[PAGE 028]
p. 28 Influence
Influence
# S1:—Influence Points—Your Reputation modifies your Influence points only when you choose "Inter-
act with the Locals" as your Action for the turn, and the effect of your Reputation applies only once, either to
increase or decrease your Influence points. Any Reputation change that occurs after Interaction begins, doesn't
affect you this turn. If you're not "Interacting with the Locals", your Reputation does not affect your Influence
points (nor do any shield tokens on a conquered CITY).
Before Lost Legion, you could play Influence cards only during an Interaction or in very specific situa-
tions (c.f., Learning). Now the rule is simpler: you can play Influence anytime, even during Combat. But know
that when you go from the Movement Phase to the Action Phase, any unspent Influence points will vanish.
Example: You play the basic effect of Learning during the Movement Phase, then generate 5 more In-
fluence for a total of 7 (any Reputation bonus/penalty will not apply). You spend 6 Influence to add an Ad-
vanced Action to your discard pile and then explore a new tile, finding a MONASTERY, and move to it to begin an
Interaction. At this point your unspent Influence point disappears and your Reputation penalty of -1 applies,
giving you an Influence deficit. If you play 3 Influence points, you'll have 2 total and can buy 1 Healing point.
Example: During Combat you play Diplomacy plus 4 additional Influence points. You can now convert
that Influence into Block points (with elements, if you played the strong effect of Diplomacy) and you can apply
them however you wish among different enemy attacks. See Miscellaneous S9.198
S2:—Interacting with the Locals—When you declare you're Interacting with the Locals, you immediate-
ly apply your Reputation modifier, even if you don't intend to buy anything at your current site. If your Reputa-
tion is positive, you could use it with Learning or even use it to recruit Units or buy Healing all by itself.199
O3:—Recruiting HEROES or THUGS—When you declare you're Interacting with the Locals, your Reputa-
tion at that moment is fixed for the entire Interaction. If you recruit HEROES or THUGS, it's this fixed Reputation
modifier that gets adjusted. But you can't take the Reputation adjustment for recruiting HEROES/THUGS and then
not recruit them. These rules are clear. But can the decision to recruit HEROES/THUGS be delayed?200
View 1: No.201
According to this view, you must immediately declare your intention to recruit HEROES/
THUGS, and you can't change your mind later (especially not after new cards are revealed). Additionally, you
also need to have the demonstrable means to be able to recruit them. If HEROES/THUGS get added to the Unit Of-
fer only after Interaction begins, you can't recruit them—even if your Reputation modifier is zero.
View 2: Yes. Under this view, you can retroactively account for the effect your decision would've had
on your Reputation modifier and on your allowable actions: if you knew from the get-go what you would do,
could you have afforded what you actually bought? If so, you can even recruit HEROES/THUGS that're added to
the Unit Offer after Interaction begins.
Fortunately situations where this matters don't arise very often, but I tend to side with View 2.202
Example: If you have a -3 Reputation and recruit THUGS this turn, it'll cost you 2 additional Influence to
do so. If you then recruit a standard Unit, say, SHOCKTROOPS, it'll cost you an additional 6 Influence to do so.
S4:—Recruiting Multiple Units—You can recruit multiple Units during a single Interaction, and even
spend the first Unit you recruit to help you recruit a second Unit. See Silver Units Q3.203
S5:—Low Reputation—If you're on the "X" spot of the Reputation track, you can still take actions that
decrease your Reputation, such as pillaging a VILLAGE, burning a MONASTERY, or attacking a fortified site.204
Q6:—Low Reputation—If you're on the "X" spot of the Reputation track and there're no ORC MARAUD-
ERS or DRACONUM to defeat, how can you gain Reputation?
A6: It won't be easy. The cards that increase Reputation are Noble Manners and Heroic Tale. The strong
effect of Noble Manners increases your Reputation if you play it during Interaction—but if you're on "X", you
can't Interact! Heroic Tale increases your Reputation for recruiting Units, but that's difficult to do while your
Reputation is on "X"—so you probably need to couple it with Call to Glory.205


[PAGE 029]
Influence, Healing p. 29
S7:—Interaction and Combat—You're allowed only one Action. If you choose to Interact with the Lo-
cals, you can't engage in Combat later in the turn. Or if you engage in Combat, whether voluntary or mandatory,
you're not allowed to Interact with the Locals later in the same turn. So if you assault a MAGE TOWER/KEEP/CITY,
or attempt to burn a MONASTERY, or fight Rampaging Enemies, you can't have Interacted with the Locals earlier
in the turn nor do so later in the turn, even if the Combat is successful. See Combat S14, S17, and S40.206
S8:—Timing of Purchases—When you spend Influence, you usually gain your items immediately: re-
cruited Units can be used immediately (even to generate more Influence to buy more items); new Spells and
Advanced Actions go facedown on your Deed deck (even if empty) and can be drawn if some effect (like Moti-
vation) permits it; and Healing points can immediately heal (or be saved for later, though they're lost if you en-
ter Combat). Purchased Artifacts, however, are gained during your End-of-Turn sequence—see End of Turn S5.
Rewards from Combat (like those gained by fighting at TOMBS and ANCIENT RUINS) are different: they're
collected during step 5 of the End-of-Turn sequence. Units earned as Combat Rewards may be delayed until the
level-up step of the End-of-Turn sequence; purchased Units may not be. See Silver Units Q3.207
S9:—Interacting with the Locals—Effects that let you recruit from any tile space (like the strong effect
of Banner of Command) generally are not considered "interaction with the locals". So when you play effects
like it, you can still do an Action of your choice during your turn and can even play them while Resting.208
S10:—HEROES/THUGS inside CITIES—It's only your Reputation modifier that gets adjusted when you an-
nounce you're recruiting HEROES/THUGS. When you recruit them inside a CITY, you calculate the Reputation ef-
fect first. Only after that do you add the (unaltered) Influence bonus from any shield tokens on the CITY card.209
S11:—When Leveling Up—Level Ups happen after your turn. You cannot Level Up mid-turn and re-
cruit a Unit with a newly gained command token. Nor may you delay taking purchased Units until after a level-
up during your End-of-Turn sequence. See S8 above.210
Cross References: CITIES S21; Miscellaneous S1, S9; Basic Action Cards S2; Ruthless Coercion S1, S2;
Diplomacy S1; Learning S1; Call to Arms S7; Bonds of Loyalty S5; Bright Negotiation S1; Dark Negotiation
S1; Faction Tokens S2; Silver Units Q3; MAGIC FAMILIARS S1; THUGS O3; HEROES S2.
Healing
# Q1:—Unit Wounds—If you play an effect that Heals a Unit, what happens to a Unit with two wounds?
A1: Only one of the wounds is Healed unless the effect says something like "the Unit is fully healed".211
S2:—Wounds not in your Hand—Healing effects remove wounds from your hand, not from your dis-
card pile or draw deck. MAGICAL GLADES throw away a wound from your discard pile, but it's not "healing".212
S3:—MAGICAL GLADE—The rulebook (p. 10) says, "the MAGICAL GLADE site does not provide healing. Its
effect may not be used on Units and cannot be combined with other healing effects." IMO the second sentence
explains the first: the difference between healing and a MAGICAL GLADE is that a MAGICAL GLADE cannot heal
Units and that a MAGICAL GLADE lets you to throw away a Wound from your discard pile. Also, since a MAGICAL
GLADE is not "healing", it can't be used with, for instance, the strong effect of Golden Grail.213
S4:—Number of Times—When Interacting with the Locals, you may pay for as much healing as you can
afford. E.g., at VILLAGES you could spend 6 Influence to Heal two wounds from your hand.214
Cross References: Combat S3; CITIES S21; Influence S8; Resting Q1, S3, S5; End of Turn S1; Miscellan-
eous S9; Rejuvenate Q1; Cure S2, O3; Golden Grail S1; Healing Ritual Q4.


[PAGE 030]
p. 30 Resting, Minimum Turn
Resting
# Q1:—Choosing which Type of Resting—Your hand at the beginning of a turn is 4 wounds and Tranquil-
ity. You want to do a Standard Rest and use Tranquility to Heal a wound. But if you play Tranquility, you'll
have only wound cards in your hand and won't be able to discard a non-wound card. What happens?
A1: You have several options: (Assume your hand size is 5 cards.)
1. Take a normal turn, play Tranquility without mana to throw away 1 wound. Then draw 2 cards.
2. Take a normal turn, play Tranquility with mana to throw away 2 wounds. Then draw 3 cards.
3. Declare a Rest, play Tranquility without mana to throw away 1 wound, then show you have only
wounds left and do a Slow Recovery, discarding 1 wound card. Then draw 3 cards.
4. Declare a Rest, play Tranquility with mana to throw away 2 wounds, then show you have only
wounds left and do a Slow Recovery, discarding 1 wound card. Then draw 4 cards.
5. Declare a Rest, discard the unplayed Tranquility card to pay for a Standard Rest and discard all 4
wounds. Then draw 5 cards.
When you Rest, you don't declare which kind of Rest you're doing (Standard Rest or Slow Recovery):
you merely announce that you're Resting. You may play Special Effects and Healing cards, and at any moment
either (a) discard 1 non-wound card and any number of wound cards (including 0 if you wish), or (b) reveal
your hand to show you have nothing but wounds and discard 1 of them.215
Q2:—No Cards to Discard—You begin a turn with an empty deck and a hand of two cards, both of
which are wounds. You use HERBALIST to throw both away, leaving you without a legal discard. Is this legal?
A2: Yes. It's a special case of Slow Recovery where you have no wound cards to discard, because you
healed yourself beforehand (even though you had cards in your hand at the start of the turn).216
S3:—Playing Cards while Resting—Resting doesn't prevent you from playing cards: it merely prevents
you from Moving, Fighting, Interacting, or doing anything "as your Action". So you can play, say, Crystallize
to use your once-per-turn die from the SOURCE, or Tranquility to Heal or draw cards, or Learning or Training to
gain Advanced Action cards (even playing non-wound cards sideways for Influence), or Gem Rings to gain
crystals or mana tokens. While Resting, you can play most Special Effects and Healing cards, though it might
not always make sense to do so. You can also use many Skills and Units. You also gain the benefit of any space
you're standing on, like a MAGICAL GLADE or a MINE. But you have no Movement Phase or Action Phase.217
S4:—No Non-Wounds in your Hand—If you begin a turn with only wounds in your hand, you ordinarily
can't play a Skill (like Flight) to move to a new space (say to a VILLAGE, hoping to plunder it between turns), be-
cause you don't have a Movement Phase while you're Resting. But if you can somehow play a card, you aren't
forced to Rest. See Minimum Turn S2 and S3.
S5:—Healing while Resting—Buying Healing at a MONASTERY is "Interacting with the Locals", an Ac-
tion, and therefore isn't allowed while you're Resting. Throwing away a wound card at a MAGICAL GLADE, how-
ever, is an End-of-Turn step and therefore is allowed at the end of turns in which you Rest.218
Minimum Turn
# S1:—Playing or Discarding a Card—Every turn you must play at least one card from your hand. Fail-
ing that, you must discard one unplayed card from your hand. Using a Unit or Skill or Site to heal a wound or
move to a new space or block an attack doesn't suffice.219
Neither does "using a Skill as your Action" (like flip-
ping Shamanic Ritual). Nor does having a Unit disband or die, even if that Unit had a Banner: it's true that the
Unit gets thrown away and any Banner gets discarded, but neither is coming directly from your hand.220


[PAGE 031]
Minimum Turn, End of Turn p. 31
S2:—Playing a Card—The requirement to play at least one card could be satisfied by . . . 221
• Using Power of Pain to play a wound card sideways for 2 Move points.
• Playing a Banner from your hand onto a Unit.
• Playing an Artifact from your hand for its strong effect (which is then thrown away).
S3:—Do Skills Count?—These Skills do not count as having played a card:222
• I Feel No Pain: Discard one wound from hand.
• Invocation: Discard a wound card to gain a mana token.
• Healing Ritual: Throw away up to two wounds from your hand.
• Regenerate: Throw away a wound from your hand.
• Ritual of Pain: Throw away up to two wounds from your hand.
o But the second part of this Skill—"play a wound sideways"—would count.
End of Turn
# S1:—Playing Effects just before End of Turn—After your one allowed Action (even if it's Combat), you
are permitted to play Special Effects and Healing Effects (and Move and Influence Effects) before your End-of-
Turn sequence begins—but of course you can't move to new spaces or reveal tiles or Interact with the Locals.223
S2:—Restrictions during End of Turn—Once you announce your turn is over and you begin your End-
of-Turn sequence, your turn is officially over: you can no longer use Skills, Spells, etc.224
S3:—Fame after PvP Combat—If you Partially Attend a PvP combat and earn enough Fame to level up,
you don't level up until your normal turn. There is no End-of-Turn sequence when Partially Attending.225
Q4:—Rolling for Crystal Color—When you earn multiple crystals at once and have to roll to determine
color (MONSTER DEN, SPAWNING GROUND, Endless Game Pouch), must you roll a single die at a time?
A4: Yes, and presumably you choose the color of any gold result before any ensuing rolls.226
S5:—Artifacts—Artifacts won during combat or bought at the red CITY are gained during the End-of-
Turn sequence. You always draw one more Artifact than the number you're receiving, select all but one to keep,
and return the extra one to the bottom of the Artifact deck. The gained Artifacts are placed on top of your Deed
Deck. See CITIES S21 and Silver Units Q3.227
S6:—Adding Cards to your Deck—If you gain an Advanced Action card from leveling up on the same
turn that you gain a reward from combat (usually a Spell or an Artifact), you don't choose the order to place
them on your deed deck. Rewards from combat are placed first in step 5 of the End-of-Turn sequence, and lev-
eling up is done in step 6, so the card from leveling up goes on top of any reward from combat.228
S7:—Leveling Up—If you level up and earn an Advanced Action card and a Skill, you have to take
them both. You can't forgo the Advanced Action card just to make it easier to draw a different card.229
See also Influence S8 and S11.
S8:—Bonus Draws—If you're in or adjacent to a conquered CITY that has your shield tokens on it, your
hand size is +1 (+2, if CITY leader). This card-drawing bonus does not stack with the card bonus for KEEPS: take
only the larger bonus. See CITIES S20. Nor do you get the KEEP bonus twice if you're adjacent to two KEEPS.230
S9:—Errata in Ultimate Edition—Step 3a in the End-of-Turn sequence on p. 11 of the Ultimate Edition
rulebook should read "Return all mana tokens . . . to the bank", not "all mana crystals".231


[PAGE 032]
p. 32 End of Turn, Forced Withdrawal, End of Round
S10:—Exceeding Your Hand Limit—If you begin your End-of-Turn sequence with more hand cards
than your current hand limit, you keep them (unless you use a MAGICAL GLADE to throw away a wound, or un-
less you choose to discard unused non-wound cards in step 7a). You're not forced to discard excess cards.232
If,
during End-of-Turn step 7b, you have as many (or more) cards in your hand than your current hand limit, you
simply do not draw cards (per step 7c).
Your hand limit can be temporarily increased by being near KEEPS or CITIES, and through other effects
like the Tactic card Planning.233
Forced Withdrawal
# S1:—Retreating from Combat—If you don't defeat all of the defenders of a fortified site, you go back to
the space from which you assaulted it. See CITIES S3. This, though, is not Forced Withdrawal.
Forced Withdrawal only applies at the end of your turn, and only if your current space is not a safe
space. This usually happens only if you conducted an assault/attack from a lake space while using some sort of
water-walking Skill. Or while assaulting/attacking from a mountain space. It can also happen if an enemy with
Pursuit moves into the space you're conducting an assault from. Or if you end your Movement Phase on a space
with another mage knight when PvP is not allowed: in this case you lose your Action Phase for the turn—see
Combat S32.234
S2:—Taking Wounds—When you retreat from a fortified site that you failed to conquer (CITY, KEEP,
MAGE TOWER), you don't take a wound. Wounds only happen if Forced Withdrawal ensues.235
S3:—Provoking Rampaging Enemies—Neither retreating from a failed assault nor making a Forced
Withdrawal provokes Rampaging Enemies. Nor does either cost you Movement points.236
S4:—Retreat Route—With Forced Withdrawal, you retrace your path, space by space, until you reach a
safe space, even if your approach path occurred across several turns. If you used magical assistance to skip over
spaces, you must decide how to handle those: you could (a) "unwind" the magical assistance and make the same
hops in reverse (for free), or you could (b) trace a retreat through the skipped-over spaces. With CITY assaults,
you undo any final hop, so there's precedence for option (a). See CITIES S23 and Mountain Lore O3.237
Cross References: Movement S4; Combat S4, S19, S32; Scenarios S1; VOLKARE O6; Frost Bridge S2;
Mountain Lore S2, O3; Song of Wind S3; Mist Form Q5; Space Bending S5.
End of Round
# S1:—Declaring End of Round—When you declare the End of the Round, you can't do anything else—
you can't use Skills, cards, or Units, and you don't do the End-of-Turn sequence (so no throwing away Wounds
while on a MAGICAL GLADE). The only exception applies to cooperative and competitive Skills in the center:
these do get returned to you. See Skill Tokens S4 and Turn Start S1.238
S2:—Competitive Skills and Interactive Spells—After the end of the Round has been announced (or an
end-of-game state has been triggered), you cannot "affect other players with competitive Skills or interactive
Spells". Competitive Skills and interactive Spells that were played before the End-of-Round was announced—
but which are still in effect—continue to apply.239
After the End-of-Round is announced, if Mana Meltdown
gets played it does nothing, and if Mana Radiance gets played it applies only to the player playing it.240
S3:—Mandatory—All End-of-Round steps are mandatory: you must ready Units and flip Skills, etc.241


[PAGE 033]
End of Round, Final Scoring, Miscellaneous p. 33
S4:—Discarding Cards—At the end of the Round, you discard all of your cards—those in your hand
and those in your deed deck, including all wounds and any cards from leveling up or from combat rewards. All
of them get shuffled together in preparation for the new Round.242
S5:—Leveling Up after PvP—If, while Partially Attending a PvP combat, you earn enough Fame to lev-
el up and then on your next turn you announce the end of the Round, you won't actually level up until the end of
your first turn in the new Round. See S1 above and End of Turn S3.243
S6:—Revealing Tokens on the Map—At the start of every Day Round, you reveal all RUINS tokens and
you reveal all enemy tokens in fortified locations adjacent to Mage Knights. This happens before any player
chooses a new Tactic card, during step 2.a of preparing the Round.244
Cross References: Movement S8; Miscellaneous S10.
Final Scoring
# S1:—Greatest Leader—When calculating the Fame for your Units (Walkthrough, p. 19), you halve the
level of each wounded Unit, round down, then sum. [A fair house rule is to sum, halve, then round down.]245
S2:—Unconquered CITIES—Shield tokens on unconquered CITIES do not count during final scoring. So
in conquest scenarios, you will not earn 4 Fame per shield token on those CITIES that were not conquered.246
S3:—Conquered-CITY Bonus—In Cooperative and Solo scenarios, the VPs for "all CITIES being con-
quered" is referring to only those CITIES that were to have been in-play during that scenario.
S4:—Award No Titles—When the Solo Variant says "award no titles", it's talking about the "trophy"
points: e.g., a player still gets 2 Fame for each Spell and 1 Fame for each Advanced Action in his deck, but he
doesn't get the extra 3 Fame for the Greatest Knowledge category. The same goes for the other Greatest ____
categories, including Greatest Beating.247
S5:—Cards Thrown Away—Any cards thrown away during the game are "out of the game" and do not
count in any way toward final scoring.248
Cross References: CITIES S17; Scenarios O31.
Miscellaneous
# S1:—Effects Must Be Usable—Effects that generate "something" can, in general, only be played during
phases in which you could use that "something". This applies to Deed cards, Units, and Skills. See Mana Draw
S2 and S3, Basic Action Cards O5, and Combat S13. So you can't play Maximal Effect and throw away Rage
unless you're in combat. You can, however, play more Attack/Block points than are currently needed—or few-
er, for that matter. And you can play Movement points and choose not to use them, or activate PEASANTS for In-
fluence and choose not to use it. See S9 below and Dual-Colored Action Cards O4.249
S2:—Examining Discard Piles—Every player's discard pile is open information, should the owner or
another player wish to examine at it. By extension, this also applies to the Dummy player's discard pile.250
S3:—Hand Limit—Your hand limit never forces you to discard cards, even if you're over that limit. See
End of Turn S10.251


[PAGE 034]
p. 34 Miscellaneous
S4:—Card Draws—Card draws are mandatory: you can't skip them. See also End of Round S3.252
S5:—Plundering VILLAGES—You can plunder a VILLAGE before your turn even if you're the first person
to take your turn during a new Round. See Turn Start S1.253
S6:—Removing RUINS—If you pay mana to activate an ANCIENT ALTAR, or if you defeat all of the enemy
tokens at a RUINS site, you discard the RUINS token and mark the site with a shield token.254
The icons on the RUINS tiles are not defined in the base game rulebook—you have to infer them from p.
16 of the base game Walkthrough. They are shown correctly in the Ultimate Edition rule-
book (p. 10, pictured to the right), but in the Ultimate Edition Walkthrough (p. 16) the Unit
reward icon is incorrect.
S7:—Refilling the Offers—The Advanced Action Offer and Spell Offer are refilled immediately when-
ever a card is taken. If you purchase two Spells from a MAGE TOWER during the same Interaction, immediately
after the first Spell is selected, another Spell is added to the Offer, giving you three choices of Spells for your
second purchase. (This does not happen with the Unit Offer, or with MONASTERY Advanced Actions cards.)255
S8:—The "Missing" Walkthrough Rules—Some rules in the Walkthrough are not in the rulebook:256
(1) Using Advanced Action cards 1-16 when playing First Reconnaissance (p. 4), and having at least
one Unit card with a VILLAGE icon (p. 5).
(2) The meaning of the RUINS icons (p. 16). See S6 above.
(3) The "rule" which limits when Unit abilities can be played. See Silver Units O8.
(4) The final scoring rules (pp. 18-19). (Fixed in the Ultimate Edition.)
(5) The icons for the various mana colors (p. 6). These can be inferred, however.
(6) Text about the "Special Effects" icon on some Unit cards (p. 9). (Fixed in the Ultimate Edition.)
(7) Not removing Advanced Action cards from the Unit Offer when a MONASTERY is burned (p. 15).
(8) Removing RUINS tokens when someone fulfills them (p. 16). See S6 above.
(9) Mage Knights cannot exceed level 10, though Fame can go past the end of the Fame track (p. 17).
(10) Assaulting an opponent's KEEP earns half Fame, rounding up (p. 14). Discard unbeaten tokens.257
(11) You cannot refresh a Unit during Combat so as to allow it to act twice during the same fight (p. 10).
(12) You should flip your Tactic card facedown once you've used its ability (p. 13).
(13) At night, black mana does not work as a wild card like gold mana does during the day (p. 18).
(14) The Lost Legion p. 6 rule about the secondary effect of Cooperative Skills in Solo Games is missing
from the Ultimate Edition rulebook (but it is on p. 17 of the Ultimate Edition Walkthrough).
S9:—Pool Points—Healing points, Influence points, and Movement points, when played, are accumu-
lated in a notional pool of points, a separate pool for each type. They are then spent from these pools of points
whenever and however the player wishes, until either the points are all spent or until the pools of points disap-
pear. (Note that Healing Effects that direct you to discard or throw away a Wound—as with I Feel No Pain or
Healing Ritual—are not giving you "Healing points".) For an example, see this endnote.258
Influence pool points and Movement pool points disappear when you transition from your Movement
Phase to your Action Phase. Healing pool points disappear when (and if) you enter Combat. And all pool points
disappear when your End-of-Turn sequence begins.
Attack points and Block points, conversely, do not go into pools. Almost always they're spent immedi-
ately to attack a single (group of) enemy tokens/block a single attack. Excess Attack/Block points cannot be re-
directed against different groups of enemy tokens or against different attacks. See Combat O46 and S49.
These concepts affect the Cumbersome ability and cards like Diplomacy: for these, you can spend Influ-
ence/Movement points from its pool against multiple attacks, even if those points came from just one card.259


[PAGE 035]
Miscellaneous p. 35
S10:—Night Rules—During Night Rounds, and while in DUNGEONS/TOMBS/NECROPOLIS/GRAVEYARD,
(but not necessarily while using the Amulet of Darkness), Night Rules are in effect. This means that (a) black
mana can be used to power effects (like the strong effect of Spells) and gold mana is unusable, and (b) Skills
with differing effects during Day/Night (Bright Negotiation, Night Sharpshooting) use their Night values.
Other things also change: MAGICAL GLADES give black mana, not gold; tokens in KEEPS/MAGE TOWERS
are not revealed when you move adjacent (but you can make blind Assaults against them); RUINS tokens are not
automatically revealed; the move cost of forests and deserts change; and you use Night Tactic cards.260
See also Mana Pull S1, Blood Ritual S2, Amulet of Darkness S1, Amulet of the Sun S1, Endless Gem
Pouch S1, Dark Fire Magic Q1, Invocation S1, Polarization S1, Regeneration S2, Universal Power S4, Orb of
Twilight S1, ALTEM MAGES Q3 and Q5, Spell Cards S2, and Movement S8.261
S11:—ANCIENT RUINS—One ANCIENT RUINS that came with Lost Legion shows four different color mana
tokens at the top. When activating this ALTAR, you pay four mana, one of each basic color: ignore the text on the
Site Description card that says "pay 3 mana of the color shown".
The ANCIENT RUINS defended by three ORC MARAUDERS, and the one defended by a gray and a violet en-
emy, both reward you with a free Unit. Take one Unit of your choice from the Unit Offer, Regular or Elite (but
never DELPHANA MASTERS or MAGIC FAMILIARS). If the Unit Offer is empty, you get nothing. See S6 above.262
S12:—Standalone Points vs. Tack-on Points—Some Deed Cards/Skills/Units give points that can be
used by themselves; others give points that must be tacked onto points gotten elsewhere.
Freezing Power provides a Siege Attack of 1: this point can be used independently despite the "+" sym-
bol on the token. The points from these Skills can all be used independently: Arcane Disguise, Battle Frenzy,
Beguile, Bright Negotiation, Burning Power, Cold Swordsmanship, Dark Negotiation, Dark Paths, Day Sharp-
shooting, Double Time, Dueling, Feral Allies, Forked Lightning, Hawk Eyes, Hot Swordsmanship, Master of
Chaos, Night Sharpshooting, On Her Own, Puppet Master, Refreshing Bath, Refreshing Breeze, Secret Ways,
and Shield Mastery. Similarly, Dodge and Weave gives standalone attack points, though of course they can be
added with other Attack points. The same goes for Bow of Starsdawn and Sword of Justice. And cards played
sideways don't need to be tacked onto other effects.263
But Leadership (a Skill that also uses the "+" symbol) does require that the Unit it benefits have a base
Attack/Ranged Attack/Block. Banner of Glory likewise requires a Unit with a base Block or Attack ability. The
same goes for Into the Heat and for the first ability of SHOCKTROOPS. Ambush, similarly, requires a base Attack
or base Block from a Deed card, as does Deadly Aim. The Move point from Spirit Guides is standalone, but the
Block point must be added onto another Block effect. Shapeshift also requires a Basic Action card that provides
points. And Vial of Toxin requires a physical attack it can modify.
If in doubt, assume points may be used by themselves unless the text clearly indicates otherwise.264
S13:—Walkthrough Errata—On Walkthrough p. 8 under "Action Cards : Special Cards", the last pic-
ture shows another way to get eight Move points: you play Promise sideways, Stamina without mana, then Im-
provisation with a red die plus a discard (a second Promise). But in MKBG no player starts with two Promise
cards (though there's a rare way to gain a second Promise card using Mind Steal). A close-eyed reader, there-
fore, might wonder if this example is showing a single Action card being used for two purposes: being played
sideways for one Move point and then being discarded to satisfy Improvisation. But doing that wouldn't be le-
gal: you can't use a card for more than one purpose. See Minimum Turn S1.265
S14:—Exploring ADVENTURE SITES—To enter and explore an ADVENTURE SITE—RUINS, DUNGEON, TOMB,
MONSTER DEN, SPAWNING GROUND, MAZE, LABYRINTH—you must be standing on the space: being adjacent isn't
sufficient. (A MAZE/LABYRINTH cannot already be marked with one of your shield tokens.) While on an ADVEN-
TURE SITE you can also choose to ignore it, whether just passing through the space, beginning your turn on it,
ending your turn on it, pausing there during the Movement Phase, or attacking Rampaging Enemies from it. See
Combat S18 and S40.266


[PAGE 036]
p. 36 Miscellaneous, Variants
S15:—SPAWNING GROUND—Anytime you enter an unvanquished SPAWNING GROUND, you will face two
MONSTERS, a new token being drawn to replace a previous kill. You have to kill both of them to earn the stated
Combat reward and to place a shield token there.267
S16:—Enemy Tokens in ANCIENT RUINS—Some ANCIENT RUINS have enemies you must defeat to earn a
reward. These enemies—when encountered elsewhere—are often Fortified, fought under Night Rules, or earn
you Reputation for defeating them. But those things, in general, don't necessarily apply to enemies in RUINS.268
S17:—Drawing Cards—Whenever a rule says to draw a card, unless it explicitly states otherwise, it's
referring to drawing a card from the top of your shuffled, face-down, as-yet-undrawn Deed card deck.
S18:—Reshuffling Decks—If the Advanced Action, Spell, Artifact, or Unit decks are ever exhausted,
they don't get reshuffled. During play, most cards are simply returned to the bottom of their respective decks.
But note that some cards do get removed from the game (replaced or killed Units, and thrown away cards). In
any case it's seldom that you'll play all of the way through these decks, especially with low player counts: but if
you do play through them, simply keep cycling your way through the various decks. [A house rule that says you
reshuffle exhausted decks is also perfectly playable.]269
Variants
# S1:—Starting at a Higher Level—When starting at a higher level and wanting to begin the game with a
MAGIC FAMILIARS, you must pay the Influence cost of 6 and also provide a crystal (which you could buy for 3
Influence). You would not, however, have to pay another crystal at the beginning of the first Round.270
S2:—Starting at a Higher Level—When starting at a higher level, the two crystals you begin with will
be different colors. E.g., GOLDYX gets one green and one blue: he can't choose to get two greens.
Players should draw and select Skills in secret at the same time as other players, and also buy their
Units, crystals, and other things before revealing any of them. The specific cards that're chosen are kept secret,
but players should tell others what type of items they've spent points on so the others can check their math.
No Skills from the Dummy get added to the initial Common Skill Offer. And the Dummy deck and crys-
tals are the same as they would be if you were not playing with this variant.
Cards and Skills are gained on even-numbered levels, not odd-numbered as incorrectly stated: you draw
Advanced Action cards and Skills at levels 2, 4, 6, etc. This erratum was fixed in the Ultimate Edition.
In the p. 13 table, the number of Units dealt to each player should be "character level + 1", not "charac-
ter level + 2". (This was corrected in the Ultimate Edition.) If NOROWAS selects Bonds of Loyalty, he adds 2 reg-
ular Units to his pre-setup deal of Units (two more than specified in the table), and gets -5 to the cost of 1 Unit.
You draw "2 + character level" Advanced Action cards. You choose your free cards from this stack.
You may then buy others from this stack for 6 Influence each. So if you're beginning at level 3, you would draw
5 Advanced Action cards, pick one for free, and then may buy any of the remaining four for 6 Influence each.
When ready, place your shield token on the leftmost space of the Fame track on the row corresponding
to your starting level (e.g., start with 3 Fame at level 2; 8 Fame at level 3). If playing Blitz (or a scenario that
grants extra Fame for every line), add 1 Fame per level (e.g., start with 4 Fame for level 2; 10 for level 3).271
O3:—Rampage!—Rampaging Enemies don't respawn if there's a Knight on their space.
The Reputation you earn for killing Rampaging Enemies is ambiguously worded: "Reputation +1 for
each enemy token defeated" could mean that all respawned Rampaging Enemies earn 1 Reputation each, or that
the Reputation for non-respawned Rampaging Enemies is also altered, or that all enemy tokens (even non-Ram-
paging Enemies) earn Reputation, or that the +1 Reputation is in addition to that for killing Rampaging Enemies
on these respawned spaces (1 for brown respawned enemies, 2 for green, 3 for red). You could argue for any of
these. (I give brown respawned Rampaging Enemies 1 Reputation, and leave green and red enemies as is.)272


[PAGE 037]
Variants, Scenarios p. 37
S4:—Interactive Combat—When facing an enemy token that has Multiple Attacks and your opponent
chooses the red die face, he adds +1 to only one of the Attacks—but he gets to choose which Attack. And he
chooses the die face after the enemy token is revealed.273
S5:—Daytime Variability - Darkness is Coming—With this variant, you may see more than three Day
turns or three Night turns. If so, you could run out of Tactic cards, which means you'll have to adjust the rules.
Perhaps force players to play a Round without choosing any Tactic card, or reduce the number of Tactic cards
that are discarded, or provide a mechanism to return some or all of the thrown-away Tactic cards to play.274
S6:—Team Rules—Hand limit increases for being adjacent to an ally's Keep does not apply for being
adjacent to an ally's CITY: to gain hand limit increases for CITIES, you must have shield tokens in that CITY.275
Scenarios
# S1:—Faction Leaders in Combat—276
The Faction Leader's Army: In a TEZLA scenario, you can defeat a faction leader without killing all of
its followers. If you do so, at the end of Combat you remove any followers that're still alive and you remain in
the now-liberated space. You don't, though, earn Fame or Faction Tokens for any followers that "get away".277
If the faction leader dies in the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase, its followers can still attack you in the
Assign Damage Phase, but the faction leader cannot (nor can any Summons Attacks it had while alive).278
Whether or not you kill the faction leader, any slain followers do not respawn.
Assaults on Faction Leaders: An attack on a faction leader is always an Assault (c.f., HEROES), but you
don't lose Reputation for it.279
If you don't defeat him, you retreat and must reenter his space to re-fight him.280
The NECROPOLIS and HIDDEN VALLEY are not fortified, but they are unsafe spaces until liberated. So if
you fail in battle, you retreat to where you attacked from and this isn't considered a Forced Withdrawal. It's dif-
ferent when fighting enemy tokens in GRAVEYARDS: there, you are not forced out if you don't succeed.
Killing Faction Leaders: If a faction leader is, say, level 4, you must Attack with 4 times his armor to
kill him. But you cannot make, say, an Attack 20 in the Attack Phase and a separate Fire Attack 16 in the same
Attack Phase and include him in both attacks.281
You can, though, attack him in the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack
Phase and then again in the Attack Phase. Any damage he takes in the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase does not
lower his Attack in the Block Phase (unless he was fully defeated in the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase), nor
does a successful Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase attack lower his armor during the Attack Phase.
The Defend Ability: The Defend ability can be used on a faction leader to increase his armor, and that
increase will apply during the entire Combat Phase. See Combat S23 and S29.
S2:—Cooperative Assaults vs. Faction Leaders—Cooperative Assault agreements on how many levels
each player will damage a leader are permitted, but they are non-binding and don't deny players Fame for the
full damage they do inflict (3 Fame per level defeated, whether or not the leader dies). If he has already been
killed, you can't attack him, and any player who resolves her attacks after his defeat is not attacked by him.282
S3:—Cooperative Assaults vs. Faction Leaders—When victorious, the player who initiated the coopera-
tive assault remains in the space where the battle was fought. The other players return to their earlier spaces.283
Q4:—Faction Leader Summon Attacks—If you make an assault on the DARK CRUSADER faction leader
(DCBOSS) when the DCBOSS is at level ten, he'll summon two DARK CRUSADER DRACONUM enemy tokens (DC-
DRAGONS) to use for his attack. Treat the DCDRAGONS like summoned MONSTERS: you draw them at the start of
the Block Phase and they stand in for the attack of the DCBOSS.
There're 4 DCDRAGON tokens in total. If the DCBOSS begins the scenario at ≥ level 8, he gets 2 DCDRAG-
ONS in his army. So if players make a cooperative assault against a level-ten DCBOSS, does the DCBOSS draw 2
different DCDRAGONS for his attacks on each player, or does he use the same 2 DCDRAGONS for all opponents?


[PAGE 038]
p. 38 Scenarios
A4: The DCBOSS draws his tokens from the DARK CRUSADER pool if non-empty, otherwise he draws them
from the non-faction pool.284
(DCDRAGONS in the dead pile are available to be drawn.)
So if 2 DCDRAGONS are in the DCBOSS army and 1 DCDRAGON is elsewhere on the map, there'll be only 1
DCDRAGON token that can be drawn. In cooperative assaults, each player conducts her attacks in turn against the
level-ten DCBOSS, and that boss draws 2 DCDRAGONS during the Block Phase for his attack. If only 1 DCDRAGON
token is not in use, (and if the first player doesn't defeat either DCDRAGON in the DCBOSS army), then the first
two players in the cooperative assault will face an Attack from the DCBOSS as represented by the draw of the
same fourth DCDRAGON and the draw of a different (non-faction) DRACONUM token. If the 2nd player defeats
both DCDRAGONS in the DCBOSS army, the 3rd player in the cooperative assault faces an attack from the DCBOSS
as represented by the draw of two of the three now-available DCDRAGON tokens.
If any summoned enemy token is itself a SUMMONER, see SHROUDED NECROMANCER S1.285
S5:—Running out of Enemy Tokens—If you run out of faction enemy tokens, reshuffle any killed enemy
tokens of the same faction and type (MONSTER, ORC MARAUDERS, DRACONUM) to form a new draw stack. If you
still fall short, decide randomly which enemy tokens—faction or non-faction—go in each location.286
S6:—GRAVEYARD and NECROPOLIS—Night Rules (Miscellaneous S10) apply when you're in a GRAVE-
YARD or NECROPOLIS. If you start your turn there at night, you get a black mana token—but not during the day.
A Skill that has different day/night values (e.g., Day Sharpshooting) uses the night version. The strong effect of
Endless Gem Pouch gives a black mana token. If you explore a tile during the day while on a GRAVEYARD/NE-
CROPOLIS, you won't reveal RUINS nor reveal enemy tokens in adjacent KEEPS/MAGE TOWERS. See S1 above.287
S7:—GRAVEYARD—A GRAVEYARD is not a MAGICAL GLADE nor does it become one when you kill every-
thing in it—it remains a GRAVEYARD.288
Q8:—Removing Tactic Cards—In which scenarios do Tactic cards get removed at the end of a Round?
A8: In Solo Conquest, both used Tactic cards get removed. In Solo games versus VOLKARE, only the
player's Tactic card is removed (VOLKARE'S card remains available). In Multiplayer Coop with a dummy player,
players must agree on and remove one of their own Tactic cards—the dummy's Tactic card remains in play.289
S9:—Cooperative Scenarios—In cooperative scenarios, Team Rules are used with everyone being on
the same team. Conquered KEEPS are friendly and can be entered without combat; Units can be recruited there;
and your hand limit is increased when you're adjacent to them by one card for every KEEP you yourself own.290
S10:—First Reconnaissance—Use only Advanced Action cards 1 through 16. Redraw the Unit Offer
until at least one Unit has a VILLAGE icon. Reveal Countryside Tiles in increasing numeric order.291
S11:—Mines Liberation—MINES are safe spaces. You can be on a MINE without having to fight either
enemy. The brown enemy isn't revealed by being adjacent to the space, or even by being on the space.292
S12:—Mines Liberation—There's no official solo mode. A custom approach is to use 7 countryside tiles
(4 having MINES), 1 core tile (with a MINE), and the red CITY, and to play the scenario for 4, 5, or 6 Rounds.293
S13:—Mines Liberation—If you're given a crystal at the start of a Round and already have three crystals
of that color, you gain nothing, not even a same-colored mana token. See Mana S9.294
O14:—Mines Liberation—At the beginning of new Rounds, are you given crystals from liberated mines
before or after you "pay" your MAGIC FAMILIARS? And can you choose which color comes from DEEP MINES?
A14: There's no definite answer to this, so I'd permit players to receive the crystals first. I'd also allow
players to choose their crystal color, since there's nothing overruling the DEEP MINES site card.295


[PAGE 039]
Scenarios p. 39
S15:—Druid Nights—An "uninhabited space" is any space where you can't Interact with the Locals.296
S16:—Dungeon Lords—KRANG cannot enter-and-quit a tunnel via the same entrance/exit just to use
Savage Harvest to gain crystals.297
Q17:—Dungeon Lords—If KRANG plays the strong effect of Savage Harvesting and enters a tunnel, can
he discard a card for every hex he travels underground in order to gain a crystal?
A17: Yes. The scenario says you pay "for each space you travel", so you are moving "spaces".298
Q18:—Dungeon Lords—What happens if you can’t legally place a secret tomb near a MONASTERY, like
when placing tile 12, as the MONASTERY is surrounded by a mountain, a Rampaging Enemy, and a swamp?
A18: You don't place any secret tomb, as you can't fulfill the restrictions on where to place one.299
S19:—Conquer and Hold—Surviving enemy tokens in opponents' KEEPS/MAGE TOWERS are removed:
when you attack again, draw new enemy tokens.300
S20:—Conquer and Hold—If the other player is present in his KEEP, you conduct a PvP combat instead
of a combat with enemy tokens, just as you would in a standard scenario.301
S21:—TEZLA Scenarios—Except in Lost Relic, when placing enemy tokens on map sites and in ADVEN-
TURE SITES, use regular (non-TEZLA) enemy tokens unless DARK CRUSADER/ELEMENTALIST tokens are specified.
If players use the option of placing extra countryside tiles after the last core tile has been placed, oddities
can occur if you place GRAVEYARDS on newly exposed MAGICAL GLADES. So maybe exclude extra countryside
tiles that have MAGICAL GLADES, or treat the newly exposed spaces as basic MAGICAL GLADES.302
S22:—Life and Death—When you enter a TOMB/DUNGEON/MONSTER DEN/SPAWNING GROUNDS/MAZE/
LABYRINTH/RUINS, pick your enemy tokens from the base game tokens, not from the faction enemy tokens.303
S23:—Life and Death—When you clear the HIDDEN VALLEY/NECROPOLIS, the hexagonal tile remains on
the map and is treated as a MAGICAL GLADE/GRAVEYARD. (A CITY doesn't suddenly appear out of nowhere.) Nor
does clearing it count as liberating the space: you aren't rewarded an Artifact/Spell/Reputation (but it's now a
safe space). Nor is there a reward for defeating the High Priestess/Necromancer, other than Fame per level.304
The 3rd
paragraph under MAGICAL GLADES in the Ultimate Edition can leave you wondering if only one
enemy token is placed on "border" MAGICAL GLADES. The original rulebook is clear, though: you place two.305
S24:—Realm of the Dead—If played with four or five players, the 5th GRAVEYARD has a brown and a
red DARK CRUSADER token, and the 6th has a green, brown, and red DARK CRUSADER token. The reward for the
5th GRAVEYARD is an Artifact and Advanced Action card; for the 6th, an Artifact and a Spell.306
S25:—Realm of the Dead—Various rulings:
• Rampaging Enemies placed while exploring new map tiles do not gain Pursuit until the ensuing turn.
• Although it is always night in GRAVEYARDS, GRAVEYARDS do not provide black mana during day turns.
• The MAGICAL GLADE on the green CITY tile is a MAGICAL GLADE, not a GRAVEYARD.
• MAGICAL GLADES on tiles placed during setup are GRAVEYARDS, so remember the order of placement.307
O26:—Lost Relic—Lost Relic uses the Blitz rules, but doesn't list a starting Reputation. This is likely an
oversight. Many players start the scenario with +2 Reputation, as in other Blitz scenarios.308
S27:—Lost Relic—You use PORTAL tile side B, so you begin with three countryside tiles revealed.309


[PAGE 040]
p. 40 Scenarios, VOLKARE
S28:—Lost Relic—DESTROYED CITIES provide no fortifications.310
ORC MARAUDERS Rampaging Enemies
that replace DRACONUM Rampaging Enemies score only one Reputation bonus when killed, and DRACONUM on
DESTROYED CITIES score no Reputation bonus.311
S29:—Lost Relic—The text "you grab the relic from its treasure hoard" is simply flavor text.312
O30:—End Conditions—The Full Conquest and Blitz Conquest scenarios say, "When all cities are con-
quered, all players . . . have one last turn. If the Round ends during this, the game ends immediately." The Full
Cooperation and Solo Conquest scenarios lack the latter sentence, but that's likely an oversight.313
O31:—Solo Scoring—In Solo Conquest, there's no mention of a player receiving victory points for be-
ing a CITY leader, but the scoring card lists the CITY Leader victory points under the heading "Conquest Scenari-
os" and Solo Conquest is certainly a Conquest scenario. I include these victory points but many players do not
—so be aware of this when comparing scores.314
S32:—Mines Liberation—To keep the difficulty of this scenario constant, if you own Lost Legion, take
the six Countryside tiles that have MINES, shuffle them, and discard two. Separately shuffle the other Country-
side tiles and draw as many as are needed to make your pool. For non-CITY Core tiles, remove tiles 1, 9, and V.
When playing with 3 players, consider including five MINE Countryside tiles, and six with 4 players.315
S33:—Realm of the Dead—GRAVEYARDS can be sealed on the turn they're liberated, or later on.316
VOLKARE
# S1:—Orienting the CAMP Tile—In all but the most recent Ultimate Edition printing, the
rulebook shows the tile orientation correctly for VOLKARE'S Quest but the scenario card does not.
If you ignore the scenario card and orient the CAMP tile according to the tile-placement
rules, trace the arrows in relation to the other map tiles as shown on the scenario card, not in rela-
tion to the CAMP tile orientation. I.e., VOLKARE always moves toward the PORTAL, not toward the
edge of the map: the green arrow points from VOLKARE'S CAMP tile diagonally down to the left; the
white & blue arrows, diagonally down to the right. Don't move off the CAMP tile through the yellow
Desert-Village when moving along the white & blue arrows—move through the Hill hex.317
S2:—The CAMP Tile—In VOLKARE'S Return, VOLKARE'S CAMP does not count as a core tile in the sense
of permitting you to place elite (gold) Units in the Unit Offer: you must reveal a non-CAMP core tile first.318
S3:—The CAMP Tile—When playing with the "VOLKARE'S CAMP in Place of a CITY" variant, multiple
Mage Knights are not permitted to be in the CAMP simultaneously (unlike with CITIES and the PORTAL).319
Q4:—When Attacked by VOLKARE—Is it an Assault when you're defending against VOLKARE?
A4: No.320
Nor are the green and red enemy tokens in VOLKARE'S Army "Rampaging Enemies": you
don't provoke them by movement nor do you earn Reputation points for defeating them.321
Q5:—When Attacked by VOLKARE—When attacked by VOLKARE (or by another player), do you get to use
the “at the start of your turn” effects like MAGICAL GLADE?
A5: Only if you fully attend the combat. See Turn Start S1 and PvP Combat Q1.322
If, in VOLKARE'S Quest, VOLKARE draws a red card and attacks you from an adjacent space, and you fully
attend the fight and defeat enough of his army to slow him down, then the following happens: used mana dice
are returned to the SOURCE, then on your turn you flip your round token face up, then VOLKARE on his turn flips
his round token face up, then you take a full turn, then VOLKARE takes a full turn, etc.323


[PAGE 041]
VOLKARE p. 41
O6:—When Attacked by VOLKARE—What happens if VOLKARE moves onto a Mage Knight's space to at-
tack her, and the Mage Knight retreats, but none of the surrounding spaces are safe? [I'd say the player can
choose her retreat direction, but can't retreat to or through the space VOLKARE advanced from. I'd also say she
has to choose from among the shortest retreat (number of spaces), and has to avoid prohibited terrain.]324
Q7:—VOLKARE on a Fortified Site—Can you attack VOLKARE if he's on an unconquered fortified site?
A7: Yes. VOLKARE'S presence overrides everything else: you face VOLKARE'S Army and disregard the
unconquered fortified site (except for the fortification it gives to VOLKARE'S Army). The terrain of the space is
used only to determine movement costs, though you can't attack VOLKARE if you can't enter the terrain. The for-
tified site and the enemies in it are otherwise ignored. If you end up on an unsafe space after defeating VOL-
KARE, you'll have to retreat—not that it matters, as scenarios usually end when you defeat VOLKARE.325
Q8:—Revealing VOLKARE'S Army—When is VOLKARE'S Army first revealed?
A8: In VOLKARE'S Return and VOLKARE'S Quest, the Army is revealed when a player begins a combat
against VOLKARE. The Army isn't revealed from an adjacent space, or if you decline combat and flee.326
With the "VOLKARE'S Camp in Place of a CITY" variant, tokens behave as if there were in a CITY.327
Q9:—Revealing VOLKARE'S Army—What if you run out of tokens when revealing VOLKARE'S army?
A9: Improvise, because component shortages are not meant to be a limiting factor.328
S10:—Revealing VOLKARE'S Army—Enemy tokens added to VOLKARE'S Army after it has been revealed
are added face-down. See Q8 above.329
S11:—VOLKARE'S Recruits—In VOLKARE'S Return, VOLKARE recruits the Unit marked by the matching
crystal. But what that Unit is doesn't matter, as VOLKARE always takes a random gray enemy token.330
S12:—Indecisive Units—When playing VOLKARE'S Return Epic scenario at the Thrilling race level with
4 players, there'll be 6 indecisive Units. Use a black and gold crystal for the 5th and 6th indecisive Units. And
yes, players can recruit indecisive Units just as they can recruit Units that aren't indecisive.331
S13:—Indecisive Units—If you aren't using gold or black crystals to mark indecisive Units (see S12
above), and you roll gold or black on the die, then Volkare has failed to recruit an indecisive Unit.332
S14:—Defeating VOLKARE'S Army—If you assault VOLKARE'S CAMP and provoke/challenge Rampaging
Enemies as you do so, and if you defeat the last of VOLKARE'S Army in the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase but
not the Rampaging Enemies, then VOLKARE will not attack you during the Block and Assign Damage Phases: he
is already defeated—Rampaging Enemies have no effect on that.
Note also this new rule in the Ultimate Edition (p. 8): Special Case: If a player defeats their entire por-
tion of enemies in [the] Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase, their part of the combat ends. They do not have to face
VOLKARE'S attack, even if VOLKARE is not defeated because another player does not defeat all their troops.333
S15:—Victory in VOLKARE'S Quest—If you don't stop VOLKARE and kill all of his defenders by the end of
six rounds, you lose.334
S16:—Number of Players—In VOLKARE'S Quest, he is slowed down if he loses twice as many tokens
from his army as there're players in the game: this is referring to human players and doesn't count VOLKARE.335
S17:—Adding Skills to the Skill Offer—VOLKARE'S Quest and VOLKARE'S Return say to "add one Skill of
[a randomly chosen] hero to the Common Skill Offer after each of your level ups." Most players see this as a
typo and think it should say "add a Skill after each of your level ups in which you gain a Skill".336


[PAGE 042]
p. 42 VOLKARE
S18:—Placing New Tiles—VOLKARE and players obey the same rules when placing tiles. Neither can
explore in illegal directions. If VOLKARE wants to move illegally, move him in the closest legal direction.337
In VOLKARE'S Quest, all of the map tiles are already laid out. Nothing, however, overrides the rules about
tile exploration by the players. For players to reveal a facedown tile, that tile must be adjacent to the requisite
number of already-revealed tiles. VOLKARE, however, isn't limited by that.338
S19:—Being Knocked Out—In VOLKARE'S Return, if a knocked-out player has nothing but wounds in
her hand when VOLKARE attacks again, she won't be able to fully attend the combat—but it's legal to keep as-
signing her wounds. Although his might make it easier to win, it will dramatically decrease her score.339
Q20:—VOLKARE'S CAMP in Place of a CITY—Do you add an extra die to the SOURCE and an extra Unit to
the Unit Offer when playing with the "VOLKARE'S CAMP in Place of a CITY" variant? If so, would this add two
dice and two Units if playing a Blitz scenario?
A20: No. No. Rules for adding extra dice and Units appear only in specific scenarios, not in the rules for
the "VOLKARE'S CAMP in Place of a CITY" variant. There's an official FAQ entry that suggests otherwise:
How many dice & Units should there be in a solo game?
The dummy player does not count as a player for these purposes, so it would normally be 3, but you add
an extra one if playing Blitz scenario or against Volkare.
But this FAQ entry is likely meant to cover standard scenarios, not optional variants. The "VOLKARE'S
Return - Blitz" scenario should be the only time you add two dice and two Units.340
S21:—An Empty CAMP—In VOLKARE'S Return and VOLKARE'S Quest, once VOLKARE leaves his CAMP,
you cannot interact with that space: treat it as an empty space costing 2 Movement points to enter.341
And in both scenarios, the CAMP tile begins the game face up.342
S22:—VOLKARE Draws a Red Card—In VOLKARE'S Return and VOLKARE'S Quest, treat VOLKARE'S draw-
ing of a red card as being synchronous with step 1 in the SoP under PvP Combat Q1.343
S23:—Team Rules—In cooperative scenarios against VOLKARE, I suggest using Team Rules even
though it's not mentioned in the scenario rules, as that was probably just an oversight.344
S24:—Skill Use when Partially Attending—Lost Legion p. 7 says when a player Partially Attends an at-
tack by VOLKARE, handle it the same as when partially attending a PvP Combat. But it also says the player is on-
ly restricted from using Skills that're usable once a turn; the base game rulebook, conversely, limits you to only
those Skills usable during other players' turns. It's likely that the Lost Legion wording here is simply loose.345
Q25:—VOLKARE'S Return—In the Third Phase (the Battle for the CITY), if you're attacked by VOLKARE
while you're in the CITY and defeat at least one enemy token in his army per real player in the game (but not all
of the enemy tokens), what happens?
A25: At the end of Combat, VOLKARE and his army retreat to a space two spaces away from the CITY
(with one space between VOLKARE and the CITY). You then take your next turn, which if you fully attended the
Combat consists of turning your round order token face up. Then VOLKARE takes a normal turn which, unless he
draws a Spell card or is in a Frenzy, will bring him adjacent to the CITY again. You then take a regular turn.
(However if VOLKARE did draw a Spell card or was in a Frenzy and has attacked you again, and if you fully at-
tended that resulting Combat, then you would instead flip up your round order token again).346
S26:— VOLKARE'S Return vs. Megapolis—With a MEGAPOLIS in place of the CITY, VOLKARE can assault it
from any space adjacent to either of its spaces, and he faces all Mage Knights then inside the MEGAPOLIS, re-
gardless of which space of the MEGAPOLIS they're on.347


[PAGE 043]
VOLKARE p. 43
Q27:—Mage Knights Attacked in a CITY during VOLKARE'S Quest—What happens if two Mage Knights
are in a conquered CITY and VOLKARE moves to attack them?
A27: This happens either by VOLKARE moving into or through the conquered CITY (after drawing a blue,
green, or white card), or by him staying put but fighting a Mage Knight close to him (after drawing a red
card).348
In either case, the Mage Knights can retreat (taking wounds) or stay and fight.349
If either or both Mage
Knights stay and fight, and VOLKARE ends his turn on the conquered CITY, then the player(s) have to withdraw
(even if VOLKARE lost twice as many army tokens as there are Mage Knights in the game and has to flip his
round order token facedown).
If one or both Mage Knights decide to stay and fight, follow the instructions on pp. 7-8 of Lost Legion.
The Mage Knights decide whether to fully or partially attend the combat. If both Mage Knights fight, they can
do a cooperative defense, dividing VOLKARE'S army between them as discussed on p. 8 of Lost Legion.350
S28:—Fearful Units—Lost Legion p. 18 refers to "fearful" Units: elsewhere these Units are called "in-
decisive" Units.351
S29:—VOLKARE Attacks from a Fortified Space—In VOLKARE'S Return, if VOLKARE draws a red card and
attacks from a fortified space, he is not treated as being fortified. Although his piece doesn't physically enter
your space, he is nevertheless sallying out of his space to engage you (and then returning to it after the battle).352
S30:—Player on PORTAL in VOLKARE'S Quest—If a player is on the PORTAL, and VOLKARE is adjacent to it
and draws a non-red Spell card, what happens after VOLKARE moves onto the PORTAL and attacks the player?353
• If the player eliminates VOLKARE'S army, the player wins.
• If the player kills too few of VOLKARE'S army to slow him down, the player withdraws from the PORTAL, and
VOLKARE moves through the PORTAL for the second portion of his spell card and wins.
• If the player kills enough of VOLKARE'S army to slow him down, the player withdraws from the PORTAL and
VOLKARE remains there and turns his Round Order token face down. For the second portion of his spell card,
he turns his Round Order token face up and his turn ends. The player(s) then have one last turn to kill him.
S31:—VOLKARE Attacks Players in a CITY—If players choose to do a Cooperative Defense against an at-
tack by VOLKARE (Lost Legion p. 8), they can't play Healing Effects before Combat per the discussion in PvP
Q1, but they can play Special Effects before and during Combat—see PvP S2. And if fully attending, players
can also play Healing and Special Effects after Combat.354
In VOLKARE'S Return, if VOLKARE attacks the CITY but the players in it don't want to defend it, they stand
aside, let VOLKARE destroy the CITY walls, don't take Wounds, and remain in the CITY afterwards (Lost Legion,
p. 15). If some players wish to defend but not all of them, or if they can't agree on who does what, then follow
the instructions on p. 8 under the "Agreement" paragraph. In all cases, defending players individually choose
whether to partially or fully attend any resulting Combat (p. 8).355
S32:—VOLKARE is not a Dummy Player—When playing VOLKARE'S Quest or Return, VOLKARE has a
fixed deck: you don't add cards to his deck at the end of a Round. Cards are added to the Dummy player (base
game rulebook p. 12), but in these scenarios VOLKARE "replaces" the Dummy player, so you treat the Offers per
the base game rulebook p. 4, simply discarding the lowest one [or two--Lost Legion p. 12] Offer cards.356
S33:—Scoring—In VOLKARE'S Quest/Return, the Race level multiplies only the sum (30/40/50 +
2/card).357
