Cross References: Movement S3; Combat S10; PvP S7; CITIES S11; Scenarios Q8; Counterattack O2;
Flame Wave O5; Time Bending S8; Veil of Mist O3; Banner of Fear Q8; HEROES S1; Sparing Power S1.


[PAGE 044]
p. 44 Basic Action Cards
Clarifications by Component
Basic Action Cards
# S1:—Identification—All Basic Action Cards have a Mage Knight emblem in the upper
right corner. Advanced Action Cards look like Basic Action Cards, but lack the Mage Knight
emblem. Artifacts resemble Action Cards, but the strong effect has a brown background and is
separated from the basic effect by a line. Spells have a purple border and no image.358
S2:—Timing of Effects—Effects (aside from Move, Influence, Healing, Attack, or Block points) work
immediately when played, whether the effect comes from a card, Skill, or Unit. Effects cannot be delayed and
no new effect can be played before the previous effect is fully processed. See Miscellaneous S9.359
Even effects that provide Attack and Block points usually have to be used at that time (see Combat S49).
Exceptions can apply if the wording of an effect specifies that the effect will be available in future phases, as
with Ambush or Dodge and Weave. See Dual-Colored Action Cards O4 and Sacrifice S4.
S3:—Duration of Effects—Effects remain active until the end of your turn (unless stated otherwise).360
[For players only Partially Attending a PvP combat, presumably effects last until the end of their first full turn.]
S4:—Multi-Card Plays—When a card allows you to play another card (or Skill or Unit ability) as part
of its effect (such as Concentration), both cards are evaluated as if playing a single effect.361
See Combat Q7
and Resistance Break S2.
O5:—Paying Costs—If the only card in your hand is Concentration, you can't use your free mana die to
power its strong effect because you don't have another card to pay the additional "cost" of the effect, since it
says ". . . play another Action card with it".362
Similarly, you can't play the basic effect of Magic Talent if you don't have a card to discard. You also
can't use Magic Talent to play a Combat Spell (with crossed swords) if you're not in Combat. Nor, vice versa,
use Magic Talent to play a Healing Spell (with a hand icon) if you are in Combat. See Miscellaneous S1.363
Many effects require an additional cost, and all effects provide a benefit. But the cost has to be paid be-
fore the card, Skill, or Unit ability is put into effect.364
When it comes to the benefit, things get murky. The card Training says, "Throw away an Action card
. . . then take a card . . . from the Advanced Action Offer". Throwing away an Action card is clearly mandatory,
but what about "taking a card from the offer"? Some players see benefits as voluntary and allow you to throw
away a card that doesn't match any of the colors in the Advanced Action Offer, or to simply skip taking a card.
It gets more complicated with Maximal Effect. Its basic effect says "Use the basic effect of that card
three times." Throwing away "that card" is clearly part of the cost, but is "use the basic effect of that card three
times" voluntary? Must a player use the effect of "that card" even once?365
(To avoid gamey tactics, I suggest
that at the very least players should have to execute the secondary card at least once.)
Another problem with the idea that "benefits are voluntary" is that the rules don't seem to support it.
A cleaner approach, IMO, is to say that any text written in the imperative tense (e.g., a command) has to
be followed, even benefits. This doesn't apply, of course, to text with "may" or "or". And there are cases where
the rules themselves permit laxness (the rules say Influence/Movement/Block/Attack/Heal points need not be
used effectively or even used at all; and when we're told to draw cards, we draw only as many as we can—even
if it's zero). This stance IMO is more consistent with the rules, is straightforward, and guards against gamey tac-
tics. In this Compendium, my stance is that beneficial effects are mandatory; with Maximal Effect, the second
card's effect must be used as many times as specified. For further discussion, see Maximal Effect S1 and S9.366
S6:—Basic Effect vs. Strong Effect—The basic effect (top portion) of Basic Action and Advanced Ac-
tion cards does not require any mana; the strong effect on the bottom portion does require mana.367
With Deed
cards, you use either the basic effect or the strong effect—never both. See Movement S13.368


[PAGE 045]
Basic Action Cards p. 45
Cold Toughness (TOVAK)
# S1:—Counting Enemy Abilities—The strong effect of Cold Toughness gives Ice Block 5, plus an addi-
tional +1 for each ability and each color-of-attack the enemy has. Here's an exhaustive list of these abilities:369
• Offensive: Swift, Cumbersome, Brutal, Poison, Paralyze, Assassination, Vampiric.
• Defensive: Elusive, Fortified, Unfortified, Defend, Arcane Immunity.†
• Resistances: Fire, Ice, Physical.
• Color of Attack: Ice, Fire. (Cold Fire Attacks have both colors and therefore count as two.)
† - Arcane Immunity is crossed out because Cold Toughness itself specifies that Cold Toughness
gives only the base Ice Block 5 against enemies that have Arcane Immunity.
Examples:
(1) HIGH DRAGON (Cold Fire Attack, Brutal, Fire and Ice Resistance): 5 + 5 = 10 Ice Block.
(2) DELPHANA MASTERS (Cold Fire Attack, Paralyze, Assassinate, Fire and Ice Resistance): 5 + 6 = 11
Ice Block.
(3) DRAGON SUMMONER (Summons Attack x2, Physical Resistance, Arcane Immunity): per the enemy
token you are blocking—see O4 below.
(4) ELEMENTAL PRIESTESSES (Fire Attack, Ice Attack, Fire and Ice Resistance): 5 + 4 = 9 Ice Block.
Q2:—Site-Conferred Abilities—Does an enemy in a fortified location or in the green CITY count the ad-
ditional abilities granted by those sites when executing Cold Toughness?
A2: No. Cold Toughness gives +1 for only those abilities that are physically printed on the token.370
S3:—Ice Resistance—Non-Attack/non-Block effects of blue cards don't apply to enemies with Ice Re-
sistance, but that doesn't stop Cold Toughness from causing additional Ice Block points against them.371
O4:—SUMMONERS—When Cold Toughness blocks a Summoned MONSTER, do you count up the abilities
of the SUMMONER or of the Summoned MONSTER? What if the SUMMONER has Arcane Immunity?
A4: Go by the icons printed on the Summoned MONSTER token because Cold Toughness refers to each
ability and color "depicted on the blocked enemy token you blocked".
And as for the second question, Arcane Immunity prevents the strong effect of Cold Toughness from
giving an increased Ice Block (only the base Ice Block 5 applies)—and this reduction to Ice Block 5 only ap-
plies if the Summoned MONSTER itself has Arcane Immunity. See S1 above.372
Cross References: Shapeshift S1, S3.
Concentration
# Q1:—Improvisation—When Concentration is used with Improvisation to gain the strong effect of Im-
provisation "for free", do you still discard another card? (This answer will also apply to Will Focus.)
A1: Yes. When deed cards give players the strong effect of another card "for free", they're referring only
to the standard mana cost (Mana Overload S2). Any "costs" in the text effect itself must still be paid: see Basic
Action Cards O5.373
S2:—Attacks—The strong effect of Concentration can increase the strength of all kinds of Attacks, in-
cluding physical Ranged Attacks, Ranged Fire Attacks, Siege Ice Attacks, Cold Fire Attacks, etc.374
S3:—Blocks—The strong effect of Concentration can increase the strength of all kinds of Blocks, in-
cluding physical Blocks, Fire Blocks, Ice Blocks, and Cold Fire Blocks.375
S4:—Strong Effects—When you play the strong effect of Concentration, and then another Action card,
say Swiftness, you cannot couple the +2 bonus of Concentration with the basic effect (Move 2) of Swiftness:
you must always use the strong effect of the latter card, in this case "Ranged Attack 3".376


[PAGE 046]
p. 46 Basic Action Cards
S5:—Into the Heat—If you play the strong effect of Concentration with Into the Heat, the bonus from
Concentration will not apply since Heat isn't directly providing Move, Influence, Block, or Attack points. See
Maximal Effect S13.377
S6:—Action Cards Only—The strong effect of Concentration can only be played with other Action
cards (Basic or Advanced), not with Spells or Artifacts. It works fine with cards like Pure Magic.378
Cross References: Combat S5, Q7; Basic Action Cards S4, O5; Improvisation Q1; Tirelessness S3; Will
Focus S1, S4; Ambush S5; Blood of Ancients S5; Counterattack S1; Dodge & Weave S2; Maximal Effect Q3,
S9, S10, S11, S12, S13; Shield Bash S5; Deadly Aim Q2; Mana Overload S6; Shapeshift S2, S3; Fire Gem S3.
Crystal Joy (GOLDYX)
# S1:—Reclaiming Crystal Joy—Crystal Joy lets you discard another card to return Joy to your hand. This
must be done at the end of the same turn in which you played Joy. See End of Turn S2.
If Joy is thrown away, say with Maximal Effect, you cannot return Joy to your hand.
Taking Joy back into your hand should happen before step 3 of the End of Turn sequence.379
You can reclaim Joy after you've been Knocked Out, if you can make a card discard somehow.380
Crystallize
# S1:—No Gold or Black Crystals—You can't gain gold or black crystals because they don't exist.381
Cross References: Mana S5, S12; PvP Q1; Resting S3; Maximal Effect S1, S31.
Determination
# S1:—Blocks Cannot Be Split—Block points from a single effect can be played only while Blocking a
specific enemy attack, and all points must go toward Blocking that one Attack. Excess Block points are lost.382
Cross References: Diplomacy S1; Maximal Effect S5, S11; Circlet of Proficiency S14.
Improvisation
# Q1:—Concentration—When using Concentration to play the strong effect of Improvisation "for free",
do you still need to discard another card?
A1: Yes. When deed cards give players the strong effect of another card "for free", they're referring only
to the standard mana cost. Any "costs" in the text effect itself must still be paid: see Basic Action Cards O5.383
S2:—Usage—With Improvisation—as with most cards—you choose only one effect: Move 3 or Influ-
ence 3 or Attack 3 or Block 3. Cards that let you to use the full list will explicitly state so. And with Improvisa-
tion, you not only discard an extra non-Wound card, but Improvisation itself is of course also discarded.384
S3:—Discard from Hand—The non-wound card discarded to enable Improvisation cannot have been
used or played or discarded to achieve some other purpose: it must come from your hand and be unused.385
Cross References: Miscellaneous S13; Concentration Q1; Maximal Effect S7, S8, S9; Time Bending S7.
Mana Draw
# S1:—Timing—With Mana Draw, just as with other cards or Skills or Units that permit you to take mana
dice from the SOURCE, you may take the dice either before or after you take your once-a-turn free mana die.386
S2:—Use Required for the Basic Effect—You can't use the basic effect of Mana Draw simply to re-roll
a mana die at the end of your turn: you have to use the mana die in some way. See Miscellaneous S1.387


[PAGE 047]
Basic Action Cards p. 47
S3:—Use Not Required with Strong Effect—You need not use the mana gotten with the strong effect.388
S4:—Mana Tokens Only—With the strong effect, you're allowed to use both mana tokens but you can-
not use the mana die itself.389
S5:—End of the Round—The die that's set with the strong effect does not persist across a Round bound-
ary.390
S6:—Black Mana—With either effect you can choose black mana, but you're allowed to use that black
mana only under the usual constraints: see Mana S3 and Q7.391
S7:—Depleted Dice—With the strong effect, you can choose to take a "depleted" mana die.392
S8:—Returning the Die to the SOURCE—With the strong effect, you return the changed die to the SOURCE
during your End-of-Turn sequence, not immediately after setting it to a new face. One consequence of this is
that you know it'll have that color during the next turn—unless you're at the end of a Round (see S5 above).393
Cross References: Mana Q1, S12; PvP Q1; SOURCE Freeze S1; Who Needs Magic Q1.
Mana Pull (ARYTHEA)
# S1:—Wild Black Mana—With the basic effect you can take a black die as your additional die—even
during the day—but only this black die is wild. See Mana Draw S1.394
O2:—Taking Only One Die—With the strong effect, must the player take exactly two dice? If so, then
the strong effect of Mana Pull can't be played when there's only one mana die remaining in the SOURCE. [It's fair
to permit ARYTHEA to play the strong effect of Mana Pull even when there's only one mana die remaining in the
SOURCE. This case arises most often when playing solo, when fewer dice are in the SOURCE: in my opinion, ARY-
THEA shouldn't lose the punch of one of her two specialized cards when playing solo.]395
Cross References: Mana Q2; Mana Draw S1-S8; Polarization S5; Who Needs Magic Q1.
March
# Cross References: Movement S13; Agility S1; Maximal Effect S10, S11, S18; Mountain Lore O3.
Noble Manners (NOROWAS)
# S1:—Spending Points Isn't Required—You can gain the Fame (and Reputation) bonus even if you don't
spend the Influence points, just so long as you play Noble Manners when Interacting with the Locals as your
Action while you're on a space where Locals exist: a VILLAGE, MAGE TOWER, etc.396
S2:—Permissible Sites—You cannot choose to Interact if there're no Locals in your space.397
S3:—At the end of the Turn—The Ultimate Edition removed the phrase "at the end of your turn" from
both effects of this card because Lost Legion made it clear that changes to Reputation and Fame are now applied
immediately (Combat Q16). Yet Noble Manners still works the same as before because if you play it after
choosing Interaction as your Action, your Reputation modifier will have already been applied.398
Cross References: Influence Q6; Threaten S1.

Rejuvenate (NOROWAS)
# Q1:—Healing Card—Are all Rejuvenate effects "Healing effects" and unusable in combat?
A1: Yes. Healing effects aren't limited to healing wounds. Readying Units, for instance, is often treated
as a Healing effect. And, with Rejuvenate, even gaining a crystal is considered a Healing effect because the card
doesn't also have a Special Effects icon in the upper left corner, only a hand (healing) icon.401
S2:—Select One Only—You can select only one option from the list.402
Cross References: Combat S3; Maximal Effect S26.
Ruthless Coercion (KRANG)
# S1:—Readying Effect—With the strong effect, you ready Units the moment you play Ruthless Coercion.
You cannot ready a Unit with Coercion, gain more Influence by expending that Unit, then again ready the same
Unit, ad nauseam. But you can play other Influence points before playing Coercion, and use those Influence
points with it. [During playtesting, this card was played as is stated here. This is consistent with how Regenera-
tion (S2) and Rebirth (S2) work.]403
S2:—Discount Optional—With the basic effect of Ruthless Coercion, the discount to recruit a Unit is
optional, so if you recruit a Unit at full price you will avoid the Reputation reduction.404
O3:—Use during Combat—This card has a hand symbol: can it be used during Combat?
A3: I allow its non-Readying effects to be used during Combat (say, to influence THUGS to take damage
or to influence HEROES to participate in an Assault). Ruthless Coercion is an upgraded version of Threaten, a
card that can be played during Combat, so in my opinion Coercion should be treated similarly.405
Cross References: Maximal Effect S25; Dual-Colored Action Cards O4; Regeneration S1.
Savage Harvesting (KRANG)
# S1:—Discard Precedes Movement—You decide which card to discard as you make your move into the
new space, and you don't reveal garrisons until after you have entered that space and discarded your card. See
Scenarios S16 and Q17.406
O2:—Move a Space—Movement in MKBG is done either through paying terrain Movement costs or
through effects that directly move you one or more spaces (such as Temporal Portal). With these latter "direct"
modes of movement, I don't count the intervening spaces, only the terminal space. See Movement O9.407
Cross References: Scenarios Q17; Maximal Effect S22, S23.

Tirelessness (WOLFHAWK)
# S1:—No Retroactive Bonus—The extra "1 Move" from the strong effect of Tirelessness does not apply
retroactively to previously played cards, only to cards played after Tirelessness is played.409
S2:—Agility—See Agility S3.
S3:—Concentration—The strong effect of Concentration on Tirelessness gives you Move 4 from Tire-
lessness and Move +2 from Concentration. Concentration itself doesn't trigger the +1 Move bonus because, in
this case, only Tirelessness is directly providing Movement points.410
S4:—Units—Units that provide Move points don't benefit from the +1 Move granted by Tirelessness.411
S5:—Duration of Bonus—If played during the Movement Phase, unused Move points don't transfer to
the Action Phase, but the bonus for future Move cards does.412

Will Focus (GOLDYX)
# S1:—Ambush—The strong effect of Will Focus with Ambush gives 7 (4+3) Move points. The Attack
and Block parts of it are not boosted because, technically, Ambush isn't itself giving Attack or Block points.414
A similar logic follows with Agility: only the Move 4 is boosted.
S2:—Magic Talent—If you use the strong effect of Will Focus to play Magic Talent, you still must pay
a mana of the corresponding color to take the Spell.415
S3:—Types of Attack—Will Focus does increase Ice Blocks, Siege Fire Attacks, etc.416
S4:—Concentration—Will Focus works like Concentration, so all Q&A apply to both.417

p. 50 Advanced Action Cards
Advanced Action Cards
Agility
# S1:—Using Move Points in Combat—The strong effect of Agility (updated in Lost Legion), lets you
convert Move points into Ranged Attacks (at a 2-for-1 rate) or into standard Attacks (at a 1-for-1 rate). If in ad-
dition to powered Agility you play powered March for an extra Move 4, you could convert, say, 6 points into 3
Ranged Attack points, leaving you 2 Move points to spend later during Combat, perhaps to reduce a Cumber-
some attack by 2, or to gain Attack 2 in the Attack Phase. See Miscellaneous S9.418
S2:—Entering the Action Phase—Movement points don't transfer to the Combat Phase (Movement S1),
but the Agility ability to convert Move points into Attack points does. So after playing Agility in the Movement
Phase, you could play additional Movement cards (or sideways cards) in the Combat Phase and convert those
Move points into Attacks.419
S3:—MAZE and Tirelessness—If you play the strong effect of Tirelessness as you enter a MAZE, you gain
Move 4 and a +1 bonus to all other Move cards. If you then play the basic effect of Agility, you'll have a total of
7 Move points. You could spend 6 of these to take the longest path into the MAZE. Then later in Combat you
could play a card sideways for Move 2 (Tirelessness still applies) and so have three Move points to convert into
Attack points. See Movement S1.420
Cross References: Movement S1; PvP S7; Will Focus S1; Ambush S6; Maximal Effect S8; Deadly Aim
Q2; Fire Gem Q2; Ghostly Elixir Q2.
Ambush
# S1:—Deed Cards Only—Ambush does not benefit Units or Skills.421
S2:—Sideways Cards—Ambush does apply to cards played sideways for Attack or Block points.422
S3:—The First Card—If the first Attacks or Blocks you make during your turn come from Units or
Skills, you'll still gain the benefits from Ambush when you do play an Attack card or a Block card.423
S4:—Timing—You can play Ambush even if you aren't going to be using its Move points. If you play it
during Combat, it need not be played before your first Attack or Block: you can delay playing it until immedi-
ately before any Attack or Block you wish to affect. But once played, Ambush affects the very next Attack or
Block (whichever is first) that comes from a (non-Unit) card.424
S5:—Will Focus/Concentration—See Will Focus S1. Ambush works similarly with Concentration.425
S6:—Diplomacy/Agility—Diplomacy is not a Block card, so Ambush doesn't apply to it. So if Diploma-
cy Block points are the first Attack or Block played, then Ambush could apply to an Attack card played in the
Attack Phase. The same reasoning holds for Agility when used with Ambush. See also Fire Gem Q2.426
Q7:—Shield of the Fallen Kings—With the two or three Blocks against different attacks from Shield of
the Fallen Kings, do you add the +X Block from Ambush to the first Block or to both Blocks?
A7: Per the designer, Ambush plus Shield gives a Block 4+X (first) and then a Block 4 (second).427
Q8:—"Attack" Cards—Do the Bow of Starsdawn and the Sword of Justice count as "Attack cards" when
played with Ambush? Does Disintegrate?
A8: Yes, but Ambush only adds +1 (or +2 with the strong effect) regardless of how many cards you dis-
card with the Sword or the Bow.428
Disintegrate, however, does not count as an Attack card.429


[PAGE 051]
Advanced Action Cards p. 51
S9:—Dodge and Weave—If after Ambush you play the basic effect of Dodge and Weave, then Dodge
and Weave does count as an Attack card if you do not take any Wounds to your hand before the Attack Phase.
This might require some tricky figuring in deciding which is the first Attack/Block card played if, after Dodge
and Weave, you play a Block card in the Block Phase, because Dodge and Weave is, strictly speaking, a condi-
tional Attack card and you won't know for certain if it is one until the Attack Phase. [To avoid having to retro-
actively adjust the whole Combat Phase, you could say that—when used with Ambush—the card Dodge and
Weave will not count as being played until the Attack Phase begins (even if it's physically played earlier).]430
O10:—When Playing a Card in an Offer—If, after Ambush, you use Blood of Ancients or Tome of All
Spells or Magic Talent to "play" a card in the Advanced Action Offer/Spells Offer, the card you play from the
Offer can trigger the Ambush bonus. See Tome of All Spells S2.431
Cross References: Combat S5; PvP S7; Miscellaneous S12; Concentration S2, S3; Will Focus S1; Blood
of Ancients S3; Maximal Effect S11, S15, S16; Shield Bash S5; Shield of the Fallen Kings S1; Sword of Justice
S7; Tome of All Spells S2.
Blood of Ancients
# S1:—Taking a Wound—With the basic effect, the Wound goes into your hand, not onto a Unit.432
S2:—Fire Resistance—With the strong effect, you pay a red mana to use an Action card in the Ad-
vanced Action Offer, but the color of this red mana is immaterial for effects that target enemy tokens who have
Fire Resistance: what matters is the color of the card in the Offer. See Combat Q7 and Maximal Effect Q3.433
S3:—Ambush—See Tome of All Spells S2 and Ambush O10.
S4:—Steady Tempo—If you play Steady Tempo via Blood of Ancients, neither Steady Tempo nor Blood
of Ancients goes on top of your Deed deck (Blood of Ancients says Steady Tempo remains where it is).434
S5:—Concentration—If you use the strong effect of Concentration to play Blood of Ancients, any Move
/Influence/Block/Attack points provided by a card in the Advanced Action Offer do not benefit from Concen-
tration's "+2" effect because Blood of Ancients is not providing the points directly.435
S6:—Pure Magic—If you use the strong effect of Blood of Ancients to play Pure Magic from the Ad-
vanced Action Offer, you still must pay the additional mana cost of the specific effect that you desire.436
S7:—During Combat—You cannot use Blood of Ancients during Combat to play a Healing effect, such
as Regeneration.437

Blood Rage
# S1:—Assigning Wounds—The Wound goes into your hand, not onto a Unit.
S2:—Timing of Wounds—If you take a Wound from Blood Rage, you can immediately use that Wound
with Power of Pain or Invocation, and you can even add any Attack points you gain through those Skills onto

Blood Ritual
# S1:—One Use Only—With the strong effect, you gain at most one crystal [and strict play requires you to
gain it immediately]. Crystallize, of course, could make a crystal out of a second mana, etc.439
S2:—Black Mana during the Day—With the strong effect, you can take a black mana token during the

S3:—Knocked Out—If you use Blood Ritual during Combat, the wound counts toward being knocked
out, but not if Blood Ritual is played before or after Combat.441
Chivalry
# S1:—Faction Leaders—Avatars (faction leaders) count as enemies if defeated (that is, reduced below
level one) by this attack. GENERAL VOLKARE does not count.442
S2:—Grouping Enemy Tokens—All enemy tokens that are grouped together for an Attack that includes
Chivalry will qualify for the Reputation and Fame bonuses.443
S3:—Reputation Adjustments—Reputation adjustments for defeating enemy tokens apply the instant
they're defeated, see Combat Q16. If you use Chivalry to defeat HEROES, the +1 and -1 Reputation adjustments
are applied simultaneously and cancel out. See Enemy Tokens: HEROES S1.444
Counterattack
# S1:—Attack Points Must Be Combined—The Attack 2s/3s gained for blocking enemies must be used
together in one combined Attack. See Maximal Effect Q4. If you use Concentration to play Counterattack, Con-
centration adds a total of +2 to the Attack, not +2 for each enemy blocked.445
O2:—VOLKARE—Does VOLKARE count as an enemy?
A2: Faction leaders are considered enemy tokens (TEZLA, p. 5), and VOLKARE has an attack similar to that
of faction leaders and enemy tokens, so blocking his attack should permit the same kind of benefit.446
S3:—PvP—The "number of enemies blocked" only counts enemy Units that contributed to the last At-
tack (plus possibly one for the enemy hero)—but only count them if you decreased that last Attack to zero.447
S4:—DRAGON SUMMONER—If you block only one of a DRAGON SUMMONER'S summoned MONSTERS, it
doesn't count toward Counterattack. But if you block both MONSTERS, it counts as blocking the (one!) enemy.448
S5:—Multiple Attacks—All of the multiple attacks of a single enemy must be fully Blocked for that ene-
my to count toward "each enemy blocked this turn".449
S6:—Summoned MONSTERS—Blocking a summoned MONSTER does count as having blocked an enemy.
But see S4 above.450
Cross References: Maximal Effect S6; Shield Bash S4; Burning Shield Q1.
Crystal Mastery
# Q1:—All Crystals Count—With the strong effect of Crystal Mastery, which crystals do you get back?
A1: Crystal Mastery returns all of the crystals you used this turn, including those used before and after
you played this card, and any you paid at RUINS. If a blue crystal powered the card, you get that back too.451

Dodge and Weave

S2:—Concentration—If played through the strong effect of Concentration, the Attack 2 is eligible for

S3:—Swiftness/Brutal—This card reduces "one enemy attack by 2" (not "reduce the damage by 2"). If

Learning
# S1:—Timing—You can play Learning at any point during your turn, since it's a Special Effects card.472
If you aren't Interacting with the Locals, Reputation doesn't apply nor do shield tokens on conquered
CITIES. So if your Reputation is negative, consider playing Learning outside of Interaction (even during the

Magic Talent

S3:—Artifacts—Artifacts cannot be discarded with Magic Talent: they have no color.476

Mana Storm
# S1:—Limited Mana Dice—In Solo Conquest, there're only three dice in the SOURCE. If you power Mana
Storm with a die from the SOURCE, you'll only be able to reroll and use at most 2 dice.479
S2:—PvP—You can use Mana Storm in PvP combat even if you're only Partially Attending.480

Maximal Effect
# S1:—General—Maximal Effect essentially copies the text of another card 2 or 3 times. For instance, if
you throw away Rage with the basic effect of Maximal Effect, you get three Attack 2s. (See Q4 below.)
If you play the basic effect of Maximal Effect with Crystallize, you convert precisely three mana into
crystals: you don't convert one mana into three crystals. If you cannot convert three mana, you cannot play this
combination. [Some players allow you to convert up to three mana into crystals: see Basic Action Cards O5.]
If you play the strong effect of Maximal Effect with Crystallize, you get the text "gain a crystal of any
color" twice, so you gain two crystals and they don't need to be the same color.481
S2:—Sideways Cards—You cannot play a card sideways for use with Maximal Effect.482
Q3:—Fire and Ice Resistance—When you play a follow-on card with a base card like Concentration,
Will Focus, or Maximal Effect, how does this work against enemies with Fire or Ice Resistance?
A3: Only the color of the card played second matters for purposes of Fire and Ice Resistance: the first
card is simply making the second card better. If you play the strong effect of Maximal Effect (red) with Ice
Shield (blue) against an enemy with Ice Resistance, the armor reduction does not apply. See Combat Q7.483


[PAGE 056]
p. 56 Advanced Action Cards
Q4:—Attack Points—Can the Attack points from a Maximal Effect combination be used separately?
A4: No. Maximal Effect repeats the text on the follow-on card two or three times. When used with Rage
you get "Attack 2. Attack 2. Attack 2." or "Attack 4. Attack 4." You then process this effect immediately and
treat it as a single effect, giving you one "Attack 6" or one "Attack 8". See S1 above, Basic Action Cards S2,
and Miscellaneous S9.484
S5:—Determination—The strong effect of Maximal Effect with Determination gives Block 10 that has
to be used against a single attack, not as two separate Block 5's. See Basic Action Cards S2 and Miscellaneous
S9.485
S6:—Counterattack—After having Blocked two enemies, if you play the basic effect of Maximal Effect
with Counterattack, you get an Attack 18 = 3x2 + 3x(2+2). See Q4 and S5 above.486
S7:—Improvisation—Playing Maximal Effect with Improvisation (or Battle Versatility) cannot give both
Block points and Attack points, because you cannot play an Attack effect in the Block Phase, or vice versa.487
S8:—Improvisation—The strong effect of Maximal Effect with Improvisation can provide Block 5 and
Move 5 when blocking an Enemy (don't forget to discard two cards to power both uses of Improvisation). The
Block must be used immediately, but the Move points can be saved for other purposes. Or you could instead
gain both Block and Influence, the Block to counter an immediate Attack and the Influence to, say, convince
HEROES to join in an Assault and even get them to contribute to the same Block. The Influence points may be
used later during your turn, but surplus Block points are wasted.488
S9:—Improvisation/Concentration—When using Maximal Effect with Improvisation, you must also dis-
card three hand cards with the basic effect or two with the strong effect, to pair with each use of Improvisation.
Similarly, with Concentration and Decompose. And you aren't allowed to play Maximal Effect with a card like
Improvisation or Concentration if, at the moment you play it, you don't have enough cards to complete the two-
or three-card cost. So if you play Maximal Effect plus Concentration, but have only Tranquility left in your
hand (intending to use the two-card-draw effect of Tranquility), that would be prohibited if you have undrawn
Wound cards in your draw deck, as drawing Wounds would prevent you from completing the combination.489
[Some players say you need not use the second card to its full extent, as it is a benefit and hence volun-
tary: Basic Action Cards O5.]490
S10:—Concentration and March—If you play the strong effect of Maximal Effect then Concentration
then March for six Move points, you cannot wait until after you expend those Move points (to, say, explore a
new tile) before choosing what other card you'll be playing with Concentration. All card plays have to be re-
solved before you spend those Move points. See Miscellaneous S9 and also S12 below.491
S11:—Concentration and Tirelessness/Ambush—If you play the strong effect of Maximal Effect, then
Concentration, then Tirelessness, then March, the bonus of Tirelessness will apply to March. Similarly, if dur-
ing the Block Phase you play the strong effect of Maximal Effect, then Concentration, then Ambush, then Deter-
mination, the Block bonus from Ambush will apply to Determination—see Ambush S4.492
S12:—Concentration and Tranquility—If you play Maximal Effect, then Concentration, then Tranquili-
ty, you resolve the effects one at time and therefore do get to see which cards you draw with Tranquility before
having to commit which card(s) you'll play with the second (and third) application of Maximal Effect.493


[PAGE 057]
Advanced Action Cards p. 57
S13:—Concentration then Maximal Effect—If you play the strong effect of Concentration then Maximal
Effect then Rage, you get two Attack 4's that have to be used in the same Attack. Maximal Effect itself doesn't
directly provide any "points" that Concentration can increase by 2, so the only benefit of playing Concentration
before Maximal Effect is to allow a green mana to power Maximal Effect instead of a red mana.494
Remember
that Maximal Effect doesn't provide Attack points directly, a key insight that helps with other similarly worded
effects like Deadly Aim or Mana Overload, and combinations like Will Focus plus Ambush.495,496
If you play the cards in the opposite order—the strong effect of Maximal Effect then Concentration then
both Swiftness cards—this gives you two Ranged Attack 5's for a single combined Ranged Attack 10. And in
this case the Concentration card is thrown away, but not the Swiftness cards.497
S14:—Will Focus—Will Focus plays similar to Concentration: see S13 above.498
S15:—Ambush—If you play the strong effect of Maximal Effect then Ambush, you get the strong effect
of Ambush twice: Move 8, and then either +4 to your first Attack card or +8 to your first Block card. (You do
not get +2 to your first Attack card and +4 to your first Block card.)499
S16:—Ambush and Rage—If you play Ambush then Maximal Effect then Rage, all using their basic ef-
fects, you will get Move 2 and either Attack 1 (from Ambush) plus 3 (from Maximal Effect) x Attack 2 (from
Rage) = Attack 7, or Block 2 (from Ambush) plus 3 (from Maximal Effect) x Block 2 (from Rage) = Block 8.
The Attack and Block bonus from Ambush itself is not doubled or tripled.500
S17:—Tirelessness—If you play the strong effect of Maximal Effect then Tirelessness, you get Move 8
(4+4) and +2 Move points for every additional movement card you play later this turn. A player might wonder
if the second instance of Tirelessness should benefit from the first playing of Tirelessness and result in Move 9,
but remember that you're playing the card Tirelessness only once but are using its effect twice.501
If you play the basic effect of Maximal Effect then Tirelessness, you get Move 6 (2+2+2) and +3 Move
points for the next movement card you play during this turn.502
S18:—Tirelessness—If you play the strong effect of Tirelessness then the basic effect of Maximal Effect
then March, you'll gain Move 4 from Tirelessness and then 3 x Move 2 from March, plus 1 additional for a total
of 11. You're playing March once, but using its effect three times.503
S19:—Magic Talent—If you play the basic effect of Maximal Effect then Magic Talent, you must dis-
card three additional cards regardless of whether or not you cast any Spells from the Spell Offer: see S9 above.
You must also pay a mana of the matching color for each Spell you choose to cast. If you throw away the right
color cards, you could cast the same Spell more than once.504
When playing the strong effect of Maximal Effect then Magic Talent, you must pay two mana, one for
each use of Magic Talent. These mana must match the colors of Spells in the Spell Offer: see Magic Talent O4.
[Some players say you need only discard a card (basic effect)/pay a mana (strong effect) for each use
you make of the Magic Talent card: see S9 above.]
S20:—Crystal Mastery—If you play the strong effect of Maximal Effect, then Crystal Mastery, you
don't get double the crystals: each crystal gets returned to your inventory twice, but no matter how many times a
crystal is returned to you, it's still just the one crystal.505
S21:—Spell Forge—If you play the basic effect of Maximal Effect then Spell Forge, you gain three
crystals, and you can choose the same Spell card in the Spell Offer all three times. When playing this combina-
tion using the strong effect, you may choose the same two Spell cards both times.506

S22:—Savage Harvesting—If you play the basic effect of Maximal Effect then Savage Harvesting, you

S23:—Savage Harvesting—If you play the strong effect of Maximal Effect then Savage Harvesting, you

S26:—Rejuvenate—If you play the basic effect of Maximal Effect then Rejuvenate, you're allowed to

S27:—Into the Heat—If you play either effect of Maximal Effect then Into the Heat, all of your Units

S28:—Force of Nature—If you play the basic effect of Maximal Effect then Force of Nature, you can

S29:—Steady Tempo—If you play the basic effect of Maximal Effect then Steady Tempo, you won't be

S30:—Temporal Portal—If you play Maximal Effect (basic or strong effect) and then Temporal Portal,
you'll be able to use the effect of Temporal Portal only once, as doing so counts as your Action for the turn.515
S31:—Diplomacy—If you play the strong effect of Maximal Effect with Diplomacy, you can choose
both fire and ice, using some Influence points for Ice Blocks and other Influence points for Fire Blocks (but you
cannot get Cold-Fire Blocks). This is similar to how Crystallize would work when used with the strong effect of
Maximal Effect: you would be able to choose two (possibly different) color crystals.516
S32:—Training—When playing the basic effect of Maximal Effect and throwing away Training, you
then—one-at-a-time—throw away three other hand cards and take three cards from the Advanced Action Offer.
The Advanced Action Offer is refreshed after each card is chosen so you'll get to see an additional card before
choosing which card to throw away next. But to play this combination in the first place you need to have at least
five cards in your hand: Maximal Effect, Training, and three other cards. And these three other cards must also
match the colors of the cards in the Advanced Action Offer at the moment that you play Maximal Effect. See
Training S3.517
[Some players let you choose how many additional cards to throw away, between zero and
three. And they don't insist that these discards have to match the colors of the cards in the Advanced Action Of-
fer, and they also permit you to choose not to draw any cards from the Offer.]

S33:—Blood Rage—The optional cost of Blood Rage—taking a Wound—is part of the effect, so you

S34:—Stout Resolve—If you play Maximal Effect then Stout Resolve, the discard ability of Resolve can

three wounds, each of which would give you a +1. So if you chose Move 4 and Influence 2, the three discarded

Mountain Lore
# S1:—Card Draws—The card-draw effect of Mountain Lore occurs when you draw up to your hand lim-
it, not when you draw any card(s)—and the effect will apply across a Round boundary.520
S2:—Forced Withdrawal—You may end your turn on a mountain space after using the strong effect of

face Forced Withdrawal during your End-of-Turn sequence. An exception applies if you declare the End of the
Round during that ensuing turn, then you won't be subject to Forced Withdrawal during that turn.521
O3:—Safe Spaces—Are mountain spaces safe for you during your turn?
A3: Yes. So you can move onto mountain spaces by other movement effects later during this turn, in-
cluding by Underground Travel, Temporal Portal, Wings of Wind, etc. This, though, could make Forced With-
drawal on a future turn trickier when the Mage Knight must retrace his steps. See Forced Withdrawal S4.522

Shield Bash
# Q1:—Additional Block Cards—With the strong effect, can you play other Block effects to earn an even
larger reduction in armor?
A1: Yes, though those Block points are not automatically doubled against Swiftness. See O3 below.535


[PAGE 061]
Advanced Action Cards p. 61
S2:—Ice Resistance—Shield Bash Block points do count twice against an enemy that has both Swiftness
and Ice Resistance, but no armor reduction applies.536
O3:—Armor Reduction—Vlaada Chvátil, the designer, posted on BoardGameGeek that "if, while using
[the strong effect of Shield Bash], an enemy with Swiftness is successfully Blocked, the resulting armor reduc-
tion is based on the excess undoubled Block points, not on the excess doubled Block."537
Applying Vlaada's clarification, if the strong effect of Shield Bash (Block 5, counts twice vs. Swiftness)
is used against an enemy token with Attack 3 and Swiftness, there'll be an excess of (5x2 - 3x2 = 4), or "two
doubly-counted Block points". So this excess would reduce the enemy's armor by two.
Applying the Shield Bash effect, though, gets complicated if . . .
(a) a player plays additional Block points against an enemy with Swiftness, and/or
(b) if some of the Block points are inefficient (and hence halved).
So how do you determine the reduction to an enemy's armor?538
I see two sensible options: (Approach A
has a small following, so Approach B is colored as a house rule.)
A. Apply these three rules:
1. If the enemy attack has Swiftness, apply the doubled Block points of Shield Bash first.
2. If there are any doubled Block points from Shield Bash left over, undouble them.539
3. Halve any Block points that are inefficient against this attack, even those Block points
that're in excess of the number needed to fully block this attack.
B. Ask, how many more attack points could've been blocked? That's the armor reduction.
Examples:
• You play Rage (Block 2) and Shield Bash against an enemy token with Attack 3 and Swiftness:
o Approach A: You have 2 + (5x2) Block points against the 3 Attack (doubled for Swift-
ness). You apply the doubled Shield Bash points first, which leaves you with 2 + (2x2)
excess Block points. The excess Shield Bash points are now undoubled, leaving you with
2 + 2 excess Block points. You reduce the enemy token's armor by 4.
o Approach B: There's a total of 2 + 5 Block points played (with the 5 points from Shield
Bash being doubled), which gives you 2 + 5x2 = 12 points. You need 3x2 = 6 points to
fully block this attack. With the remaining 6 Block points, you could've blocked an addi-
tional 3 Swiftness Attack points, so you reduce the enemy's armor by 3.
• You play Shield Bash against an enemy token with 2 Fire Attack (without Swiftness):
o Approach A & B: In this case the Block is inefficient, so you have a surplus of (5÷2) - 2
= ½ Block point, and therefore will not earn any reduction to the enemy's armor.
• You play Shield Bash (Block 5 x 2), Shield of the Fallen Kings (Block 6), and a sideways Deed
card (Block 1) against a STORM DRAGON (4 Ice Attack with Swiftness), and you play the third
ability of your SHOCKTROOPS Unit to "reduce one enemy attack by 3".
o Situation: The SHOCKTROOPS reduce the STORM DRAGON'S attack to "1 Ice Attack with
Swiftness". The total Block points you are playing are (5x2) + 6 + 1 = 17.540
o Approach A: You apply Shield Bash's doubled Block points first, but these are ineffi-
cient against Ice Attacks, so this takes 2 of Shield Bash's 5 base Block points, (halved for
being inefficient and doubled for going against an attack with Swiftness). This leaves you
with (3x2) + 6 + 1 = 13 excess Block points. You now undouble the excess Shield Bash
points: 3 + 6 + 1 = 10 excess points. And since the attack is an Ice Attack, these points
are inefficient, so that leaves you with 5 excess Block points for an armor reduction of 5.
o Approach B: You use 4 of the 17 Block points to handle the "1 Ice Attack with Swift-
ness" (needing 4 Block points due to the Swiftness and inefficiency of the Block points).
This leaves you with 13 Block points which could've blocked an additional "3 Ice Attack
with Swiftness" (plus a useless remainder of 1 Block point). The armor is reduced by 3.
o Trick Question: The armor reduction is actually zero because STORM DRAGONS have Ice
Resistance, so blue-card non-Attack/non-Block effects have no effect.541


[PAGE 062]
p. 62 Advanced Action Cards
S4:—Multiple Attacks—If Shield Bash is used against an enemy with Multiple Attacks, any resulting
armor reduction is based solely on the excess Block points from the single Attack which Shield Bash was used
against. None of the other Multiple Attacks need to be Blocked in order to qualify for the armor reduction.542
S5:—Concentration—When the strong effect of Concentration is used with Shield Bash against an ene-
my with Swiftness, the two additional Block points gained from Concentration will also be doubled. The same
goes for Ambush, and also if Ambush and Concentration are used together on Shield Bash.543
Q6:—SUMMONERS—If the strong effect of Shield Bash is used against a MONSTER summoned by an ORC
SUMMONER, can it reduce the ORC SUMMONER'S armor?
A6: No. No effects that affect Blocked MONSTERS affect the SUMMONER.544
S7:—Maximal Effect—Even if you use Maximal Effect to play the strong effect of Shield Bash, Shield
Bash is still considered to be powered by blue mana and any enemy with Ice Resistance will be immune to the
armor reduction effect. And at most only one enemy can ever be affected, as Blocks must be combined for use
against a single Attack. See Maximal Effect Q3 and S5.
Cross References: Combat S33; Arcane Immunity S5.

Song of Wind
# O1:—Lakes—Does "travel through lakes" require that it be "through", or may you voluntarily stop on a

either, as there're negative consequences for stopping on a lake so it's not likely to be abused. [Some players,
though, think the opposite.] See S3 and S6 below, and Frost Bridge S2.545
S2:—WALLS—You need to pay the additional movement cost of WALLS if you cross them.546
S3:—Provoking Rampaging Enemies—If, while moving onto a lake, your movement provokes Rampag-

during your End-of-Turn sequence. If your move does provoke an attack, you can challenge additional adjacent
Rampaging Enemies to join in the resulting combat. See O1 above.547
S4:—Path Finding—If you play the basic effects of both Song of Wind and Path Finding, no matter
which order you play them in, the movement cost of plains is reduced to one, not to zero, due to the "to a mini-

O5:—Paying Blue Mana—If you want to move onto lakes, must you pay the blue mana when you play

Training
# S1:—Blood of the Ancients—A clever combination: activate the strong effect of Blood of the Ancients,
target Training in the Advanced Action Offer, throw away a green Action card, then use Training to target itself
and pull Training into your hand.561
S2:—Paying the Cost—You cannot throw away the Training card itself to fulfill the requirement to
"throw away an Action card from your hand". Once you play Training, it is no longer in your hand. (The same
reasoning applies to Decompose and similar cards.)
S3:—Matching Color—As I read the card, you don't have an option: the thrown away card must match
the color of a card in the Advanced Action Offer, and you must take that card. [Others argue that while you do
have to discard a card, it doesn't have to match any colors and you don't have to take a card. See Basic Action
Cards O5.]
S4:—Advanced Actions Offer—You cannot take cards put into the Unit Offer by MONASTERIES.562

[PAGE 065]
Dual-Colored Action Cards p. 65
Ultimate Edition Dual-Colored Action Cards
# S1:—Instructions—The Ultimate Edition includes special instructions on a single-sided, single-page
insert for the four new dual-colored Advanced Action Cards: be sure to read that.
S2:—Unofficial Rulings—Very few, if any, official answers have been provided for the questions listed
below. In place of official answers, we can make extrapolations based on how MKBG generally works.
S3:—Dummy Player—If a dual-colored action card in the dummy's deck comes up, treat it as being the
color that has the greater number of crystals. (E.g., pick the color that's more challenging for you.)
[Playing strictly by the text, you could treat the action card as being both colors and turn over a number
of cards equal to the total of both crystal colors, but this seems drastic and likely wasn't intended.]563
O4:—Card Icons—Every card and Skill has at least one gold icon. Before Lost Legion, these icons re-
stricted when effects could be played. With the rule changes in Lost Legion, these restrictions were explained in
greater detail even as some of them were loosened, yet a few questions remained. Before discussing the icons
on the dual-colored Action cards, we need to know that there're differing views about handling these gold icons:
View One: You can essentially ignore all of the icons except . . .
1) The Hand (Healing) Icon, which indicates that the card or Skill can't be played during Combat
(unless the text explicitly allows it to be played or the effect also has a Crossed-Swords Icon).
2) The Crossed-Swords (Combat) Icon, which indicates that this card or Skill can only be played in
Combat (unless there're additional icons in the upper left corner).
The "A" (Action) icon can be ignored because the restrictions relating to it are spelled out in the ef-
fect text itself, and the other icons—Head (Influence), Foot (Move), and Plus-Sign (Special Effects)
icons—can pretty much be played anytime during your turn (Lost Legion, p. 9).
View Two: When an effect has multiple gold icons, associate each sub-part of the effect text with one of
those icons. You're allowed to use a sub-part of an effect only if its associated icon can be played
during the current phase of the turn (e.g., Crossed-Swords effects in Combat, Hand effects outside of
Combat).564
View Three: When you play a card, choose one of the gold icons, and all of the sub-parts of the effect
that can be played under the restrictions of this icon are now playable.565
If this seems overly complicated to you, you're not alone. The good news is that, whatever your view,
it'll affect only a limited number of cards: Ruthless Coercion (Can Influence be used during Combat?), Druidic
Staff (Which sub-parts of the effect are playable during Combat?), and three of the four new Advanced Action
cards that came with the Ultimate Edition—see the corresponding entries for further discussion. [Ben Kyo566
believes these three new cards are missing an icon: Power of Crystals should also have a special effects icon;
Chilling Stare should also have an influence icon; Explosive Bolt should also have a special effects icon. I really
like this solution and have added the icons to my own cards.]567
S5:—Both Colors—When you play these new cards and wonder whether Ice or Fire Resistance in an
enemy token limits the card's effect, know that these cards are considered to be both of the shown colors, even if
you use the strong effect and paid for it with the "other" color mana. See also Combat S33.568
Chilling Stare
# S1:—Effect—This is a blue- and white-mana card that gives Influence and affects an enemy attack.
S2:—Attack Abilities—With the basic combat effect, the chosen physical attack or ice attack or fire at-
tack or cold-fire attack remains as is, but it loses any assassination, cumbersome, swiftness, brutal, poison, para-
lyze, or vampiric characteristics it may have had. This effect of Chilling Stare fully applies against an attack
from an enemy with Arcane Immunity but not from an enemy with Ice Resistance. The other effect—Influence
points—can be played no matter what enemy token you're facing in combat. See Arcane Immunity S1.569


[PAGE 066]
p. 66 Dual-Colored Action Cards
S3:—Strong Combat Effect—The strong combat effect that prevents an enemy from attacking does not
affect enemy tokens with Arcane Immunity or Ice Resistance, regardless of which color mana (blue or white) is
used to power the card.570
S4:—SUMMONERS—The basic combat effect of Chilling Stare must be applied against a summoned MON-
STER, not against the SUMMONER. If a SUMMONER has Ice Resistance or Arcane Immunity, this doesn't affect the
basic combat effect of Chilling Stare when it's used against the summoned MONSTER—only Ice Resistance in
the MONSTER itself would matter.
Conversely, the strong combat effect of Chilling Stare must be applied against the SUMMONER, not
against the summoned MONSTER. The strong combat effect of Chilling Stare cannot prevent a SUMMONER from
summoning a MONSTER if the SUMMONER has Ice Resistance or Arcane Immunity (nor can it prevent the sum-
moned MONSTER from attacking). See Combat S23.
O5:—Use outside of Combat—This card has only a Combat icon in the upper left corner. Can it be
played outside of Combat?
A5: The Influence points ought to be usable outside of Combat. It's probable that this card should be
treated as if it also had an Influence [Head] icon: see Dual-Colored Action Cards O4.571
S6:—A Chosen Enemy—This card affects only one enemy token. See Disintegrate S3.
If used with Maximal Effect, I would allow each usage to affect a different enemy token.572
Cross References: Combat S33, Explosive Bolt O5.
Explosive Bolt
# S1:—Effect—This is a red- and white-mana card that gives a crystal (basic effect) or a Ranged Attack
and armor reductions (strong effect).
Q2:—Armor Reduction—With the strong effect, if you defeat two enemies with the Ranged Attack 3,
must the -1 armor be applied to two different enemies, or can it be stacked as -2 armor against one enemy?
A2: You can do either.573
S3:—Fire Resistance—Fire Resistant enemy tokens defeated by the strong effect do count toward re-
ducing another enemy's armor, but Fire Resistant enemy tokens cannot have their own armor reduced (regard-
less of which mana—red or white—is used to power this card).574
S4:—Multiple Attacks—If you stack the Ranged Attack 3 with other Attack points and kill several ene-
my tokens in a single group, each of the defeated enemy tokens provide a -1 armor effect.575
O5:—Use outside of Combat—This card has only a gold combat icon. Must it be played in Combat?
A5: The basic effect of Explosive Bolt should be playable outside Combat. It's probable that this card
should be treated as if it also had a Special Effects icon: see Dual-Colored Action Cards O4.576
S6:—Timing and Duration—The armor reduction is immediate (so you can apply it to other enemy
units attacked during the same sub-phase of combat) and it endures through the entire Combat Phase (so an ar-
mor reduction earned in the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase can apply to enemy units in the Attack Phase).577
O7:—Maximal Effect—With the strong effect, the Ranged Attacks must be combined, but each defeated
enemy should only reduce armor by -1 (not by -2). With the basic effect, take 3 wounds and gain 6 crystals.
Cross References: Combat S33.


[PAGE 067]
Dual-Colored Action Cards p. 67
Power of Crystals
# S1:—Effect—This is a blue- and green-mana card that gives crystals (basic effect) or Move points or
Heal points or card draws (strong effect).
O2:—Bonus Effects—With the strong effect, if you chose "Move 4" and have one of each crystal color,
must you choose "Move 2"—or could you choose "Heal 1" or "draw 1" instead?
A2: Opinions differ, for the wording is ambiguous. Some believe you can choose any of the options,
even a different option for each complete set of crystals you own; others believe your choice must match the
base choice. This latter seems more in keeping with how other card effects are usually worded and handled.578
When used with Maximal Effect, I would treat each subsequent usage of Power of Crystals separately,
allowing a different base effect and bonus option for each.
If you play that "the crystal bonus effect can be chosen by the player", then that crystal bonus effect
must be chosen by the player at the moment that she plays the card: she can't use the 4 new Movement points
she gets to move around and learn new information, and then decide which type of crystal bonus to receive.579
O3:—Use during Combat—This card has a healing (hand) icon on it, and also a movement (foot) icon.
Can it be played during combat (seeing as it lacks verbiage similar to what Refreshing Walk has)?
A3: An open question. It's fair to allow its use in Combat, but only its strong effect, and only for Move
points. [Other players allow all but the Heal points.] See Dual-Colored Action Cards O4.580
S4:—Gain a Crystal—The basic effect gives you at most one crystal, and you choose it from among the
basic crystal colors you lack. If used with Maximal Effect, you cannot gain two crystals of the same color, yet I
would allow its use with Maximal Effect even if the player already has two or more colors of crystals.
S5:—Gain a Crystal—With the basic effect, if you had a crystal of a certain color earlier this turn but
have since spent it, and currently have no crystals of that color, you can gain a crystal of that color.581
Rush of Adrenaline
# S1:—Effect—This is a red- and green-mana card that gives card draws for taking wounds.
S2:—Which Wounds Count—This card is referring only to new wounds you receive into your hand.582
S3:—Retroactive Effect—Both effects also apply retroactively during the turn in which you play them.
O4:—Power of Pain—If ARYTHEA takes a Wound, and then plays Power of Pain and discards that
Wound, can she then play the strong effect of Rush of Adrenaline? If so, does she throw away the discarded
Wound? If not, would she throw away the next Wound she takes?
A4: I think ARYTHEA would be prevented from playing the strong effect because she cannot pay the re-
quired cost, that is, throwing away the first Wound she received this turn (as she has already discarded it).583
S5:—Knockout—With the strong effect, the Wound that is thrown away—if it was gained during Com-
bat—still counts toward your being knocked out.584
Q6:—Knockout—If you take enough Wounds to get knocked out, do you draw your Rush of Adrenaline
card(s) before the knockout rule makes you discard your hand, or after?
A6: Core rule mechanics should have priority of execution over card effects unless card effects explicit-
ly state otherwise. So after you finish taking Wounds into your hand from this enemy's current attack, you
check to see if you are knocked out. If you are, you discard all non-wound cards from your hand. Only then do
any card draws from Rush of Adrenaline apply.585


[PAGE 068]
p. 68 Dual-Colored Action Cards
Q7:—Paralyze—What happens when an enemy with the paralyze ability wounds your Mage Knight:
does the paralyze ability cause you to first discard all non-wound cards before making any dictated card draws?
A7: The paralyze damage is an inherent part of taking the Wound, so treat the resulting paralyzation as
happening immediately, before Rush of Adrenaline can initiate card draws. See Q6 above.586
S8:—Fire Resistance—Neither effect of Rush of Adrenaline is lessened in any way when Wounds are
taken from an enemy token that has Fire Resistance: the full effect of Rush of Adrenaline continues to apply.587
S9:—Maximal Effect—With the basic effect of Maximal Effect, I would draw three cards for each of the
first three wounds. With the strong effect, only the first wound is thrown away, but you draw two cards for the
first four wounds you take, not one.588


[PAGE 069]
Spells p. 69
Spell Cards
# S1:—Spell Color—Spell cards are considered to be the color of the mana used to power their basic ef-
fects. So if you use a red Spell card (either its basic effect or its black-mana-boosted strong effect) against an
enemy with Fire Resistance, then any non-Attack/non-Block effects of that Spell will not affect that enemy. See
Combat Q8, Maximal Effect Q3, and Disintegrate S2.589
S2:—Basic Effect—The basic effect of Spell cards—as with all Deed cards—can be played at night as
well as during the day.590
Burning Shield
# Q1:—SUMMONERS—If ORC SUMMONERS summon a HYDRA, and you use Burning Shield to Block one of
the HYDRA'S three Attacks but take wounds from its other two Attacks, does the second part of Burning Shield
kick in for your having used it as part of a successful Block?
A1: There's a distinction between successfully blocking an attack and successfully blocking an enemy.
The wording of Burning Shield appears to describe the former. If so, then the Fire Attack of Burning Shield is
available to attack the ORC SUMMONERS with. See Combat S23. [Some players disagree.]591
S2:—Permissible Targets—The gained Fire Attack need not be used against the same enemy that the
Fire Block was used against. It can even be used on a SUMMONER whose MONSTER you successfully blocked.592
S3:—No Second Mana Needed—If you Block successfully, you don't pay any mana to activate the Fire
Attack. And the Fire Attack works against enemies with Arcane Immunity and those with Fire Resistance.593
Cross References: Exploding Shield S3; Deadly Aim Q4; Mana Overload O4; Spirit Guides S3.
Call to Arms
# S1:—Timing—You can cast Call to Arms early in a turn and then use its effect sometime later in the
same turn. And you can use Call to Arms during Combat, as indicated by its Special Effects icon.594
S2:—Sites—You can't benefit from Call to Arms while in a DUNGEON/TOMB because Units can't be used
there. And if you've already taken a Unit with you into a MAZE/LABYRINTH, you can't cast it there either.
[Can you use Call to Arms in a MAZE/LABYRINTH if you've gone in unaccompanied and have drawn the
enemy token(s)? I'd say yes. What if your accompanying Unit has just been killed by a Paralyze attack? Again,
I'd say yes, although the site card does refer to your Unit "during the entire action", so some may disagree.]595
S3:—Limitations—With Call to Arms, you cannot ready a spent Unit that you already control, nor re-
fresh the ability of a Unit that you have used. And you cannot use it with Bonds of Loyalty to notionally "take
this Unit with you" where it's otherwise prohibited, nor to force a Unit to help you burn a MONASTERY. See S13
below.596
Nor can you use Call to Arms to use a MONASTERY Advanced Action card in the Unit Offer.597
Against enemy tokens with Arcane Immunity, you can use Unit Abilities per the usual restrictions.
Q4:—SHOCKTROOPS—What happens if you use Call to Arms and the bottom effect of SHOCKTROOPS?
A4: The designer, regarding using Into the Heat with SHOCKTROOPS, has said that for SHOCKTROOPS "we
should have written 'even if not allowed otherwise' instead of 'even if that enemy has Assassination'."598
So, go-
ing by the re-worded text, any damage from the attack should be assigned to the SHOCKTROOPS, giving it a
Wound (even though it's still in the Unit Offer). See Q6 below and SHOCKTROOPS S4.599
S5:—SHOCKTROOPS—If a SHOCKTROOPS that was put into play by Call to Arms absorbs damage from a
Paralyze Attack, it should be removed from the Unit Offer. (If Units can be wounded while in the Unit Offer, it
stands to reason they can also be killed while there.) See the previous entry and the next entry.


[PAGE 070]
p. 70 Spells
Q6:—UTEM SWORDSMEN—What happens if you use Call to Arms ("You cannot assign damage to this
Unit.") and you choose the lower effect of the UTEM SWORDSMEN ("This Unit becomes wounded.")?
A6: The Unit becomes wounded, but note that you're not "assigning damage" to it. It'll remain wounded,
and so can't be used in this way again. However the rules state that any Unit recruited from the Offer is recruited
ready and healthy, so when recruited, the wounded UTEM SWORDSMEN will at that moment be Healed.600
S7:—ILLUSIONISTS—This is legal: use Call to Arms to invoke the Influence ability of an ILLUSIONISTS,
and then use those Influence points to help you recruit the very same ILLUSIONISTS (assuming your Action for
the turn is Interact with the Locals and you're at a site where you can recruit them). You could then spend the
newly acquired ILLUSIONISTS to recruit yet another Unit, all in the same turn.601
S8:—MAGIC FAMILIARS—See MAGIC FAMILIARS S2: Call to Arms does not work on them.602
S9:—DELPHANA MASTERS—See DELPHANA MASTERS S1: Call to Arms does not work on them.603
S10:—Into the Heat—Call to Arms can be combined with Into the Heat to give a Unit in the Unit Offer
an increase to its Attack and Block.604
O11:—Leadership—Call to Arms can be used in concert with Leadership, which adds additional Attack/
Block points to a single Unit.605
S12:—PvP—If Call to Arms is used in PvP combat, and you need to know how many enemies you're
facing (for effects like Flame Wave), then be aware that the Call to Arms Unit can count toward the number of
enemies.606
S13:—MONASTERY—You cannot use Call to Arms on a Unit when burning a MONASTERY, for you have to
treat that Unit "as if were one of your recruits".607
S14:—Inspiration—You cannot re-ready a Call-to-Arms Unit with Inspiration.608
S15:—Costs—If there's a mana cost to use an ability of the Unit in the Offer, it must be paid.609
S16:—HEROES—You can use Call to Arms on the Influence ability of a HEROES Unit to help you recruit
that self-same HEROES Unit. And if you do so, the HEROES Unit will come to you unused (ready).610
Cross References: Call to Glory S1; Leadership Q3; MAGIC FAMILIARS S2; SHOCKTROOPS S4; UTEM
SWORDSMEN S1; DELPHANA MASTERS S1.
Call to Glory
# S1:—Adventure Sites—You can cast this while in a DUNGEON/TOMB, or while accompanied by a Unit in
a MAZE/LABYRINTH, but this new Unit doesn't join you in the ADVENTURE SITE (unless you're in a MAZE/LABY-
RINTH and you disband and replace the Unit accompanying you, or unless that Unit just died). [Some players
disallow calling a Unit to join you in a MAZE/LABYRINTH if you went in alone, but I allow it. See Call to Arms
S2.]611
S2:—Locals Unneeded—You don't have to be on a site with Locals to recruit with Call to Glory.612
S3:—MAGIC FAMILIARS—See MAGIC FAMILIARS S2: Call to Glory does not work with them.613
S4:—DELPHANA MASTERS—See DELPHANA MASTERS S1: Call to Glory does not work with them.614

Spells p. 71
S5:—Combat—You can use Call to Glory during Combat, due to its "special effects" icon.615

Charm
# S1:—When Playable—You're allowed to play Charm and not spend the resulting Influence points. And
if you're at a site that allows Interaction with the Locals and you choose Interaction as your Action for the turn,
then playing Charm can gain you a crystal which you can use immediately if you wish.616
Chill
# S1:—Ice Resistance/Arcane Immunity—Chill and Lethal Chill have no effect on enemies with Ice Resis-
tance, nor on enemy tokens with Arcane Immunity.617
Q2:—Timing—Can you use Chill/Lethal Chill on an enemy after it has already assigned damage?
A2: The loss of Fire Resistance/Armor seems like a secondary effect tied to the primary effect of "enemy
does not attack." However, since delaying the play of these Spells reduces their effectiveness, I see nothing
game-breaking in using them after the targeted enemy token has already assigned its damage. (Why couldn't
you make an enemy chilly after he punches you instead of before?) A player could even play Chill/Lethal Chill
between the attacks of an enemy with multiple attacks. See AMOTEP FREEZERS O2.618
S3:—SUMMONERS—Chill/Lethal Chill prevent SUMMONERS from drawing MONSTER tokens, but of course
only if they don't have Ice Resistance, per S1 just above. See ILLUSIONISTS S2.619
Cross References: Combat S33; AMOTEP FREEZERS O2.
Cure
# O1:—Readying Units—A Unit need not be fully Healed to become readied, but it must have had a least
one Wound Healed to be readied.620
S2:—All Healing Counts—You draw a card for each Wound Healed from your hand, not only those
healed earlier in the turn but also those healed after you play Cure. The means of healing do not matter, whether
it's through Skills or other cards or map sites. But Wounds thrown away in a MAGICAL GLADE or removed
through effects not explicitly labeled "Healing" do not count.621
O3:—Healing Ritual—Does using Healing Ritual to throw away Wounds earn you card draws from
Cure?
A3: Healing Ritual has "Healing" in its name, despite the word not appearing in the body of its descrip-
tion. And there's a hand icon on the Skill description card, so in my view it is Healing.
[Some players disagree, saying the phrasing is like that used on the MAGICAL GLADE site card.]622
Q4:—Multiple Heals and Draws—If there's a Wound in your hand when you play Cure, and you heal
and discard it, then you will draw a card. If this bonus card is also a Wound, can you heal that too?
A4: Yes. And you draw a second bonus card from doing so. See Miscellaneous S9.623
Cross References: Healing Ritual Q4.

p. 72 Spells
Demolish
# S1:—Site Fortifications—This Spell negates site fortifications only (not the fortified icon on enemy to-
kens)—but it negates all site fortifications. If an enemy is in a fortified site behind a WALL, Demolish negates
both fortifications.624
Q2:—Fire Resistance—Does the Demolish Spell work on enemies with Fire Resistance?
A2: The first part does, as it targets the structure not the enemy itself. The armor reduction does not.625
S3:—Arcane Immunity—Site fortifications get ignored even for enemies with Arcane Immunity. See Q2
just above.626
S4:—Fortifications Only—Only fortifications are affected (and the armor of enemy tokens). The bonus-
es that CITIES provide—armor or elemental attack increases, Brutal or Poison attributes—still apply.627
S5:—Future Turns—If you use Demolish to negate site fortifications (whether those fortifications come
from WALLS, or CITIES/MAGE TOWERS/KEEPS, or both) but then fail to defeat all of the enemy tokens, you might
re-attack that site on a future turn. If you do so, the fortifications will once again be intact. The benefits of De-
molish do not extend to other Mage Knights participating in a Cooperative Assault.628
Cross References: Combat S33; Earthquake Q2; Expose S1; ILLUSIONISTS S3.
Disease
# S1:—Multiple Attacks—All of the Attacks of an enemy need to be successfully Blocked for the armor
reduction to apply.629
S2:—White CITY Bonus—When Disease is played against an enemy token inside the White CITY, the +1
armor bonus of the CITY is applied first, then Disease reduces the Armor to 1. See CITIES S7.630
Cross References: CITIES S7; Counterattack S4; Shield Bash S4; Burning Shield Q1.
Disintegrate
# S1:—Fire Resistance / Arcane Immunity— Disintegrate will not destroy enemy tokens with Fire Resis-
tance, such as FIRE MAGES. If you use Disintegrate to destroy a susceptible enemy token, other enemies with
Fire Resistance will not suffer -1 to their armor. The same goes for enemy tokens with Arcane Immunity.631
S2:—Tome of All Spells—If you cast Disintegrate through the Tome of All Spells, any enemies with Fire
Resistance will still be immune despite your paying no mana to cast it. See Combat Q7 and Maximal Effect Q3.
S3:—Target of the Spell— Disintegrate eliminates only one enemy token: whichever single enemy to-
ken the caster chooses (as long as the token is susceptible to this attack—e.g., no Fire Resistance, no Arcane
Immunity). And whenever a Mage Knight casts Disintegrate, she must destroy an enemy token in order for oth-
er enemies to get -1 to their Armor.632
Cross References: Combat S33; PvP S9; Ambush Q8.
Earthquake
# S1:—Underground Attack—If you play Underground Attack followed by Earthquake, only the lesser
armor reductions of Earthquake apply because the enemy tokens will no longer be fortified (except for enemies
with the fortified icon on their token, as these are still fortified.)633


[PAGE 073]
Spells p. 73
Q2:—Demolish—If you assault a CITY and play both Earthquake and Demolish, what happens?
A2: Order matters. Play Earthquake first to reduce enemy armor by the larger amounts (-6 or -4), then
play Demolish to remove the site fortifications.634
S3:—Doubly Fortified—The larger armor reduction still applies even if an enemy is doubly fortified.635
S4:—Block Phase Use—You can wait till the Block Phase before playing Earthquake and still get the
larger reduction versus fortified enemies.636
S5:—Mixed Fortifications—If you play Earthquake when some enemies are fortified and some are not,
and you choose the armor reduction for "all enemies", then the unfortified enemies get the lesser (-2) armor re-
duction and the fortified enemies get the larger (-4) armor reduction.637
S6:—Arcane Immunity—Neither Earthquake nor Tremor affect the armor of Arcane Immune ene-
mies.638
Cross References: Mass Expose S1; Tremor S1; Invocation S2; Know your Prey Q1; DELPHANA MAS-
TERS S6.
Energy Flow
# S1:—Spending Opponent's Units—When you "spend" Units in other players' areas, simply slide their
command token onto their Unit: you don't get to use any of their abilities.639
S2:—Spending Opponent's Units—You may spend an opponent's Unit even if that Unit is wounded.640
O3:—Spending Opponent's Units—When you spend Units in other players' Unit areas, you can choose
to do so for some players and not for other players. [The wording on this card is unclear, so I let myself be guid-
ed by the "may" and allow the choice.]641
Energy Steal
# Q1:—DUNGEON / TOMB—Can you cast Energy Steal while you're in a DUNGEON during the day?
A1: No. Energy Steal is a Healing Spell and cannot be cast during Combat. As soon as you enter a DUN-
GEON/TOMB, you're in Combat; and as soon as the Combat ends, you are no longer in the DUNGEON/TOMB and,
since it's day, you can no longer use black mana. See Combat Q1.642
S2:—Spent Units—If you have an unspent wounded Unit, you cannot heal it with this Spell. But you
can ready a spent unwounded Unit and then still spend your opponents' Units.643
S3:—Wounded Units—If a Unit has two Wounds, only one Wound is healed.644
S4:—Spending Opponent's Units—You may use Energy Steal to spend an opponent's Unit even if that
Unit is wounded. See Energy Flow S2.
Cross References: Mana Q7; Combat Q2.
Exploding Shield
# S1:—Fire Resistance—If you use the Fire Block to Block an enemy with Fire Resistance (e.g., FREEZ-
ERS), that enemy will not be destroyed but the Fire Block will work as usual. The same is true for an enemy with
Arcane Immunity.645


[PAGE 074]
p. 74 Spells
Q2:—SUMMONERS—If Exploding Shield blocks a summoned MONSTER, does it destroy the SUMMONER?
A2: No. The card says you destroy the blocked enemy, which is the summoned MONSTER. And note that
destroying the summoned MONSTER does not earn you Fame.646
S3:—Multiple Attacks—For blocking an enemy with Multiple Attacks, see Burning Shield Q1. If you
use Exploding Shield to successfully block one of the attacks of a (non-Fire Resistant, non-Arcane Immune) en-
emy that has Multiple Attacks, the enemy token will be destroyed and none of its attacks will be assigned dur-
ing the Assign Damage Phase. [As discussed in the referenced Burning Shield entry, some players hold the op-
posite view and require that all of the attacks of an enemy token must be successfully blocked.]647
Cross References: Combat S23, S33; Tome of All Spells S2.
Expose
# S1:—Arcane Immunity—An enemy with Arcane Immunity does not lose its fortifications or its Resis-
tances.648
The Ranged Attack, however, can be used normally against any enemy with Arcane Immunity. See
SORCERERS S2 for more discussion on the use of the "Ranged Attack 2".649
Cross References: Combat O46; PvP O10; Resistance Break S2; SORCERERS S1, S2.
Fireball
# S1:—Unplayable vs. Fortification—In the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase, if all enemy tokens are for-
tified or doubly fortified, Fireball cannot be played, even if you simply wish to spend a red mana die.650
See
Combat S13.
Firestorm
# S1:—Wound—The Wound taken is an inherent cost of casting the Spell. And it's always only one
Wound, even if you assign the resulting Siege Fire Attack points to enemies with poison.651
Flame Wave
# S1:—Faction Leaders—Avatars (faction leaders) do count as enemies. See O5 below.652
S2:—Counting Enemies—Any enemies defeated during the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase will not
increase your Block. Any enemies defeated before the Attack Phase will not increase your Attack.653
S3:—Counting Enemies—When counting how many enemy tokens you're facing, count all enemy to-
kens present, not just those you're grouping together for your current Attack. [I include any enemy tokens
you've already blocked or killed during this sub-phase of combat (but exclude any Summoned MONSTERS, per
Q4 just below).] If you're participating in a cooperative CITY assault, count only those enemy tokens (including
any Rampaging Enemies) that're assigned to your Mage Knight.654
Q4:—SUMMONERS—When facing an enemy token that summons MONSTER(S), do you count the sum-
moned enemy tokens when determining the size of the Block?
A4: No—only count the SUMMONER'S token. [A few players argue that, during the Block Phase, you
should count the Summoned MONSTERS but not the SUMMONER—but that argument seems contrary to the official
statement cited in Combat S23.]655


[PAGE 075]
Spells p. 75
O5:—VOLKARE—Does VOLKARE count as an enemy token?
A5: The rules explicitly state that faction leaders are treated as enemy tokens. VOLKARE makes the same
kinds of attacks as faction leaders do, so it makes sense to count him as an enemy token when computing Flame
Wave Block strength. But Flame Wave attacks do not affect VOLKARE directly, so it also makes sense that Flame
Wave Attack strength should only count "attackable" enemies, not VOLKARE.656
Cross References: Call to Arms S12.
Lethal Chill
# Cross References: Chill S1, Q2, S3.
Mana Bolt
# S1:—Mana Cost—When you play Mana Bolt, you must pay two mana: one blue mana and one mana of
a basic color. You cannot pay the initial blue mana and then omit paying the second mana of a basic color. This
Spell can be played only during Combat. Similar statements can also be made regarding Mana Thunderbolt,
where a black mana also has to be paid, raising the number of mana that must be spent to three.657
Cross References: Mana S12; Blood of Ancients S6; Mana Overload S2.
Mana Curse
# S1:—Mana Storm—If Mana Curse is in effect and a player uses the basic effect of Mana Storm, that
player will not take a Wound because that player isn't "using mana". See Who Needs Magic Q1.658

Mana Meltdown
# S1:—Crystal Choice—You choose the color of the crystal you gain: it isn't chosen randomly.
S2:—Crystal Loss—If a player has two blue crystals and one red crystal, then she has a two-thirds
chance of losing a blue crystal and a one-third chance of losing the red crystal.659

# Cross References: End of Round S2.

Mass Expose
# S1:—Earthquake—If you play Earthquake on a CITY (reducing the armor of all fortified tokens), then
play Mass Expose (causing enemies to lose fortifications), this reduces the enemies in the CITY to unfortified,
low-armor targets. See Earthquake Q2 and the endnotes for Expose S1.660
