Meditation
# S1:—End of Round—The gain to your hand size (but not the ability to reorder cards in your deck) will
carry over to the next Round if you play this card on the last turn of the current Round.661


[PAGE 076]
p. 76 Spells
S2:—Chosen Cards—You do get to look at the cards after randomly choosing them. And either (a) both
of the drawn cards must go on top of your Deed deck, or (b) both of the drawn cards must go below your Deed
deck—you don't get to split them. But you do get to choose the order of the two cards.662
[I see a textual argument for a player being able to split the cards above and below (all the more so as
this spell seems relatively weak), but I play by the majority opinion that the cards cannot be split.]
O3:—Card Draw—If you somehow play Meditation/Trance twice during the same turn, or if you cast it
twice via the Time Bending Spell, do you draw two extra cards next turn—or do you draw four?
A3: With Time Bending, you wouldn't draw the extra cards during the first of the two turns because you
skip that portion of the turn. So the choice is either to draw +2 or +4 cards during the upcoming End-of-Turn
sequence. I'd allow a player to stack the abilities and draw +4, especially as the player may have spent up to
four mana to bring this situation about, a steep price!663
S4:—An Empty Discard Pile—You are permitted to play Meditation even when your discard pile is em-
pty. See also S1 above.664
Cross References: Turn Start S1; Mind Read S2; Trance S1.
Mind Read
# S1:—End of Round—You cannot steal a card with Mind Read after the End of the Round has been an-
nounced by a player, nor after the end of a scenario has been triggered by a game-ending condition.665
S2:—Timing—Someone could potentially play Midnight Meditation or Motivation after you announce
you're playing Mind Read or Mind Steal, but not after you've announced the chosen color.666
S3:—Action Card—Action cards include both Basic Action cards and Advanced Action cards.667
Cross References: End of Round S2; Dual-Colored Action Cards S1; Midnight Meditation S1.
Mist Form
# S1:—Hills/Mountains—You cannot enter hills or mountains with the Move 4 you gain from this card.
S2:—Duration—The reduced terrain cost applies for the remainder of your turn.668
S3:—Provoking Rampaging Enemies—Mist Form movement can provoke Rampaging Enemies.669
O4:—Mountain Lore—If played in the same turn with Mountain Lore, can you enter mountains?
A4: I rule no: Mist Form is prohibitive (You cannot . . .), while Mountain Lore is merely permissive
(You can . . .). Furthermore, Mist Form specifically prevents you from entering mountains, which you can't do
under ordinary circumstances anyway, so the developers could very well have had the effect of Mountain Lore
in mind when they wrote the text for Mist Form.
[A few players rule the other way, and say the ability to enter mountains depends solely on the order in
which you play these two cards, the card played later overruling the earlier card. But see Movement S10.]670
Q5:—Lakes—Are lakes safe spaces for you on turns in which you play Mist Form?
A5: No. This effect doesn't say they're safe. You can voluntarily pause on lakes, but if you're still on
them during your End-of-Turn sequence, Forced Withdrawal rules will apply. (Mountain Lore explicitly allows
you to end your turn on a mountain, and it specifically states mountains are safe spaces for you. Since Mist
Form doesn't explicitly state the same, we must assume that lakes are not safe spaces.)671


[PAGE 077]
Spells p. 77
S6:—Walls—You do pay the move cost to cross WALLS. See Movement S5 and O9.
S7:—Move Costs—The reduced Move costs for all terrains applies for the entire turn.672
Cross References: Movement O9.
Offering
# S1:—Rewording—A possible rewording for greater clarity:673
• Gain a red mana crystal into your inventory.
• You may also discard up to 3 non-wound cards from your hand: for each card discarded, gain a
mana crystal matching that card color. (For a discarded Artifact, choose a mana crystal of any
single basic color.)
Possess
# S1:—SUMMONERS—To determine the Attack value of a SUMMONER, draw a MONSTER token and use its
values, but you must play Possess before this MONSTER token is drawn (see Combat S23). The summoned MON-
STER (since it is not a separate, targetable entity) cannot be used to attack its own SUMMONER.
Physical Attacks, Ice Attacks, Fire Attacks, Cold-Fire Attacks, Summon Attacks, and Multiple Attacks
are all "types of attacks" and not Special Abilities, and so can be used by this spell.674
O2:—Complicated Cases—If you use the strong effect of this Spell against a GUNNERS token (Fire At-
tack 6 plus Brutal) defending inside the blue CITY, what Attack do you gain?
A2: You would not gain Brutal, as that is a special ability (nor could you gain Poisonous, Paralyze, As-
sassinate, Vampiric, or Swiftness). You would gain a Fire Attack 6, plus the +2 Fire Attack for the token being
a blue CITY defender (since it's still inside the CITY when it launches its attack).
If the enemy token has multiple attacks (e.g., MAGIC FAMILIARS), you would also gain those multiple at-
tacks. The question then would be, must you use them all together in a single Attack? I rule yes.675
S3:—Arcane Immunity—Possess cannot be used to take control of enemies with Arcane Immunity.676
S4:—Solo Enemy—If there's only one enemy present in a Combat, Possess can still be played, but all it
does is prevent that single enemy from attacking you: you cannot use Possess to make an enemy attack itself.
S5:—PvP—If you play Possess in PvP combat, you have to use it in your next Attack Phase. There's a
strong argument that it still couldn't be used to wound the Unit that got Possessed.677
Cross References: Taunt Q4.
Rebirth
# Q1:—DUNGEON / TOMB—Can you cast Rebirth when you're in a DUNGEON during the day?
A1: No. Rebirth is a Healing Spell and cannot be cast during Combat. As soon as you enter a DUNGEON
you're in Combat, and as soon as the Combat ends you're no longer in the DUNGEON and, since it is day, you can
no longer use black mana. See Combat Q1.678
S2:—Readying Effect Cannot Be Delayed—The Rebirth Spell can be used to ready one or more Units,
but it cannot be used to ready the same Unit twice: you have to process the entire effect immediately, and thus
you cannot use the Unit in between your readying acts. This doesn't apply to the Heal points that Rebirth pro-
vides, for as usual these points can be banked for future use: see Miscellaneous S9.679
Cross References: Ruthless Coercion S1; Magic Talent S5; Regeneration S2; Tome of All Spells S3.


[PAGE 078]
p. 78 Spells
Restoration
# Cross References: Magic Talent S5; Tome of All Spells S3.
Sacrifice
# S1:—Rewording—A possible rewording for greater clarity:680
Select exactly two mana colors: choose either green or white, and then either red or blue. Gain:
Siege Fire Attack 4 (for each green and red crystal pair in your inventory), or
Siege Ice Attack 4 (for each green and blue crystal pair in your inventory), or
Ranged Fire Attack 6 (for each white and red crystal pair in your inventory), or
Ranged Ice Attack 6 (for each white and blue crystal pair in your inventory).
Then turn all of the crystal pairs (of the two chosen colors) into mana tokens.
S2:—Lose All Crystal Pairs—A player cannot elect to hold back complete pairs of the chosen colors.681
S3:—Attack Points Must Be Combined—If you have two pairs of crystals, you get one Attack 8 or one
Attack 12—you don't get two separate Attacks 4's or two separate Attack 6's.682
S4:—Using Converted Crystals—When generating Attack points with Sacrifice, the specified crystal
pairs are immediately turned into mana tokens which you can use anytime for any purpose during the remainder
of your turn. For instance . . .
—You could use the newly transformed mana tokens to power additional Attack effects that you com-
bine with the Attack points generated by Sacrifice and apply them against a single (group of) enemy token(s).
—You could even use the newly transformed mana tokens to power non-Attack effects (such as the
green- or white-mana abilities of DELPHANA MASTERS) to cancel resistances or reduce the armor of the enemies
you'll be attacking with the Sacrifice Attack points. See Combat S28.
For further relevant discussion, see Combat S13, S28, O46, and S49.683
Cross References: Combat S49; Crystal Mastery S2.
Space Bending
# Q1:—Multiple Use—Can Space Bending be used more than once in a turn? Can you continue to hop?
A1: Yes, the ability lasts for the rest of your turn (so the Spell really isn't being used more than once).684
S2:—Exploring Tiles—Space Bending lets you explore tiles without being directly adjacent to them.685
S3:—Movement Costs—With Space Bending, you still pay 2 Movement points when you explore a new
tile. And you still need to spend the movement point cost of the space you're entering when you move.686
S4:—Combines with Other Effects—Space Bending complements and expands the capabilities of Flight,
Wings of Wind, and Underground Travel: see Flight Q1 and S2 for an example of how other effects work in
concert with Space Bending.687
S5:—Before a Failed Attack—If you move to a fortified site with Space Bending and don't defeat all of
the enemy tokens in that site, then you retreat to the space you traveled from (two spaces away). This retreat
does not cause you to take Wounds (although if the space you have traveled back to is itself not a safe space,
you'll conduct a Forced Withdrawal at the end of your turn and take Wound(s) then): see Forced Withdrawal
S4.688
S6:—Walls—Space Bending says you move to distance spaces "as if they were adjacent". When you
move like this, you ignore the movement cost of all intervening WALLS.689


[PAGE 079]
Spells p. 79
S7—Revealing Enemy Tokens—If you have Space Bending active and are two spaces from a MAGE
TOWER or KEEP during the day, you do not reveal the enemy token in it.690
Cross References: Movement O9; CITIES S10; Flight Q1, S2.
Time Bending
# S1:—Setting the Card Aside—Time Bending is set aside after it's played. If you use another card (say
Magic Talent) to cast Time Bending, that other card is set aside instead.691
S2:—End of Round—If the end of the Round has been declared, you can still play Time Bending and
take another immediate turn.692
S3:—Maximal Effect—Time Bending does not work with Maximal Effect because Time Bending is not
an Action card. See S13 below.693
S4:—DUNGEON / TOMB—You can cast Time Bending during the day while in a DUNGEON or TOMB.694
S5:—Casting It Twice—The only way to use Time Bending more than once in a Round is to use it from
the Spell Offer via Magic Talent, then acquire Time Bending from the Spell Offer via normal means. (No matter
what, you're not permitted to cast Time Bending twice in a row, even if you somehow get it into your hand again
during a Time Bent turn: see S13 below.)695
S6:—Plundering VILLAGES—You can plunder a VILLAGE between a normal turn and a Time Bent turn.696
S7:—Preparing for the Time-Bent Turn—When finishing your first, non-Time Bent turn . . .
Any used mana dice are returned to the SOURCE and all mana tokens are discarded.
Check for Forced Withdrawal, use your Space (MAGICAL GLADE, CRYSTAL MINE), receive Rewards from
Combat.
Any cards in your play area at the end of the turn are returned to your hand.
Any cards that were discarded (e.g., with Improvisation) or thrown away do not return to your hand.
Any Banners that were played onto Units remain there (see O12 below), and if the Banner of Courage
got flipped, it remains flipped.
Any Wounds taken into your hand that have not been discarded or thrown away, remain there. But you
don't consider hand limits when you re-take your turn, so Wounds won't be an immediate problem.
You can level up at the end of the first turn. (New cards placed on your Deed deck, though, won't ordi-
narily get drawn since the "draw cards" step in the End-of-Turn sequence is skipped.)
A Time Bending turn is an entirely new turn; it is not part of the turn that preceded it.697
S8:—PvP / VOLKARE—How you handle Time Bending while you're in a PvP Combat or in Combat with
VOLKARE depends on if you're fully attending or partially attending the Combat:
If you're fully attending, you immediately take your next turn—but that next turn consists of just one
step: flipping your Round Order token face up. (You keep the redrawn cards as your hand.) This also happens if
you're participating in a Cooperative Assault when you cast Time Bending: see S11 below.
If you're only partially attending, things get more complicated because you weren't taking your full turn
yet and your turn doesn't end until you do take your turn. I recommend not casting Time Bending until it is your
full turn, but if you insist on casting it while only partially attending then whenever you finish the partially at-
tended combat, leave the cards you played in your play area because you'll be collecting them again at the end
of your full turn. Then, when that full turn does end, you immediately take your Time Bent turn.698
S9:—PvP and Stolen Artifacts—You do not get back any Artifacts stolen from you in PvP combat.699


[PAGE 080]
p. 80 Spells
S10:—One Last Turn—In several scenarios you get one last turn after the last CITY has been conquered.
If you cast Time Bending while you're conquering that last CITY, you can get two more turns.700
S11:—Cooperative Assaults—If someone casts Time Bending during a cooperative assault, her Time
Bent turn doesn't begin until all of the participating players complete their cooperative assaults and any used
SOURCE dice are re-rolled. If played by the one who initiated the cooperative assault, she now gets a full turn. If
played by someone else, the Time Bending player simply turns her Round Order token face up.701
O12:—Banners—If you play a Banner onto a Unit during the first turn, does it return to your hand for
use during the second turn? The majority opinion is no, it does not. [Others rule yes, for you get to use other
types of Artifacts twice, so why shouldn't you be able to use Banners twice?] See S7 above.702
S13:—Casting It Twice—You are not permitted to take two Time Bending turns in a row, no matter
what combination of effects you play nor what arguments you make. Think of the Time Bending Spell as having
this additional text: "Time Bending is rendered powerless when attempting to cast it during a Time Bent turn".703
S14:—Skills—At the start of the Time Bent turn, previously used Once-a-Turn Skills are "refreshed" and
available for use again. Once-a-Round Skills, however, do not automatically get unflipped. Persistent Skills that
were played during the immediately preceding turn expire at the start of the Time Bent turn (except Cooperative
Skills, which can be used in the Time Bent turn as normal). See Skill Tokens S5.704
Cross References: Meditation O3; Bow of Starsdawn Q1; Mana Suppression S2; SOURCE Freeze S3.

Tremor
# S1:—All Enemies—When this card says "all enemies", it means all enemies that you're facing, not just
those that you're grouping together for a single combined attack. (The same applies for Earthquake.) In cooper-
ative assaults, the phrase "all enemies" means all the enemies that you yourself are facing.706
But even if you
group enemy tokens together, only one enemy token can receive a -3 to its armor value.707
S2:—Always Playable in Combat—Even if this Spell would have no effect in the present Combat, such
as when facing a single enemy with Arcane Immunity, it can still be played.

Underground Attack
# S1:—Arcane Immunity—You ignore site fortifications even for those enemies that have Arcane Immu-
nity, as in Demolish S3.708
S2:—Moving Through Obstacles—You are allowed to move underneath Rampaging Enemies, CITIES,
KEEPS, Mage Knights, etc. This movement will not provoke Rampaging Enemies.709


[PAGE 081]
Spells p. 81
S3:—To Move to an Empty Site—To end on an already conquered fortified site that is empty of oppos-
ing Mage Knights, simply use Underground Travel.710
S4:—Green CITY—You are allowed to attack the green CITY even though it appears to be on a swamp
space because all CITIES are actually in "CITY" terrain.711
O5:—PvP—Can you use Underground Attack to attack an opposing Mage Knight who is standing on a
mountain space after his having used the advanced action Mountain Lore to legally stop there?
A5: Underground Attack likely assumes that your movement must be to a space you're otherwise able to
enter via ordinary movement—and it has to be a fortified site! If you can't physically tunnel into such a space by
Underground Travel, then you probably shouldn't be able to do so by Underground Attack either—see Under-
ground Travel O2.712
O6:—VOLKARE—Some argue for allowing Underground Attack to attack VOLKARE in his camp tile when
it's being used in place of a CITY, but this argument feels tenuous at best. In any event, you can't use Under-
ground Attack to attack VOLKARE when he's on, say, a mountain, for a mountain isn't a fortified site.713
Cross References: Earthquake S1; Wings of Wind S1; Flight S2.
Underground Travel
# S1:—Movement is Free—You don't expend movement points to move these three spaces: it's free.714
O2:—Safe Space—When it says "end your move on a safe space", is it talking about the Underground
Travel portion of your move, or is it talking about the end of the Movement Phase as a whole?
A2: It appears to mean "your Underground Travel movement of up to three spaces".715
S3:—Combining with other Movement—During the Movement Phase you're allowed to move by ex-
pending Move points both before and after playing Underground Travel, but you can't move one or two spaces
by Underground Travel, then do a regular move, then move more spaces by Underground Travel.716
S4:—Moving through CITIES—Since CITIES are never in swamps or lakes (including the green CITY), you
can move through them.717
Cross References: Movement S2, S5, O9; Savage Harvesting O2; Mountain Lore O3; Space Bending

Veil of Mist
# S1:—Only One Wound Is Ignored—Veil of Mist ignores the first Wound received from any enemy this
turn, not the first Wound from each enemy. And it only ignores a Wound to your Mage Knight, not to a Unit.718
S2:—Brutal—If your Mage Knight herself must take damage from an Attack 7 that is Brutal, and her
armor is 3, she will take a total of 4 Wounds: (7x2 ÷ 3) - 1 = 3.67, which rounds to 4. 719
O3:—VOLKARE—VOLKARE does count as an enemy.720
S4:—Additional Effects—The phrase "ignore any additional effects of that wound" applies only to the
Mage Knight herself, not to her Units.721


[PAGE 082]
p. 82 Spells
S5:—Combat Example—If a player is facing FREEZERS (Cold Attack 3, Paralyze, Swiftness), and plays
Veil of Mist, this will allow a GUARDIAN GOLEMS Unit (Armor 3, Physical Resistance) to avoid any damage be-
cause in this case Veil conveys Ice Resistance to the GUARDIAN GOLEMS and the damage inflicted by the FREEZ-
ERS (3) is less than or equal to the GUARDIAN GOLEMS armor value (3).
If the FREEZERS' Cold Attack had a value of 4 instead, then the GUARDIAN GOLEMS would take a wound
and thereby be destroyed, due to the Paralyze effect (per S4 just above).722
Whirlwind
# S1:—Elusive—Using Whirlwind to prevent an enemy token with the Elusive ability from attacking does
not cause that enemy token to drop to its lower armor value. See Banner of Fear Q1.723

Wings of Night
# S1:—Movement Points Needed—To prevent, say, four enemy tokens from attacking during this combat,
you need to pay six Move points: the first enemy token is free, the second enemy costs 1 Move point, the third
costs 2 Move points, and the fourth costs 3 Move points: 0 + 1 + 2 + 3 = 6 Move points.724
S2:—Arcane Immunity—Wings has no effect on Arcane Immune enemies: see Arcane Immunity S1.
Cross References: PvP S7.
Wings of Wind
# S1:—Flight—See Flight S2 and Movement S5. For obvious thematic reasons, Wings of Wind will not
work with Underground Travel/Attack.
S2:—Exploring Tiles—You cannot pause Wings of Wind movement, explore new tiles, then continue to
move with Wings of Wind. Nor can you use any of the 1-to-5 spent movement points to explore new tiles (nor
use previously banked movement points to do so).725
O3:—Safe Space—When it says "end your move in a safe space", Wings of Wind is talking about the
movement provided by Wings of Wind itself, not about the end of your Movement Phase.726
O4:—Reacting to New Information—(a) As you move with Wings of Wind, can new enemy tokens be
revealed? (b) Can you change the distance you will move (and the number of Move points you spend) on the
basis of any newly revealed enemy tokens? (c) Can you change your movement path on the basis of newly re-
vealed tokens? (d) Could you move back and forth between two spaces, if you're forced to move exactly the
previously-chosen number of spaces?727
A4: Question (a) is the tricky one: but it should be answered no, via the argument in Movement O9.728
S5:—Water—You can move over water with Wings of Wind. And you can also move over KEEPS and
MAGE TOWERS and Rampaging Enemies.729

Artifact Cards
# S1:—Assigning Banners—You can assign Banners to Units at any time during your turn. But the strong
effect of a Banner card—which causes it to be thrown away—has to be played from your hand, not while the
Banner is attached to a Unit. And Banners, like all Artifacts (even those purchased at the red CITY), are gained
during the End-of-Turn sequence and so cannot immediately be assigned to Units: see End of Turn S2.730
Q2:—Multiple Use of Banners—The rules say that when preparing for a new Round, flip all Banner Ar-
tifacts face up. When do Banners get flipped face down? Can Banners be used more than once per Round?
A2: Follow the instructions on each specific Banner card. For instance if you somehow re-ready a Unit
with the Banner of Fear, you can use the Banner again. The Banner of Fortitude, though, says "once a Round",
so ordinarily this Banner cannot be reused during the same Round.731
Cross References: Influence S8; CITIES S21; End of Turn S5, S6; Miscellaneous S1; Basic Action Cards
S1; Concentration S6; Crystal Joy S1; Magic Talent S3; Offering S1; Time Bending S7, S9.
Amulet of Darkness
# S1:—Gold and Black Mana—You can gain black mana token(s) if you wish. And when you're other-
wise entitled to mana dice from the SOURCE, you're allowed to take black mana dice. And you're allowed to use
black mana, day or night, no matter its source, including using it to cast the strong spell on Spell cards. But gold
mana, although still usable during the day, remains unusable at night. And note that "Night Rules" don't neces-
sarily apply while using the Amulet of Darkness—see Miscellaneous S10. See Amulet of the Sun S1.732
S2:—Duration—The effects of both Amulets apply until the end of the turn in which they're played.733
Amulet of the Sun
# S1:—At Night—The Movement cost for desert remains three at night. And you can still use black mana
at night, but remember that gold mana cannot be turned into black mana. Skills that have differing effects dur-
ing day and night (Day Sharpshooting, Dark Negotiation, etc.) use their night values at night. See Miscellane-
ous S10, and Amulet of Darkness S1 and S2.734
Banner of Command
# S1:—General—Lost Legion p. 5 discusses this specific banner: read that section. If you reclaim this
banner at the End of a Round, the Unit is thrown away. You cannot replace the Unit having this banner with a
newly recruited Unit. A Unit with this Banner can be wounded and spent like ordinary Units.735
S2:—Recruiting a Unit—It's permissible, all in one turn, to play Banner of Command, use the Influence
it grants to recruit a Unit, and then assign the Banner of Command to that same Unit.736
S3:—No Location Restrictions—Just as with Call to Glory, you don't have to be on a site with Locals to
recruit a Unit through the strong effect of the Banner of Command. See S4.737
S4:—Strong Effect—The card text exactly matches that of Call to Glory, so it should work similarly,
including in Combat and in allowing recruitment from any tile space. See Call to Glory S2 and S5.738

p. 84 Artifacts
Banner of Fear
# Q1:—Does Not Count as Blocking—Does using Banner of Fear count as Blocking an enemy?
A1: Banner of Fear is not blocking an Attack—it is canceling an Attack. Reducing an Attack to zero is
blocking it; preventing it from attacking is not blocking it—so the higher armor value of Elusive still applies.740
Q2:—Multiple Attacks—Does Banner of Fear prevent all attacks from an enemy with Multiple Attacks?
A2: The basic effect does not: the Banner cancels only one of the attacks. But the strong effect—which
must be played directly from your hand—completely stops three enemy tokens from attacking at all.741
S3:—Spending the Unit—When a Unit gains the Banner of Fear, it gains an additional ability: the abili-
ty to cancel one enemy attack. Using this Banner's ability spends the Unit but does not "use up" the Banner: if
you can re-ready the Unit somehow, you can use the Banner again. You cannot use any other ability of this Unit
at the same time that you use the Banner of Fear ability. [I'm not sure this last limit applies when it's attached to
DELPHANA MASTERS, so I allow DELPHANA MASTERS to use their other abilities as well.]742
S4:—Wounded Unit—A wounded Unit with the Banner of Fear cannot use the Banner's ability.743
Q5:—SUMMONERS—Can the Banner of Fear prevent a SUMMONER from summoning a MONSTER?
A5: Yes—if the SUMMONER doesn't have Arcane Immunity. See Q7 below.744
Q6:—SUMMONERS—If you don't use the Banner of Fear to cancel a SUMMONER'S summoning of a MON-
STER), can you use the Banner of Fear to cancel the Attack of the MONSTER that is summoned?
A6: No, per an official ruling: "The summoning of the MONSTER" and "the MONSTER launching its at-
tack" are essentially the same simultaneous effect—they're both "the Attack of the SUMMONER". The SUMMON-
ER'S Attack is to hit you with the attack of a MONSTER whose attack it has summoned. (Perhaps the SUMMONER
summons the MONSTER inches from you, its claws ripping. Or perhaps the SUMMONER only summons the Attack
of the MONSTER, not the MONSTER itself.) In any event you don't get two bites at the apple: a chance to cancel
the summoning and then a chance to cancel the attack of the thing summoned: they're one-in-the-same event.
You can reduce a summoned MONSTER'S attack, you can block it, but once you've drawn a MONSTER you've
missed your chance to cancel its attack. See Combat S23.745
Q7:—Arcane Immunity—Can a Unit with the Banner of Fear prevent an attack by an enemy with Ar-
cane Immunity, such as that of the DRAGON SUMMONER?
A7: No. See Arcane Immunity S1.746
[Some players rule that the canceling an enemy attack is effective against enemies with Arcane Immun-
ity because Arcane Immunity permits effects that "reduce an enemy attack or that affect it in any other way".747
But to me, canceling the attack of a token with Arcane Immunity seems in violation of the first part of the rule:
they "are not affected by any non-Attack/non-Block effects . . . [and ignore effects that] prevent it from attack-
ing". Arguing that "canceling an enemy attack" and "preventing an enemy from attacking" are fundamentally
different†
appears to me to be parsing the language of the rule too finely. Plus, I can't envision how "canceling"
a monster's punch would look any different from "preventing" a monster's punch. See Arcane Immunity S1.
†
- Except of course with regard to the number of attacks that are annulled when you prevent an enemy
with multiple attacks from attacking altogether.]748
S8:—VOLKARE—You cannot use the Banner of Fear against VOLKARE. See Q7 just above.749


[PAGE 085]
Artifacts p. 85
Banner of Fortitude
# S1:—Assigning Damage—Using Banner of Fortitude "to ignore the Wound and any additional effects"
does count as using the Unit's one allowable "assigning of damage" for this combat.
And you do have to pay THUGS two influence to have them use the Banner in this way.750
S2:—Additional Effects—The "additional effects" prevented by the Banner of Fortitude include Para-
lyze (destruction) and Poison (an additional Wound) and Vampiric (+1 armor bonus for the attacker), but not
Brutal of course (which doubles the damage just before Wounds get assigned).751
Cross References: PvP Q1.
Banner of Glory
# Cross References: Miscellaneous S12.
Banner of Protection
# Q1:—"All Wounds"—Is the strong effect of Banner of Protection referring only to Wounds received
into your hand, or does it also include Wounds put into your discard pile (from Poisonous Attacks)? And does it
include Wounds your Units received? And must you throw away all such Wounds—or can you keep some?
A1: Wounds placed into the discard pile this turn due to Poisonous Attacks may be thrown away. In fact
any new Wounds placed into your discard pile this turn—either before or after playing the strong effect of this
banner—due to effects like those of Power of Pain, Invocation, or I Feel No Pain, may be thrown away.
But no, it doesn't apply to Wounds your Units have received, nor Wounds you drew from your deck.752
[Regarding whether you must throw away all Wounds you received this turn, the word "may" appears to
allow wiggle room, so I'd let players retain some of their new Wounds if they wish.]753
S2:—Timing—The strong effect of Banner of Protection can be played during combat, as indicated by
the crossed swords icon. So you can play it before you get knocked out (which it doesn't prevent), but then re-
move all of the Wounds your hero himself took this turn when you get to the end of your turn—see Q1.754
Book of Wisdom
# S1:—Dual-Colored Advanced Action Cards—I would allow a player to match either one of the colors of
dual-colored Advanced Action cards to draw them.755
Bow of Starsdawn
# Q1:—Discards—Does the Attack bonus gained from the basic effect of Bow of Starsdawn count the
Bow itself as one of the discarded cards? Do the discarded cards get returned with Time Bending?
A1: No. No. And you can't discard Wounds for use with the Bow.756
S2:—Applies to All Attacks—The strong effect also applies to Ranged Attacks and Siege Attacks pro-
vided by Units and Skills. (With the strong effect, the discard ability of the basic effect does not apply.)757
Q3:—Attack Points Must Be Combined—Must the Ranged Attacks gained with the basic effect of the
Bow be used together in one large Ranged Attack, or can they be used separately against different groups of en-
emies? And can some cards be discarded during the Ranged Attack Phase, and others during the Attack Phase?
A3: They must all be used together in one large Ranged Attack. As for the latter question, no, for the
Bow says, "When you play this, discard . . ." 758
Q4:—Attack Phase—Does the strong effect also apply during the Attack Phase?
A4: No. See Combat S6 and Leadership S2: there is no difference between Attacks, Ranged Attacks,
and Siege Attacks during the Attack Phase—they are all treated as plain old "Attacks".759


[PAGE 086]
p. 86 Artifacts
O5:—ALTEM MAGES—IMO you can use both the strong effect of the Bow of Starsdawn (double your
Siege Attacks and make them Ranged Attacks, or double your Ranged Attacks) and the black-mana ability of
the ALTEM MAGES (all your Attacks become Siege Attacks), but each effect can be applied to an Attack only
once, not recursively. And both effects should be played before the Attack points are played.
IMO you get to choose the order in which to apply the two effects (that of the Bow and that of the MAG-
ES), and you can choose a different order for each Attack-point effect that you play.760
Neither of the effects apply during the Attack Phase because in that phase all Ranged Attacks and all
Siege Attacks are treated as ordinary Attacks. See Combat S6 and Leadership S2.
IMO you can play Ranged Attacks and assign them to a Fortified enemy (which ordinarily wouldn't be
allowed), then double those Ranged Attack points with the previously played Bow, and then with a previously-
powered MAGES ability convert those doubled Ranged Attacks into Siege Attacks (which now will be effective
against a Fortified enemy): see Combat S13 and S49.761
[Other players don't permit any doubling from the Bow
to be applied in between "the playing of Ranged Attack points against a Fortified target" and "the conversion of
those Ranged Attack points into Siege Attack points" by the MAGES.]
The black mana ability "all attacks ... become Cold Fire Attacks" of ALTEM MAGES combines just fine
with the basic effect of the Bow, and also with the strong effect of the Bow.762
S6:—Separate Attacks—With the strong effect, if you have two cards that provide Ranged Attacks, you
are allowed to double one of the Ranged Attacks and turn the other Ranged Attack into a Siege Attack. And if
you have two cards that provide Siege Attacks, you can double one of the Siege Attacks and turn it into a
Ranged Attack, while keeping the other Siege Attack as a Siege Attack.763
Cross References: Miscellaneous S12; Ambush Q8; Deadly Aim Q4.
Circlet of Proficiency
# S1:—General—The Circlet of Proficiency is to be "evaluated as playing a single effect"—Lost Legion,
p. 9. The most straightforward approach for using the basic effect of the Circlet with once-a-turn Skills is to
"play the skill twice" at the moment that you play the card: e.g., have all of the targets or Units or whatever be
determined at the moment the card is played, which generally limits the Skill to the Phase in which the Circlet
is played (although persisting or ensuing effects will work as they usually do).
Another issue is whether to restrict the "use its effect twice" to the same target, thereby treating the two
uses of the Skill as a single doubled effect. Generally, Attack points and Block points should be restricted to a
single group or target (respectively): so Cold Swordsmanship would be (Ice) Attack +4 against a single target,
and Shield Mastery would be a single Block of +6 or +4. See Basic Action Cards S2.764
S2:—Battle Frenzy / Shield Mastery / Forked Lightning—With Battle Frenzy, you must play the Attack
2 plus Attack 2 against the same group of enemy tokens. A similar thing goes for Shield Mastery: it has to be
used against a single enemy Attack. With Forked Lightning, you could stack the six Attack 1's to a maximum of
Attack 2 per any given enemy token.765
S3:—Battle Hardened / Elemental Resistance—With Battle Hardened apply both effects to the current
Attack unless the current Attack produces ≤ 2 points of physical damage or only 1 point of non-physical dam-
age, in which case the second usage of the Skill will apply the next time one or more points of damage would
otherwise be assigned to your Mage Knight. A similar thing goes for Elemental Resistance.766
O4:—Bonds of Loyalty—How does the basic effect interact with Bonds of Loyalty?
A4: The best course is to disallow using the basic effect of the Circlet with Bonds of Loyalty.767


[PAGE 087]
Artifacts p. 87
S5:—Burning Power—With Burning Power, you could get Siege Attack 1 for the first use and get Fire
Siege Attack 1 for the second, but since you must use both Attacks against a single group of enemies (because
the Circlet is evaluated as a single effect), it would never make sense to mix both types of Attack.768
S6:—Curse—With Curse, you can use its effects (a) on two different attacks from the same enemy to-
ken, (b) on a separate attack from two different enemy tokens, (c) on the same attack twice, (d) on the armor
value of two different enemy tokens, (e) on the armor value of one enemy token twice, (f) against one enemy
attack and against the armor of the same enemy token, or (g) against one enemy attack and against the armor of
a different enemy token.769
S7:—Dark Paths—With Dark Paths, you gain the doubled Move points immediately; you cannot gain
some during the Movement Phase and the rest during the Action Phase.770
S8:—Deadly Aim—This combination will affect two different cards and both should be used against the
same group of enemy tokens (because Circlet should be evaluated as a single effect, if possible). It's equivalent
to playing a single Skill that says "Add +1/+2 to a card. Add +1/+2 to a card.", i.e., to two different cards and to
do so immediately. [You could argue for applying the effect twice to the same card, or to cards in two different
Attacks, but the above interpretation seems more consistent with other rulings. See S14 and S16 below.]771
S9:—Dueling—With Dueling, you use the effect during the Block Phase and must use it twice against
the same enemy Attack. So it gives you Block 2 against a single enemy token, then Attack 2 against that same
enemy token, then Fame +2.772
S10:—Feral Allies / Hawk Eyes—With Feral Allies, you're allowed to reduce the Attack of two differ-
ent enemies by one, or the Attack of one enemy twice, but if your first use of the Circlet comes during the At-
tack Phase, you must use both Attack 1's then, which means using both together for an Attack 2. Both uses of
Feral Allies must occur in the same phase: either two Attack reductions or one Attack of 2—not one of each.
As for the cost of exploring tiles, it should only be reduced by 1 because the tile-exploring portion of the
Skill isn't restricted to "once a turn". The same is true for Hawk Eyes.773
S11:—Leadership—With Leadership, you don't apply the bonus twice to the same Unit: you apply the
bonus to two different Units. These Units can be activated at different times during your turn, perhaps one Unit
during the Block Phase and the second Unit during the Attack Phase. Nor do you need to predesignate the sec-
ond of the two Units you'll be activating.774
Q12:—Master of Chaos—How does the basic effect of the Circlet interact with Master of Chaos?
A12: Roll a spare mana die, then take the next two clockwise effects. You have to play them in order,
but you can skip either or both of them. You then remove your shield token from the Skill token.775
S13:—Shapeshift—With Shapeshift, you use it on two different cards, which you can use at any time
during your turn. [A stickler would rule that you must use Shapeshift exactly twice, and that if you can't, then
you can't play this combination.]776


[PAGE 088]
p. 88 Artifacts
S14:—Spirit Guides—Spirit Guides can already span the Movement Phase and Action Phase, so when
Circlet is used with Spirit Guides, you get Move 2 in the current phase (in either the Movement Phase or the
Action Phase—not 1 in each) and in the Combat Phase you get +1 Block to two separate Blocks (perhaps from
Determination, say, and from a Block provided by the GUARDIAN GOLEMS). Both Blocks can be used together
against the same enemy Attack if you wish. [Allowing two "+1 Blocks" to be applied to a single card/Skill/Unit
ability feels contrary to MKBG mechanics: in MKBG, we often stack multiple effects against enemy armor or
against enemy attacks or to affect terrain costs, but we seldom stack more than one modification directly onto a
single base effect. See Circlet of Proficiency S8 and S11, and Who Needs Magic S2.]777
S15:—Taunt—With Taunt, you can use it on two different enemy tokens, or twice on the current enemy
attack (choosing either the same effect twice—"Attack -2" or "Attack +4 and Armor -4"—or choosing each ef-
fect once for a composite "Attack +1 and Armor -2"). In neither case can you use it to increase a token's attack
after you have blocked it.778
S16:—Cards Played Sideways—With I Don't Give a Damn, Power of Pain, or Who Needs Magic, you
have to play the Skill on two different cards/Wounds, but you don't need to play both cards immediately: you
can play the second card later in the turn. Similarly, Universal Power is played on two different sideways cards.
Q: Why can you delay one of these cards, but not delay one of the cards in S8 above?
A: Because unlike in S8, these cards can also be used for things other than Attack or Block—things like
Move or Influence, things that don't have to be enacted immediately. See S1 above.779
S17:—Strong Effect—Where the strong effect of the Circlet says "as if acquired through a Level Up", it
does not mean that you have to wait until the end of your turn before you can use the newly gotten Skill.780
S18:—Shield Mastery—Both Blocks must be used together in one composite Block. And choosing Ice
Block 2 plus Fire Block 2 does not give you Cold Fire Block 4: it gives you Ice Block 2 plus Fire Block 2—
both, neither, or one of which might be efficient against the current attack. For combining different types of
Blocks, see Combat S4.781
S19:—Resistance Break—As with Taunt discussed in S15 above, I'd allow a player to reduce one ene-
my token's armor twice, or the armor of two separate enemy tokens once.782
S20:—Puppet Master—One enemy that you defeat in the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase, Block Phase,
or Attack Phase can be used against a different (group of) enemy tokens during the Attack Phase.
Cross References: Bonds of Loyalty S4; Tome of Relearning S2.

through impassable terrain, including spaces occupied by Rampaging Enemies and fortified sites. You also do
not provoke Rampaging Enemies when you use the Druidic Staff to move. [This latter point isn't explicitly stat-

S3:—Readying Units—You may not "Ready a Unit" or "Heal 3" during Combat. See S5 below.

Endless Bag of Gold
# Q1:—Timing—Can you play this Artifact to gain 2 Fame, thereby gaining a level and acquiring a new
command token, and then use the Influence to buy a new Unit to place under that command token?
A1: No. Level ups happen during your End-of-Turn sequence, so you haven't yet leveled up. (The only
exception to that strict sequence occurs when you gain a Unit as a combat reward at a RUINS.)788
Q2:—Unspent Points—Can you use the Bag anytime, not spending the Influence, simply to gain Fame?
A2: Yes, as it doesn't say you have to be Interacting with the Locals. See Influence S1.789

black mana token, not the gold. See Miscellaneous S10.

Golden Grail
# S1:—Fame—The wording on this card is a little awkward. With the basic effect, if you use the Heal 2 to
remove a Wound from a level-II Unit, you gain 2 Fame, since this card refers to "each Healing point" you use,
not to each Wound you remove. If you use another Healing effect together with the 2 Heal points of this card to
heal a level-III or -IV Unit, you also gain 2 Fame.790
Horn of Wrath
# S1:—Timing of Wounds—Wounds are gained immediately. So you can play Horn in the Attack Phase,
gain a wound, then pair Power of Pain with that Wound to create a combined Horn-plus-Power Attack of 7.791
S2:—Timing of Wounds—With the strong effect, you announce how much extra damage you'll be add-
ing to the Attack before you roll any dice.
Mysterious Box
# S1:—Effect—This Ultimate Edition Artifact allows you to reveal an Artifact and use the Mysterious Box
as that Artifact. Then "if [you] did not use [the] Mysterious Box, put [the Mysterious Box] back [into] your
hand; if you placed [the Mysterious Box] on a Unit as a banner, discard [the Mysterious Box]".792


[PAGE 090]
p. 90 Artifacts
S2:—Usage—793
(a) If you use the Box for the basic effect of a revealed Artifact, discard the Box at the end of your turn.
(b) If you use the Box as a Banner on a Unit, (which you do by placing the Box itself on that Unit), you
discard the Box—i.e., "the Banner"—at the end of your turn.
(1) For using the Box as the Banner of Command, see S1 under that topic.
(c) There is no separate strong effect: if you use the revealed Artifact for its strong effect, you throw
away the Box.
(d) If you don't use the revealed Artifact at all, you put the Box back into your hand.
And no matter what you do, the revealed Artifact always goes to the bottom of the Artifact deck at the
end of your turn.
Q3:—Timing—Do you reclaim the Box before drawing up to your hand limit, or after?
A3: Return it to your hand at the same time that you discard your played cards, during End of Turn step
3b, before drawing back up to your hand limit.794
S4:—Minimum Turn—Playing the Box and then taking it back into your hand on the same turn does not
in itself meet the requirements of "having played at least one card". See Minimum Turn S1.
S5:—Playing it Sideways—You can play the Box sideways the same as you could if it were any other
Artifact in your hand. But you don't get the +1 Fame if you do so and the Box gets discarded afterwards.795
S6:—Timing—When you play the Box from your hand, its effect merely activates your ability to use it
as the revealed Artifact. But when you actually do use the Box as that revealed Artifact, you must follow all of
the normal timing rules that would otherwise apply to your playing that revealed Artifact from your hand.796
Shield of the Fallen Kings
# S1:—Ambush—See Ambush Q7.797
Soul Harvester
# S1:—Timing of Rewards—The crystal reward(s) for killing enemy tokens are gained immediately on
killing them. Accordingly, you could then immediately use that crystal in attacking another enemy token, even
during the same sub-phase of Combat. (Note: you can't gain a crystal for killing a summoned MONSTER.)798
S2:—Timing—You cannot play the basic effect during the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase (unless pair-
ing it with, say, the black-mana ability of the ALTEM MAGES: see Bow of Starsdawn O5). The same restriction
generally applies to the strong effect, except that you could play the strong effect during the Ranged-and-Siege-
Attack Phase, forego the Attack 8 entirely, and still gain crystals for any enemies defeated during the Ranged-
and-Siege-Attack Phase.799
O3:—VOLKARE—You do not gain a crystal for killing VOLKARE with the strong effect of Soul Harvester
because you aren't directly killing him.800
Sword of Justice
# S1:—Timing—The doubling of physical attacks with the strong effect applies only during the Attack
Phase, not during the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase. The loss of physical resistance, though, applies to the
whole turn. If you break the Sword during the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase, the doubling of physical attacks
will still happen when you get to the Attack Phase.801


[PAGE 091]
Artifacts p. 91
S2:—Timing—If you know you're going to be knocked out during the Assign Damage Phase, you are
allowed to break the Sword before then and so enable all of your Units and Skills to double their physical at-
tacks during the Attack Phase. Contrarily, the basic effect cannot be played before the Attack Phase (unless cou-
pled with, say, the black mana effect of ALTEM MAGES: see S6 below and Soul Harvester S2).802
S3:—All Attacks are Affected—The strong effect does double physical attacks from Units and Skills in
addition to those from Deed cards.803
Q4:—Attack Points Must Be Combined—Must the Attacks gained through the basic effect of the Sword
be used together in one large Attack, or can they be used separately against different enemies?
A4: They must all be used together in one combined Attack against one group of enemies.804
That isn't
the case, though, with the strong effect where Attacks from separate deed cards, Units, and Skills can be dou-
bled and used against different targets.
S5:—Fame—You gain +1 Fame for every enemy you defeat this turn, by any means, including any de-
feated before you played the Sword. Summoned MONSTERS and VOLKARE (Soul Harvester O3) do not count.805
S6:—ALTEM MAGES—The strong effect of the Sword cannot be combined with the black-mana ALTEM
MAGES ability (to allow all Attacks to become Siege Attacks) because the strong effect of the Sword specifically
limits its effect to the Attack Phase, when there are no Siege Attacks. However the other combinations are per-
mitted: all physical attacks doubled in the Attack Phase by the strong effect of the Sword can be made into Cold
Fire Attacks by the ALTEM MAGES; and all Attacks gotten through the basic effect of the Sword can be made into
either Siege Attacks (see Bow of Starsdawn O5) or Cold Fire Attacks by the ALTEM MAGES.806
S7:—Combining Effects—If you've played the strong effect of Ambush (+2 to the first Attack), and the
strong effect of the Sword (double all physical Attacks), and then play the basic effect of Rage (Attack 2), the
final strength will be 2 x (2 + 2) = 8 Attack points, which must all be used against a single group of enemy to-
kens. The Sword doubles the final result of this combination because the "double . . . all" phrasing indicates it is
applied last. A second Rage card that is played after this point will also be doubled and can be used against a
different (group of) enemy target(s).807
S8:—Wound Cards—As always, Wounds cannot be discarded when using the basic effect text that says,
"discard any number of cards."808
S9:—Arcane Immunity—The strong effect of the Sword does not remove the physical resistance of ene-
mies with Arcane Immunity.809
Cross References: Combat S5; Miscellaneous S12; Ambush Q8; Deadly Aim Q4.
Tome of All Spells
# S1:—Mana Costs—The strong effect permits a player to forgo paying both the basic-color mana and the
black mana. Any additional cost, though, such as with Mana Thunderbolt, still has to be paid (as would also be
the case when playing the basic effect of Mana Bolt). See Blood of Ancients S6.810


[PAGE 092]
p. 92 Artifacts
S2:—General—When you cast a Spell from the Spell Offer, treat that Spell as if it substitutes for the
Tome. For instance, if you've played Ambush, and then you cast Exploding Shield through the Tome, Exploding
Shield will count as a played Fire Block and can meet the requirements of Ambush.
[Some players argue that you didn't actually play Exploding Shield, and thus it doesn't meet the require-
ments of Ambush. They argue that Magic Talent works with Ambush because Magic Talent uses the phrase
"play one Spell card", unlike the Tome which uses the phrase "use the basic/strong effect of a Spell." I think this
is parsing the text too finely, for in either case—Tome or Magic Talent—you're casting a Spell from the Spell
Offer as if it were in your hand.]
A similar argument allows Ambush to apply to an Advanced Action card put into play through the strong
effect of Blood of Ancients.811
S3:—No Healing Effects during Combat—If you use the Tome during Combat, you cannot cast a Heal-
ing spell like Restoration or Rebirth.812
S4:—Discarding an Artifact—An Artifact has no color: discarding it is of no use.813
S5:—Magic Talent—You can contrive to cast a specific Spell twice from the Spell Offer: once with
Magic Talent and once with the Tome.814

Skills p. 93
Skill Tokens
# S1:—Timing—Skills can be used only during your own turn unless the Skill description explicitly says
otherwise (e.g., Motivation). Using a Skill does not count as your Action Phase unless it says so.815
S2:—Single-Use—As you flip a Skill token, you can use its ability only once. For instance Polarization
lets you change one mana token, and one mana token only. Deadly Aim modifies only one card. Regenerate lets
you throw away only one Wound. Leadership can affect only one Unit each turn. Etc.816
S3:—Skills Offer—In general, you may not take one of your own Skills from the Skills Offer.817
S4:—Interactive Skills—Competitive and cooperative Skills that are put into play in the center of the
table always expire on your normal turn order, regardless of whether your Round Order token is face-up or
face-down (e.g., regardless of whether you've participated in a PvP Combat or a Cooperative Assault in the in-
terim). Exception: all Skills expire when a Round ends. See SOURCE Freeze S3.818
S5:—Interactive Skills—You cannot affect other players with competitive Skills either (a) after the end
of a Round has been announced, or (b) after the end of a scenario has been triggered by a game ending condi-
tion being met (e.g., in Full Conquest after the last CITY has been conquered). Skills put into play before the end
of the Round was announced/before the game end was triggered will continue to affect players as usual even af-
ter the end of the Round is announced/the end of the game is triggered. See End of Round S2.
Every Mage Knight has eleven Skill tokens. Of these, two are Interactive: one is competitive
(black helmet icon) and one is cooperative (white helmet icon). Players include only one of these two
Interactive Skills in their playable pool of ten Skills, depending on the scenario.
—In competitive scenarios, you use the competitive Skill. If you're playing with Team Rules, your
teammates ignore the negative effects of competitive Skills.
—In cooperative or solo scenarios, you use the cooperative Skill. In solo games, the secondary advan-
tage accrues to you during your ensuing turn; in cooperative games, it accrues to other players.819
The above applies to all scenarios, even those that make no mention of Interactive Skills.
Where the Team Rules talks about "Interactive Skills", they mean "competitive Skills", for there were no
cooperative Skills when the base game rulebook was written.820
S6:—Interactive Skills—Some Interactive Skills affect other players "during their turn" or "on their
turn," such as Mana Suppression and Mana Exploit. If, while these Skills are in effect, other players partially
attend a PvP combat, they are—strictly speaking—not taking their turn during that combat, and consequently
are not affected by these Skills.
But other Interactive Skills, such as Wolf's Howl (which says "Until the start of your next turn . . ."), do
apply to other players even if only partially attending a Combat. Let the text of the Skill guide you. And note
that when a player participates in a Cooperative Assault or fully attends a PvP Combat, she is taking her turn
ahead of time and so will be affected by Interactive Skills during that Cooperative Assault or PvP Combat.821
S7:—Flip-Back Skills—Three of the Skills in the KRANG expansion have a new ability: Flip Back. These
Skills are Arcane Disguise, Battle Frenzy, and Shamanic Ritual. Once a turn, these Skills can be flipped back to
be face up under a specific condition; they also flip back at the start of each Round.
With Shamanic Ritual, you could use it once, meet the special condition to flip it face-up, and then use
the Skill a second time—all in the same turn. This multiple-use possibility can't occur with the other Flip-Back
Skills seeing as their descriptions explicitly state "once a turn".822
Arcane Disguise (KRANG)
S1:—"X" Reputation—With this Skill, KRANG can even Interact with the Locals while his Reputation is
on the "X" spot of the Reputation track.823

Battle Frenzy (KRANG)
S1:—A Stand-Alone Effect—The Attack 2 or 4 given by this Skill doesn't need to be tacked onto another
Attack: it can be used all by itself as an Attack 2 or 4. The same goes for Day Sharpshooting and other similarly
worded Skills. See Miscellaneous S12.824
S2:—Resting without Wounds—You are allowed to take a Standard Rest even if you have no Wounds,
and can thereby flip this Skill back. See Skill Tokens S7, and Resting Q1 and S3.825
S3:—Flip-Back—If you don't use the Flip-Back ability of a Skill, it will nevertheless still be flipped
back up at the start of every Round.826
Cross References: Miscellaneous S12; Circlet of Proficiency S2.
Battle Hardened (KRANG)
Q1:—Brutal—Against a Brutal enemy, does the damage reduction happen before damage doubling?
A1: No. You double the damage for the Brutal ability first, then reduce the applied damage.827
Q2:—Damage Reduction—What happens if an enemy token has a 4 physical Attack, you don't Block it,
and you assign the damage to your Mage Knight who has an armor of 3?
A2: When an Attack is unblocked it becomes damage. The Attack 4 becomes four damage, which Battle
Hardening reduces to two, and this is then compared to your armor. So in this case you take one Wound.
Notice the difference between reducing damage points and reducing Attacks (c.f., Dodge and Weave). If
the enemy above had had Brutal, the 4 Attack points would've become 8 points of damage, which would've then
been reduced by Battle Hardening to 6, which would've resulted in two Wounds against your three armor. This
is different than if an effect had reduced the enemy Attack by two: in that case there would have been 4 - 2 = 2
points of damage, which Brutal would have doubled to 4, but which also would have left us with two Wounds
against our three armor—the same end result in this case, but through different math.828
S3:—PvP—In Melee, if a BLOCKER'S Mage Knight has an armor of 3, and the ATTACKER plays a physi-
cal Attack of 8, and the BLOCKER decides not to play any Blocks, and if the ATTACKER decides to assign 2x3
damage to the BLOCKER'S Mage Knight and to use the remaining 2 damage to force the BLOCKER to retreat to an
adjacent spot, the BLOCKER could then play Battle Hardened to reduce his outcome from two Wounds to one.829
Cross References: PvP Q1, O12; Circlet of Proficiency S3.
Beguile (BRAEVALAR)
S1:—Fortified Site—When you're in a fortified site, you gain only 2 Influence, not 3.830
Cross References: Miscellaneous S12.
Bonds of Loyalty (NOROWAS)
Q1a:—DUNGEON/TOMB—Can you use this Unit in a DUNGEON/TOMB? A1a: Yes.
Q1b:—MAZE/LABYRINTH—Can you use this Unit along with another in a MAZE/LABYRINTH? A1b: No.
Q1c:—MONASTERIES—Can you use this Unit for burning MONASTERIES? A1c: Yes.831
Q2:—End of Round—If you level-up on your last turn of a Round and choose Bonds of Loyalty, are the
two new Units added to the Unit Offer at that point—or do you delay them till the beginning of the next Round?
A2: They're added immediately, and they will not be replaced in the next Round.832


[PAGE 095]
Skills p. 95
Q3:—If the Unit Dies—If a Unit under Bonds of Loyalty is removed (due to Paralyze), can you recruit a
new Unit under it? If so, do you gain the 5 Influence discount all over again?
A3: Yes. Yes.833
S4:—Circlet of Proficiency—Using the Circlet to play Bonds of Loyalty will result in nothing happen-
ing. See Circlet of Proficiency O4.
S5:—Recruiting—You're allowed to recruit either a silver (regular) or a gold (elite) Unit. Other than the
Influence discount, recruiting a Unit for Bonds of Loyalty is done via the normal recruiting processes.834
S6:—Wounded / Spent—A Bonds Unit cannot be used when it's wounded or when it's unready.835
S7:—Timing—If you're gaining a new Unit as a combat reward, but you don't have an open command
token, you cannot delay taking the new Unit until you level up in the hopes of gaining Bonds of Loyalty.836
S8:—Unit Offer—You aren't restricted to recruiting one of the two Units that get added to the Unit Offer
by Bonds. Similarly, other players can recruit those two Units normally during their own turns. The extra two
Units, if not recruited before the end of the Round, are removed and are not replenished in the next Round.837
S9:—MAGIC FAMILIARS—If you have MAGIC FAMILIARS under Bonds of Loyalty, and you have a crystal,
you are not forced to use that crystal to keep them happy, although they'll still be removed from the game if you
don't.838

PHANA MASTERS S1; HEROES S3.

Deadly Aim (WOLFHAWK)
Q1:—Units—Can this Skill be used to affect Unit "cards"?
A1: No. See Silver Units S5.844
Q2:—Use with Indirect Effects—Does this Skill work with Concentration or Maximal Effect and similar
cards if the composite effect ultimately provides Attack points?
A2: The Skill description reads, "Add +1 to a card that provides any type of Attack . . ." The text effect
doesn't say that the card must provide these points directly, so you can permit those combinations. See Maximal
Effect S13 and Mana Overload S6. But you can't play Deadly Aim on the cards that Concentration or Maximal
Effect empower because you can't interrupt the execution of one effect to play another effect.845
You can't play Deadly Aim on Attack points gotten though Agility. The card Agility isn't providing At-
tack points itself; it's providing an ability to convert Move points into Attack points, an ability that can be used
multiple times. Nor can you play Deadly Aim on cards that will provide the converted Move points. See Miscel-
laneous S9.846
But you can play Deadly Aim with a card played via Magic Talent or Tome of All Spells or Blood of An-
cients because the enabling card is triggering the effect of a secondary card, and so it is giving you Attack
points. See Tome of All Spells S2.847
Q3:—Dodge and Weave—Does Deadly Aim work with Dodge and Weave plus Fire Gem?
A3: Dodge and Weave gives you Attack points, so Fire Gem ("add the Fire element to an attack . . .
from a single action card") can modify a Dodge and Weave attack.
Can Deadly Aim ("add +1 to a card that provides any type of attack") then increase this compounded
Fire Attack? Yes. And you can even play the faction token and the Skill in a later phase than when Dodge and
Weave was played.848
Q4:—Burning Shield / Bow of Starsdawn / Sword of Justice—Does Deadly Aim work with Burning
Shield, or with the basic effect of Bow of Starsdawn / Sword of Justice?
A4: The Fire Attack from Burning Shield is like the Attack of Dodge and Weave (see Q3 above), so yes.
The basic effect of Bow of Starsdawn should be eligible for the +1 of Deadly Aim, whether that Bow At-
tack is 2, or 4, or 6, etc. Similarly for Sword of Justice.849
S5:—Swiftness in Attack Phase—Playing Deadly Aim on the strong effect of Swiftness provides a +2 to
the Attack points in the Attack Phase. See Combat S6.850


[PAGE 097]
Skills p. 97
S6:—Ritual of Pain—Deadly Aim can be played on a Wound card played sideways with Ritual of Pain
to give a total of 3 + 2 = 5 Attack points during the Attack Phase. Deadly Aim works similarly with I Don't Give
a Damn, Power of Pain, Universal Power, Who Needs Magic, and Wolf's Howl.
Cross References: Combat S5; Miscellaneous S12; Maximal Effect S13; Skill Tokens S2; Circlet of Pro-
ficiency S8.
Double Time (TOVAK)
Cross References: Miscellaneous S12.
Dueling (WOLFHAWK)
S1:—General—This Skill isn't used until the Block Phase: if the enemy is dead by that time (having
been killed in the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase), you can't use this Skill on it.
If you apply the Block 1 against an enemy, you don't have to successfully Block its Attack(s) to qualify
for the Attack 1 or the Fame +1.851
S2:—SUMMONERS—If this Skill is used as a Block against a summoned MONSTER, the 1 Attack will apply
in the Attack Phase against the SUMMONER.852
[Remember that with SUMMONERS, the attack itself isn't a separate
enemy: see Combat S23. Nevertheless, some players don't allow the 1 Attack to apply to SUMMONERS. See fur-
ther discussion in Taunt O2.]
S3:—Assigning Damage—You're still considered to be "assigning damage" to a Unit even if the Unit
has a matching Resistance and enough armor to avoid receiving a Wound from the attack. See Combat S35.853
S4:—When No Block is Needed—If an effect like Whirlwind prevents an enemy token from attacking,
there's no Block Phase for this enemy, so Dueling cannot be used against it. If an effect like Swift Reflexes re-
duces an attack to zero, that attack is considered to have been successfully blocked, but you may still play addi-
tional Block points against it and thereby use Dueling to gain an Attack 1 against it during the Attack Phase.854
Cross References: Combat S23; PvP Q1; Miscellaneous S12; Circlet of Proficiency S9; Howl of the
Pack S3; Spirit Guides S2.
Elemental Resistance (BRAEVALAR)
Q1:—Brutal—When used against an enemy that does Brutal damage, does the damage reduction occur
before doubling it for being Brutal?
A1: No: you double the damage first, then you reduce the applied damage. See Battle Hardened Q2.855
S2:—Cold Fire Attack—Elemental Resistance reduces a Cold Fire Attack by 1.856
Cross References: Circlet of Proficiency S3.
Feral Allies (BRAEVALAR)
Q1:—Tile Exploration—The exploring cost reduction applies for the whole turn and applies to every tile
that is explored.857
S2:—Arcane Immunity—Enemy tokens with Arcane Immunity can be affected by attack reductions. See
Arcane Immunity S1.858
Cross References: Miscellaneous S12; Circlet of Proficiency S10.


[PAGE 098]
p. 98 Skills
Flight (GOLDYX)
Q1:—Space Bending—If you use Space Bending then Flight, can you move 4 spaces for 2 Move points?
A1: Unlike Space Bending (which produces an effect that stays in play for the entire turn—see Space
Bending Q1), Flight produces an effect that's enacted immediately. So if Flight is used after Space Bending, the
first option for Flight—"move to an adjacent space for free"—becomes "move to a hex that is 1 or 2 spaces
away for free." The second option for Flight—"move 2 spaces for 2 Movement points"—allows each of those
spaces to be "bent", so you can effectively move up to 4 spaces for 2 Move points. When using Flight, you
choose only one of the two Flight options. See Movement O9.859
S2:—Wings of Wind—You can't combine Wings of Wind with Flight because Flight says, "flip this Skill
to move to . . . a space," while Wings of Wind says, "when you play this . . . move". Both are instantaneous ef-
fects.860
And for obvious thematic reasons, Flight will not work with Underground Travel/Attack.
S3:—Obstacles—You are allowed to fly over mountains and lakes and unconquered fortified sites, and
even over or around Rampaging Enemies without provoking them.861
S4:—Revealing Tiles—You can't use Flight to move onto tiles that haven't previously been revealed.
Nor can you use Flight to reveal new tiles.862
Cross References: Movement O9; Resting S4; Mountain Lore O3; Space Bending S4; Underground
Travel O2; Wings of Wind S1; Prayer of Weather S1.
Forked Lightning (BRAEVALAR)
S1:—PvP—In PvP combat, you get Cold Fire Ranged Attack 1 against each enemy you're facing, up to
a maximum of 3 enemies. When assigning damage you must, if possible, assign at least 1 damage to that many
different enemies even if the damage has no effect. So if you're facing a Mage Knight with 4 armor who has two
Units of 3 and 5 armor, and if after Blocking you are left with 5 points of damage to assign, you would only be
able to wound the Unit with 3 armor and then to assign 1 useless point of damage each to the Mage Knight and
to the second Unit.863
S2:—Faction Leaders—Faction Leaders do count as enemies. VOLKARE isn't attacked directly, so he
does not count.864
S3:—Combining Prohibited—If you're facing only one enemy token, you can use only one of the three
Ranged Cold Fire Attack points against it.865
Q4:—Grouping Enemies—If you're attacking three enemy tokens in a group of two and a group of one
(if, say, you're grouping them by their Resistances), can you use Forked Lightning against all three tokens?
A4: You can use Cold Fire Ranged Attack 2 against the group of two and Cold Fire Ranged Attack 1
against the single token, just so long as all of the Attacks are in the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase or all of the
Attacks are in the Attack Phase.866
S5:—Timing—All three Attacks need to be used in the same sub-phase of combat.867
Cross References: Combat O46; Miscellaneous S12; Circlet of Proficiency S2.

Q1:—Adventure Sites—Can this Skill, "Move 1 for each ready unwounded Unit you control, up to 3",

Healing Ritual (ARYTHEA)
Q1:—Closest Opponent—How do you determine which Mage Knight is closest?
A1: Count the distance on the map, in hexes. The movement cost of hexes, whether or not those hexes
are passable, or whether a tile for those hexes has yet to be placed, is immaterial: magic travels in a beeline.871
S2:—Wounds—If you have more than one Wound card, you can decide to get rid of only one.872
Q3:—Wounds—If you discard only one Wound card, can you decide whether it goes to the Wound deck
or to the closest Mage Knight?
A3: Yes. [The effect doesn't explicitly demand that it go into the hand of another Mage Knight, though a
few players argue that it must.]873
Q4:—Timing—Is this Skill "Healing"? Can you use it during Combat?
A4: Yes: it's called Healing Ritual and has a hand symbol shown on the reference card.
No, as there's a hand symbol on the Skill description card. See Cure O3.874
O5:—End of Round—Can you use Healing Ritual after the end of the Round has been announced?
A5: Yes, and both Wounds would go back to the Wound deck. [Some could argue you can't separate the
"throwing away of a second Wound" from its "going into the hand of another hero". But for most Interactive
Skills, the portion of the effect that harms other players is not coupled to the portion that benefits the skill own-
er, so I feel it's fair in this situation to decouple the throwing away of the second Wound from its destination.]875
Cross References: Minimum Turn S3; Miscellaneous S9; Cure O3.

Inspiration (NOROWAS)
Q1:—Timing—Can this Skill be used to Heal a Unit during Combat?
