Know Your Prey (WOLFHAWK)
Q1:—General—What is an ability? What if, for example, you face the ALTEM GUARDSMEN that has all 3
Resistances? Can Know Your Prey also remove the bonus that a CITY gives?
A1: You can remove:
• Any offensive ability: Assassination, Brutal, Paralyze, Poison, Swift, or Vampiric.
• Any defensive ability: Defense (from TEZLA), Elusive, Fortified, or a Physical/Fire/Ice Resistance
(one of them).888
• Any color of a single attack: Fire or Ice become Physical; Cold-Fire becomes Fire or Ice (your
choice).
So you can use Prey to remove one of the ALTEM GUARDSMEN'S Resistances, effectively reducing their
armor by 7 (since whatever your type of Attack is, it can now be fully effective against them).
—To remove the Fortified ability, the ability has to be printed on the token. Defenders in a fortified site
or behind a WALL are not endowed with the Fortified ability: rather, their location prevents you from playing
Ranged Attacks against them during the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase. Similarly, the Brutal bonus which the
red CITY confers and the Poison bonus which the green CITY confers cannot be ignored by this Skill, as they're
not physically printed on the enemy token.889
—The Summon Attack is not an ability, and thus cannot be canceled. Nor is a Multiple Attack some-
thing you can remove with this Skill.890
—Cumbersome and Unfortified are also Offensive and Defensive abilities, respectively, but usually you
won't want to remove these, barring edge cases like with Earthquake.
—Remember you cannot use Prey on enemies with Arcane Immunity (which is miswritten as "magic
immunity" on the Skill description card), nor can you use Prey on the opposing Mage Knight in PvP combat
(because Mage Knights can never be targeted).891
O2:—CITY Bonuses—If you use this Skill to remove the Ice or Fire element of an enemy inside the Blue
CITY—the CITY that adds strength to Ice/Fire Attacks—does the enemy also lose this +1 or +2 Attack bonus?
Conversely, if you use this Skill to remove the Ice element of an Ice Attack from an enemy in the Green CITY—
the CITY that adds the Poisonous ability to Physical Attacks—does that enemy gain the Poisonous ability?892
[IMO the better approach is to say that enemy tokens receive the CITY bonus as soon as those enemy to-
kens are revealed, and that any changes which occur after that point cannot add or remove a CITY bonus, so the
answers to the above questions are no and no. See Resistance Break S2 and CITIES S7 (where the armor bonus
from the white CITY is the first thing to be added).
The opposite approach is to add and remove the bonuses as the abilities and Resistances of the enemy
tokens change, in which case the answers would be yes and yes. Prey changes the values of the enemy tokens
themselves, which is what the CITY bonuses modify—so in this view it's almost as if starting over again with a
new token draw. One of the playtesters sides with this view.]


[PAGE 102]
p. 102 Skills
O3:—PvP—How does Know your Prey work in PvP combats?893
[Opinions vary. I'd disallow it against Attacks coming from a Mage Knight, either directly from Deed
cards or from Skills, for I like treating Mage Knights as if they have Arcane Immunity—per PvP Combat O8.
On the other hand, allowing Prey to "remove one element of one attack" could be viewed as affecting the At-
tack, not the Mage Knight himself—if it weren't for the "not applicable to arcane immunity" symbol on the
Skill. When using Prey against Units, I'd let it remove one ability of one Unit or affect one attack of one Unit.]
Q4:—Summoned MONSTERS—Can you use this Skill to remove an offensive ability from a MONSTER that
has been summoned by an enemy token that itself has Arcane Immunity (e.g., DRAGON SUMMONER)?
A4: The summoned MONSTER replaces the SUMMONER during the Block and Damage phases, so if the
summoned MONSTER itself doesn't have Arcane Immunity, it is a fair target for this Skill during those phases.894
Cross References: PvP O8, O10; CITIES S5.
Leadership (NOROWAS)
S1:—Base Ability Required—This Skill boosts the abilities that a Unit already has. You can give +3
Block only to Units that already have a Block value. And it applies to only one Unit per turn. See Miscellaneous
S12.895
S2:—The Attack Phase—During the Attack Phase there is no difference between Attacks, Ranged At-
tacks, and Siege Attacks, so if you use Leadership during the Attack Phase you get +2 regardless of what type
of Attack is shown on the Unit. See Combat S6.896
If you have CATAPULTS (Siege Attack 3/5/5), and you have Leadership, you can't use it on them in the
Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase (because they are Siege Attacks, not Ranged Attacks); however you can use
Leadership to raise their Attack in the Attack Phase.897
Q3:—Call to Arms—Can you use Leadership with a Unit activated by Call to Arms?
A3: Sure. [Some players question this play, but it seems legitimate to me.]898
S4:—Different Types of Blocks—If Leadership (Block +3) is applied to an UTEM GUARDSMEN'S Block 4
that counts twice against Swiftness, the +3 is also doubled against Swiftness so the combined Block ends up
being a Block 14. Similarly, if the Block +3 is applied to an inefficient Block, the +3 will also be halved.899
Cross References: Combat S6; Miscellaneous S12; Call to Arms O11; Bow of Starsdawn Q4, O5; Circlet
of Proficiency S11; Skill Tokens S2.
Mana Exploit (TOVAK)
S1:—Timing—If a player takes a Wound from Mana Exploit while paying a green mana to power In
Need, the newly gained Wound does give the player an additional 2 Influence from In Need.900
Cross References: Skill Tokens S6; Polarization S3.
Mana Overload (TOVAK)
S1:—Units—For the purpose of this Skill, a Unit is not "a card". Preferably the Skill description should
have said "played card" or "Deed card". See Silver Units S5.901
S2:—Powering Cards—When this Skill is in the center, and you play the basic effect of Pure Magic or
Mana Bolt, and you pay a mana to get the desired result, the mana that you pay is not considered to be "power-
ing a card" and therefore the +4 bonus from Mana Overload does not kick in.902


[PAGE 103]
Skills p. 103
S3:—Powering Cards—If you use mana of the indicated color to power a card—and that card doesn't
give Influence, Move, Attack, or Block—then the Mana Overload Skill stays in the center of the table until
someone does use a same-colored mana to power a card that gives those types of points. See S6 below.903
O4:—Cards with Multiple Effects—What happens if you use Mana Overload to give you +4 with a card
that gives both Block and Attack points (like Burning Shield)—which ability gets the +4?904
[No one could argue against your using the +4 with the first points that end up getting used, although the
wording seems to allow you to add the +4 to any one of the specified point types that you gain from the card.]
S5:—Use Not Required—The owner of the Mana Overload Skill doesn't have to use the mana he gains.
And if he doesn't, he's still allowed to put this Skill into play.905
S6:—Indirect Effects—A card which only indirectly provides Influence, Move, Attack, or Block points
(Into the Heat, Concentration, Maximal Effect) does not trigger the +4 special effect. Similarly, the strong effect
of Blood of Ancients doesn't trigger Mana Overload no matter what color mana is on the Mana Overload token:
Blood of Ancients doesn't give Move, Attack, or Block points itself, so the red mana used doesn't meet the re-
quirements, and the Advanced Action card you trigger isn't being directly powered by a mana of any color.906
Cross References: Concentration Q1; Maximal Effect S13; Polarization S3.
Mana Suppression (KRANG)
S1:—General—This interactive Skill applies to mana no matter what form that mana is in: the first time
a player uses a mana die, expends a mana token, or expends a mana crystal of a basic color, she must pay one
extra mana (in whatever form she wishes) to do so. If she uses a gold mana, it's treated as whichever basic color
she uses that gold mana for. The extra mana must be paid immediately: she can't use mana to power an effect
that creates the extra mana that she needs to pay the cost of Mana Suppression.907
S2:—Multiple Turns—If a non-KRANG player takes two turns in a row (say, by using the Tactic The
Right Moment or by using the Spell Time Bending), turns that fall in between the turn in which KRANG plays
Mana Suppression and KRANG'S next turn, then that player would be subjected to Mana Suppression twice!908
Cross References: Skill Tokens S6.
Master of Chaos (KRANG)
S1:—Start of Round—If you didn't use Master of Chaos in your last turn of the previous Round, and
you're going first in this Round, you can draw your hand and your Tactic card before you decide whether to
move the shield token ahead, similarly to how Motivation works: see Motivation S6.909
S2:—Cost—You don't pay the marked mana color to use this Skill: the mana colors are present only to
allow a mana die to randomly select the initial ability.910
Cross References: Turn Start S1; Miscellaneous S12; Circlet of Proficiency Q12.
Motivation (several)
S1:—Solo Games—In solo games, you're considered to have the least Fame of any player. You're also
considered to have the most Fame. So if you use Motivation in solo games, you gain the mana (or the Fame).911
S2:—Timing—You can use Motivation during your own turn, even during Combat. And any Wounds
drawn from your deck this way do not contribute toward reaching the knockout threshold.912


[PAGE 104]
p. 104 Skills
S3:—Draw Deck Empty—Even if you have fewer than two cards in your draw deck, you're still allowed
to play Motivation. It does not, however, cause you to reshuffle your draw deck: if your draw deck is empty,
you get no card draws but you can still get the mana or Fame bonus, etc., if you qualify.913
S4:—Knockout / Paralyze—See Combat Q22.
S5:—Multiple Motivation Skills—Some players take the text literally and do not play another Motiva-
tion Skill "until the end of their next turn", yet the wording is likely simply meant to prevent players from draw-
ing more than two cards in a turn from Motivation Skills.914
Either way, the restriction resets at new Rounds.
S6:—Start of Round—At the start of a Round, you cannot use your Motivation Skill until after everyone
has drawn their Tactic card.915
Cross References: Turn Start S1; Mana Q8; Combat Q22; PvP Q1; Influence S8; End of Turn S10; Mind
Read S2; Skill Tokens S1; Master of Chaos S1.
Nature's Vengeance (BRAEVALAR)
S1:—Increased Attack Strength—The owner of this Skill isn't affected by the increased Attack strength
of enemy tokens: that adverse effect applies only to other players. And the +1 increased Attack strength applies
only during the Block Phase (as verified by the co-designer of TEZLA); it doesn't apply when players are assign-
ing damage. For enemies with multiple attacks, the strength of each attack is increased by one.916
S2:—Combining Effects—When an opposing player who is under the competitive effect of this Skill
faces PROWLERS (Armor 3, Attack 4, Fame 2) and uses the strong effect of Dodge and Weave to "reduce one en-
emy attack by 4", then during the Block Phase the PROWLERS will have an attack of 1. If this reduced attack of 1
goes unblocked, then no damage will be assigned during the Assign Damage Phase per S1 just above—but the
attack will not count as having been successfully blocked.917
S3:—SUMMONERS—Nature's Vengeance cannot target a SUMMONER enemy token, but it can target a sum-
moned MONSTER. For the Skill owner, that MONSTER gains -1 to one of its Attacks and all its Attacks become
Cumbersome. Against a DRAGON SUMMONER, only one of the summoned MONSTERS would be affected.918
O4:—Arcane Immunity—Attacks from enemy tokens with Arcane Immunity can gain Cumbersome.
[Other players rule oppositely: see the endnote references.]919
Cross References: PvP O14; Combat S25.
Night Sharpshooting (TOVAK)
S1:—DUNGEON / TOMB—If used in a DUNGEON/TOMB during the day, it provides Ranged Attack 2.920
Cross References: Combat Q2; Miscellaneous S12.
On Her Own (WOLFHAWK)
S1:—Weak—As Skills go, On Her Own feels weak. One possible enhancement would be to give it a
base of two Influence, plus an additional 2 Influence if you do not recruit any Unit for Influence this turn.
Cross References: Miscellaneous S12.

Skills p. 105
Polarization (ARYTHEA)
S1:—Black / Gold Mana—You're permitted to take a depleted dice from the SOURCE and turn it into its
opposite color. The abilities and restrictions on gold and black mana, however, still apply if you change the die
into one of those two colors. If the die was depleted, you still reroll it at the end of your turn.921
S2:—Die Must Be Used—You cannot change a die in the SOURCE with Polarization if you won't be us-
ing that die (e.g., you can't change a die simply to be able to re-roll it at the end of your turn).922
S3:—Gold Mana—Using gold mana is "the same as using mana of the desired color . . . for all purpos-
es." If we apply this to Polarization, then using a black mana as a gold mana during the day requires you to then
use that gold mana as a basic color mana. See Mana S5. So the original black mana should be considered to be
the basic color mana it ultimately gets changed into (red, blue, white, or green), including when applying Skills
like Mana Exploit and Mana Overload to that mana's use.923
S4:—Mana No Matter the Form—You can use Polarization on mana no matter its form: crystal, mana
token, or SOURCE die (assuming you're otherwise allowed to use the SOURCE die). You then must use the
changed mana!924
S5:—Mana Pull—You cannot interrupt the execution of one effect to execute a different effect. At night
you can use Mana Pull to gain access to a gold mana from the SOURCE and then (having finished with Mana
Pull), use Polarization to turn it into a black mana. This is fine—just so long as you immediately use that black
mana (see Mana S5). What you can't do is then go back to Mana Pull and try to use the newly created black
mana as if it were a "mana of any color".
Nor would the opposite order work: if you take (as your one-allowed free mana) a gold mana from the
SOURCE, and then use Polarization to change it into black mana, you now have to use that black mana. In any
event it's no longer in the SOURCE, so playing Mana Pull at this point will have no effect on it.925
Cross References: Skill Tokens S2; Invocation S3; Who Needs Magic Q1; ALTEM MAGES Q3.
Power of Pain (ARYTHEA)
Q1:—Timing of Wounds—Can you take a Wound from a single enemy with Multiple Attacks, then use
that Wound with Power of Pain and use it to Block a second Attack from the same Enemy?
A1: No, because all blocking occurs in the Block Phase, and only after all blocking is done does the As-
sign Damage Phase occur.926
S2:—Not Cumulative with Similar Skills—You can't combine Power of Pain with Universal Power on
the same Wound card. A sideways Wound card augmented by Power of Pain won't work with Universal Power
as it's now +2, not +1—and because you can't interrupt one effect to execute another. Similar arguments hold
for I Don't Give a Damn, Who Needs Magic, and Wolf's Howl. See Who Needs Magic S2.927
S3:—Knocked Out—If you begin the Combat Phase with no Wounds, then take a Wound during com-
bat, then use Power of Pain to discard that Wound, then take more Wounds until you have in your hand one
Wound less than your hand size, you'll still be knocked out despite your having discarded the early Wound.928
Cross References: Minimum Turn S2; Blood Rage S2; In Need S1; Rush of Adrenaline O4; Banner of
Protection Q1; Circlet of Proficiency S16; Horn of Wrath S1; Deadly Aim S6; Ritual of Pain S2; Universal
Power S3; Wolf's Howl S1.

p. 106 Skills
Prayer of Weather (NOROWAS)
S1:—General—This Skill affects the movement cost of terrain. Flight ignores the cost of terrain, so
Prayer of Weather has no effect on Flight. And note that Prayer of Weather doesn't change the cost of explor-
ing tiles—but it does change the cost of entering CITIES.929
S2:—Order of Effects—Prayer of Weather, when used against other players, applies after all other ef-
fects that affect the Movement cost of terrain. This ruling is from the game designer. So if you play Frost
Bridge when Prayer of Weather is in effect, you pay two Move points to enter swamps: Frost Bridge reduces
the cost to one, then Prayer of Weather (for players hurt by this competitive Skill) raises it by one. See Move-
ment S10.930
Cross References: Movement S10.
Puppet Master (KRANG)
S1:—General—The only properties of "kept" enemy tokens that still apply are their Attack(s), armor,
and elemental types. See S5 below. Arcane Immunity no longer protects the enemy, and the higher armor from
Elusive doesn't apply. That said, you can collect enemy tokens that have Arcane Immunity, and you can use
Puppet Master effects against enemy tokens that have Arcane Immunity.931
S2:—SUMMONERS—If you want to use Puppet Master to Block with a previously collected enemy token
that had the summoning ability, you use the dead SUMMONER'S armor. If you want to use the dead SUMMONER to
make an attack, you must reveal a randomly Summoned MONSTER (two MONSTERS, if the dead enemy token is a
DRAGON SUMMONER): from this point on you can no longer use Puppet Master to attack with a different dead
enemy token (since new information has been revealed); and when this sub-phase of combat ends, the dead
SUMMONER token will have to be discarded regardless of whether or not you were able to make good use of its
attack. But you can wait until after you have drawn the Summoned MONSTER(S) to decide which enemy token(s)
or group(s) of enemy tokens you'll use the dead SUMMONER'S attack(s) against.932
Another fact to note is that if you wish to combine a dead SUMMONER'S Attack(s) with the black-mana
ability of an ALTEM MAGES Unit, you must first decide how you'll be using the ALTEM MAGES's ability: to gener-
ate Cold Fire Attacks during the Attack Phase, or to generate Siege Attacks during the Ranged-and-Siege-At-
tack Phase. Only after making that decision do you draw the Summoned MONSTER token(s): you don't draw the
MONSTER token until actually making the Summon Attack, and you can only use the Summon Attack in the
Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase if you're committed to turning its plain Attack into a Siege Attack.
S3:—Multiple Attacks—When enemy tokens have Multiple Attacks, you can apply them all against the
same enemy or split them among different enemies. Whichever you choose, they must all be used during the
same Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase (if using them, say, in concert with the ALTEM MAGES's ability as dis-
cussed in S2 above), or they must all be used during the same Attack Phase.933
S4:—Faction Leader—A Faction Leader can be taken once it is fully defeated, but you must use its At-
tacks and armor as they are at level 1.934
S5:—Elements—If the enemy token has a Cold Fire Attack, the Attack you gain is also a Cold Fire At-
tack. If the enemy token has both Ice and Fire Resistance, the Block you gain is a Cold Fire Block.935
If the ene-
my has Physical Resistance, it doesn't affect Puppet Master's Attacks or Blocks.936
S6:—Sites—Enemy tokens are not Units, so you can use them without restriction whenever in DUN-
GEONS/TOMBS, or MAZES/LABYRINTHS, and whenever you burn MONASTERIES.937


[PAGE 107]
Skills p. 107
S7:—Accumulating Enemies—You may, with Puppet Master, accumulate more than one enemy token,
but you can only use this Skill once a turn, either to collect one enemy token or to expend one enemy token, not
for both. Other than that restriction, you get to decide when (or if) to use this Skill.938
S8:—First Use—If you gain this Skill through an End-of-Turn level up, you can't use it during that
same turn to collect an enemy token that you killed earlier during the turn because your turn at this point is offi-
cially over, making the Skill unusable. See Skill Tokens S1, and End of Turn S2.939
S9:—Attack Points—The Attacks you gain are not Ranged Attacks nor Siege Attacks: they're just "At-
tack points", so they ordinarily can't be used during the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase. See S2 above.
Puppet Master Attack (or Block) points can be played all by themselves: you don't need to combine
them with Attack (or Block) points from other cards or Units—see Miscellaneous S12.940
Cross References: Miscellaneous S12; Circlet of Proficiency S20.

Regenerate (BRAEVALAR , KRANG)
S1:—Wound Required—You can't simply pay a red (or green) mana to draw a card: you must also

S2:—Black Mana Permitted—At night you can pay a black mana to throw away a Wound. You cannot,

Shapeshift (BRAEVALAR)
S1:—Cold Toughness—If TOVAK somehow gains Shapeshift, the strong effect of Cold Toughness can
only give 5 Move or 5 Ice Attack—never more, because Shapeshift works only with "fixed amounts of Move,
Attack, or Block."953
S2:—Concentration—The bonus from the strong effect of Concentration is not a fixed amount, so Con-
centration doesn't work with Shapeshift.954
S3:—One with the Land—Despite the word "fixed" in the Skill description, you can use Shapeshift on
the Block that BRAEVALAR gets with the strong effect of One with the Land and turn that into a Move or an At-
tack. The word "fixed" is to prevent Shapeshift from being used with Concentration, or from benefiting from
variable amounts of Block depending on the target, such as with Cold Toughness.955
S4:—Basic Action Card—The "Basic Action card" phrase is easily overlooked: it limits this Skill to use
with those Action cards that a Mage Knight begins the game with (or possibly takes with Mind Steal).956

SOURCE Freeze (GOLDYX)
S1:—Using Mana Dice—If SOURCE Freeze is in play, you're denied your usual one free die from the
SOURCE, but you can still play cards like Mana Draw to take "additional" mana dice from the SOURCE.957
S2:—End of Round—If SOURCE Freeze is in play when a Round ends, all of its effects immediately
cease, including the effect which allows the owner to receive a crystal on his next turn.958
S3:—Timing—On GOLDYX'S next turn after having played this Skill, he doesn't have to retrieve it at the
start of his turn, but can manipulate the SOURCE dice through other means—if he's able—and so influence what
color crystal he ends up with "on that next turn". And note that a gold dice does not represent any basic color.959

Skills p. 109
SOURCE Opening (GOLDYX)
S1:—Solo Games—In solo scenarios, on the first turn in which you put this Skill in play, you reroll a
mana die. On your next turn, you may use a second mana die from the SOURCE, and if you do so you also gain a
crystal of that color. You may then decide to re-roll this second die at the end of the turn or leave it as is.960
S2:—Cooperative Games—In cooperative scenarios, the crystal you gain comes from the bank, not
from the player who uses the extra die.961
S3:—Re-rolling the Die—You decide whether or not to re-roll this mana die before any of the other
mana dice in the SOURCE are re-rolled. All mana dice should be re-rolled at the same time.962
S4:—Extra Mana Die—The other player can use "an extra die from the SOURCE" before he uses his free
mana die from the SOURCE, and he can do so even if he never uses his one free mana die from the SOURCE.963
S5:—Gold Mana Die—You can't use a gold die as "an extra die of a basic color", because it isn't.964
Spirit Guides (KRANG)
S1:—Any Type of Block—The "Block of any type" gives +1 to the Block of a card you play, or to a Skill
that provides Block, or to the Block value of a Unit you use to Block. See Combat S5.965
S2:—Diplomacy—If you play Diplomacy, which allows you to use Influence points as Block points,
you are permitted to couple Spirit Guides with those Influence-Block points to increase the Block even though
the Block in question isn't coming directly from a card, but rather from Influence. That also applies to the strong
effect of Diplomacy which creates Ice Blocks or Fire Blocks.966
S3:—Timing—Since the text of this Skill contradicts the general rule of "you have to generate Block/At-
tack points at the moment you use them," the Skill text prevails (as it does with Burning Shield, Dueling, etc.),
so you immediately generate a Move point (that you can use in the Movement Phase of your turn if you generat-
ed it then; or in the Action Phase of your turn if you generated it then), and the +1 Block that you gain can be
used any time during your Block Phase.967
Cross References: Combat S5; Miscellaneous S12; Circlet of Proficiency S14.
Taunt (WOLFHAWK)
Q1:—Arcane Immunity—Can you use Taunt to reduce the Attack of an enemy with Arcane Immunity?
A1: Yes. You cannot reduce its armor, though.968
O2:—SUMMONERS—You can use the Attack -1 against a summoned MONSTER. I believe you should also
be able to use the other option—Attack +2, armor -2—against a SUMMONER and its summoned MONSTER, espe-
cially if you accept the view that that SUMMONER is "attacking you with the attack of the summoned MONSTER
token" (per Combat S23) and if you picture Taunt as angering the SUMMONER into exposing himself more dan-
gerously in an effort to direct his summoned attack against you more ferociously.
[Other players rule oppositely, arguing that the armor reduction won't affect the SUMMONER for it isn't
"the same enemy". Or they rule that the Attack effect and the armor effect are inextricably linked together as a
single simultaneous effect, and as such it can't affect the SUMMONER while the summoned MONSTER is present.
In my view, though, the Taunt effect is composed of two sub-effects, both resulting from a longer-last-
ing activity, one sub-effect taking hold during the Block Phase and the other taking hold during the Attack
Phase. See Dueling S2, Howl of the Pack S2, and SHOCKTROOPS O9 for effects that face similar questions. In
this view, the wolf's howl is lingering in the air, WOLFHAWK'S dueling prowess is sticking in her enemies' minds,


[PAGE 110]
p. 110 Skills
the SHOCKTROOPS are continually slamming into their opponents, and the ongoing taunting is still baiting the
SUMMONER out from behind his defenses.]969
Q3:—Timing—If you use Taunt to increase an enemy's attack and reduce its armor (e.g., if the enemy's
armor goes from 5 to 3), can you kill it in the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase with a Ranged Attack of 3?
A3: No. As stated in the Skill description, it has to be used in the Block Phase. So during the Ranged-
and-Siege-Attack Phase the enemy will still have its full armor. And note that you cannot increase the enemy
token's Attack after you have blocked it.970
Q4:—Possess—If you play Taunt to "increase one attack . . . and reduce [the] Armor of the same ene-
my", can you then play Possess to use the increased attack against another enemy token?
A4: No. Taunt negates the Armor reduction "if that enemy is prevented from attacking", and Possess
clearly says, "One enemy does not attack." So whether the first half of the Taunt effect (increasing one attack by
2) applies is wholly irrelevant: the enemy in this situation never attacks (per Possess). Rather, with Possess, you
gain an Attack equal to whatever is printed on the enemy token.971
Cross References: Circlet of Proficiency S6, S15.
Universal Power (GOLDYX)
S1:—Sideways Cards—In all other respects except its strength, a card played sideways for use with Uni-
versal Power only grants those things that a sideways card can grant: Influence points, or Move Points, or non-
elemental Block points, or non-elemental non-siege non-ranged Attack points.972
S2:—Not Cumulative with Similar Skills—You cannot play more than one of I Don't Give a Damn, Uni-
versal Power, Who Needs Magic, and Wolf's Howl on the same sideways card. See Who Needs Magic S2.973
S3:—Not Cumulative with Similar Skills—Universal Power does not stack with Power of Pain: see
Power of Pain S2.974
S4:—Depleted Dice—You cannot use depleted dice from the SOURCE (i.e., you cannot use a black mana
during the day or a gold mana at night) to place on a sideways card.975
S5:—Black Mana—If you add black mana at night to a sideways card, that card gives only +3, even if
that card is a Spell card.976
S6:—Artifacts—Artifacts have no color, so they only ever give +3 with Universal Power.977
Cross References: Deadly Aim S6; I Don't Give a Damn S1; Power of Pain S2; Who Needs Magic S2;
Wolf's Howl S1.
Who Needs Magic (TOVAK)
Q1:—Mana Dice—If you use Who Needs Magic and have not used a die from the SOURCE yet, you get
+3 instead of +2. Can you then use a mana die later in your turn?978
A1: No.
[The Skill reads, "If you use no die from the SOURCE . . . " Two further questions arise: (1) Is this sen-
tence—by using the singular declension of "dice"—referring solely to the single free die that all players get
from the SOURCE every turn, or does it prevent the player from using any dice in the SOURCE whatsoever? (2)
What does "use" mean: to directly use a mana die as "mana", or to make any use whatsoever of any mana die?
(1) The argument based on the singular declension of the word "dice" to argue that this Skill refers only
to the free mana die every player gets each turn seems tenuous at best, especially given the phrase "no die".


[PAGE 111]
Skills p. 111
(And if that's what was intended, why not have written more clearly, "If you don't use your free mana die from
the SOURCE . . . ") There seems to be general agreement that you can't use any dice that are in the SOURCE.
(2) Regarding the second question, I take a narrow interpretation of the word "use" to mean "actually
using it for mana". If you search the base game and Lost Legion rulebooks, you'll see that in every place where
you encounter the phrase "use a die from the SOURCE", it's clearly talking about using a mana die as mana.
Therefore, when abiding by the "if you use no die from the SOURCE this turn" clause, I would disallow
the basic effects of Mana Draw and Mana Pull, but I would allow their strong effects, seeing as the strong ef-
fects are only "manipulating" the mana dice and then providing mana tokens that match those manipulated dice.
I would also allow both effects of Mana Storm (as these are also manipulations), but of course prohibit the play-
er from using the (optional) "three extra dice" that're allowed through the strong effect. I would also prohibit the
use of Polarization on mana dice in the SOURCE, per Polarization S2.
Some players, in regard to question (2), prohibit any use whatsoever of the dice in the SOURCE.
a) I don't know, though, how they treat the last sentence of SOURCE Freeze—"gain one crystal of
any basic color represented in the SOURCE"—if they would consider even this to be the use of a
mana die. Nor do I know if they would allow a player to voluntarily forgo this SOURCE Freeze
crystal in order to allow that person to play Who Needs Magic for its +3 effect at some point later
in the turn. See Basic Action Cards O5.
b) And what about Orb of Twilight: with this faction token you're not really using a mana die,
you're simply changing a black die to gold or a gold die to black—does that count as "use"?
c) Another question that arises is, if an effect requires manipulation of SOURCE dice, are only those
portions of the effect voided, or does Who Needs Magic prohibit the playing of the entire effect
in the first place?
To avoid having to render additional rulings in these (and possibly other) cases, it seems advisable to
limit the meaning of the phrase "use a die from the SOURCE" to the way the rulebook itself uses the phrase: that
is, to "use a mana die as [pure] mana"—see Mana Q10.]
S2:—Not Cumulative with Similar Skills—You cannot play I Don't Give a Damn, Universal Power,
Who Needs Magic, and Wolf's Howl on the same sideways card: these Skills specify a replacement effect ("+2
instead of +1"), not an addition effect ("increase it by +1"). So even if you could play a second Skill of this cate-
gory, it would merely replace—not complement—whatever effect the first Skill gave you.979
S3:—Mana Steal—If you use Who Needs Magic for its +3 effect, you can still use a mana die that is sit-
ting on the Tactic card Mana Steal. See Mana Steal Q1.980
Cross References: Circlet of Proficiency S16; Deadly Aim S6; I Don't Give a Damn S1; Universal Pow-
er S2; Wolf's Howl S1.
Wolf's Howl (WOLFHAWK)
S1:—Not Cumulative with Similar Skills—This Skill reads, "One card played sideways gives +4 instead
of +1." This skill cannot be combined with Universal Power or Power of Pain or I Don't Give a Damn or Who
Needs Magic on the same sideways card. See the discussion in Power of Pain S2 and Who Needs Magic S2.981

[PAGE 112]
p. 112 Faction Tokens
Faction Tokens
# S1:—Drawing Faction Tokens—Faction tokens are drawn during the End-of-Turn sequence at the same
time as other rewards from combat. Since faction tokens may only be used during your turn (unless otherwise
noted), you'll have to wait until your following turn to use any faction tokens that are earned during this turn.982
S2:—Exchanging Faction Tokens—If you discard a faction token for 1 Fame or 3 Influence, you must
do so while Interacting with the Locals, on a space where there are Locals. You can discard more than one Fac-
tion Token at the same time, even Faction Tokens with identical names, since you're "discarding" them, not
"using" them.983
S3:—When Partially Attending—When you partially attend a PvP Combat or an attack by VOLKARE,
you may not use Faction Tokens unless the Faction Token text states otherwise.984
Amulet of Reawakening
# S1:—No Discard Pile—If your discard pile is empty, you can still play this faction token, which will
then have the sole effect of increasing your hand limit by one during your next draw phase.
# S2:—Random Card is Known—Your Discard Pile is known information, so you can look to see which
card was randomly placed at the bottom of your Deed Deck. See Miscellaneous S2.
Cloak of Shielding
# S1:—Arcane Immunity—You can use the Cloak even if you're being attacked by an enemy token with
Arcane Immunity.985
S2:—Additional Effects—Brutal takes effect prior to Wounds being assigned, and therefore doesn't fall
under the "additional effects" phrase. Poison, Paralyze, and Vampiric, however, do fall under "additional ef-
fects" and so do not take effect due to the Wound that was avoided. If more than one Wound would've been
caused by the attack (other than the extra Wound caused by Poison that was avoided per the previous sentence),
those additional Wounds hit with their full and usual effect.986
Fire Gem
# S1:—Elements—This token adds an element to an Attack or Block. For example a Rage card played
with red mana is Attack 4; using an Ice Shard with it will make it an Ice Attack 4. Using a Fire Gem combined
with the basic effect of Ice Shield (Ice Block 3) will make it a Cold-Fire Block 3.987
Q2:—From a Single Action Card—Can Fire Gem / Ice Shard be used with Diplomacy or Agility?
A2: No. Also note that Spell cards and Artifact cards are not Action cards.988
S3:—From a Single Action Card—Attack and Block points from the strong effect of Concentration
paired with another Action card are not attacks or blocks gotten "from a single action card". Neither are Attack
or Block points gotten through the play of Maximal Effect. However Improvisation is acceptable with Fire Gem
because the second card is not the playing of an additional card but the paying of a cost.989
O4:—Ranged and Siege Attack Phase—Can Fire Gem/Ice Shard add Fire/Ice to Ranged Attacks or
Siege Attacks?
A4: Into the Heat does apply to Ranged and Siege Attacks (see Into the Heat S2), so Fire Gem/Ice
Shard—which has similar wording—should also.990


[PAGE 113]
Faction Tokens p. 113
O5:—Cards Played Sideways—Can Fire Gem/Ice Shard be used on cards played sideways?
A5: You could argue this either way, but I think the better answer is "no" so as to be consistent with
Ghostly Elixir S3. In any event, you can't use them on a Spell or Artifact or Wound played sideways because
those aren't "Action cards"—which is another good reason to disallow its use on cards played sideways.991
Cross References: Deadly Aim Q3.
Ghostly Elixir
# S1:—Timing—Like the black-mana effect of ALTEM MAGES, the Attack-transforming effect of Ghostly
Elixir should be played before the Attack which it will affect because, without it, you cannot play plain Attack
points during the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase.992
Q2:—On a Single Action Card—Can this ability be used on Movement points changed into Attack
points via the Agility card?
A2: No, because it is not an "attack on a single Action card", but rather an attack coming from the pool
of Movement points. See Fire Gem Q2 and S3.993
S3:—Cards Played Sideways—The effect text of Ghostly Elixir restricts its use to Basic Action cards
and Advanced Action cards, so it's likely not intended for use with Deed cards played sideways since that would
necessarily have to also include Spell cards and Artifact cards. This ruling was confirmed by the designer.994
Cross References: Vial of Toxin S2.
Healing Herbs
# S1:—Timing—You can use Healing points after combat is over, but you draw Faction Tokens during the
End-of-Turn sequence, so you can't use Healing Herbs in the same turn that you draw it. See End of Turn S2.995
S2:—Healing—The Faction Token explanation card shows this Token as being an Influence effect, but
that's an error: it should show a hand symbol not a head symbol. You cannot use this token during Combat.996
Ice Shard
# Cross References: Fire Gem S1, Q2, S3, O4, O5.
Mystical Map
# S1:—Timing—This token must be used during the Movement Phase of your turn. You don't, though,
have to actually move anywhere, or even have Move points available to be used.997
Orb of Twilight
# S1:—Day / Night—Either option is available during the day; either option is also available at night.998
Cross References: Who Needs Magic Q1.
Tome of Relearning
# S1:—Choosing Skills—The Skill you trade into the Skill Offer may already be flipped. You may even
trade in a Competitive or Cooperative Skill that's currently in play in the center of the table. Either way, the
Skill you take in exchange comes to you unflipped. You're also allowed to take one of your own Skills from the
Common Skill Offer. And this Skill swapping is permanent: you don't unswap it after one use.999


[PAGE 114]
p. 114 Faction Tokens
S2:—Complex Example—You could potentially use a once-a-turn Skill, trade it in for a different Skill
through the Tome of Relearning, re-learn it with the strong effect of Circlet of Proficiency (receiving it in its
unused state), and then use it again, all in the same turn.1000
Vampiric Chalice
# S1:—Knocked Out—This token does not prevent you from being knocked out.1001
S2:—Wound Card—The Wound cards to be discarded must come from your current hand; they cannot
come from your draw pile or your discard pile, nor can they come from your Units.1002
Vial of Toxin
# S1:—Applicability—The bonus can be added to any source of physical Attack, including an Attack by a
Unit or an Attack from a Deed card played sideways.1003
S2:—Sequence Matters—During the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase you cannot play Rage (Attack 2),
then Vial of Toxin (+3 Attack), then Ghostly Elixir to generate a Ranged Attack of 5, for you cannot play plain
"melee" Attacks during the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase. Instead you would have to play Ghostly Elixir,
then Rage, giving you a Ranged Attack of 2, and then Vial of Toxin (+2 Ranged Attack), making it a Ranged
Attack of 4.1004
S3:—The Attack Phase—During the Attack Phase, there's no difference between Attacks, Ranged At-
tacks, and Siege Attacks. So if you use Vial of Toxin during the Attack Phase, you gain +3 to the Attack regard-
less of what type of Attack it is being applied to—Ranged, Siege, or a plain Attack—just so long as it's a phys-
ical Attack. See Combat S6.1005
S4:—Attack No Longer Physical—As stated on the description card, if after playing Vial of Toxin anoth-
er effect makes the attack elemental (i.e., non-physical), then the bonus from Vial is lost.
Cross References: Combat S6; Miscellaneous S12.


[PAGE 115]
Silver Units p. 115
Silver Units
# S1:—Unit Offer—After the first core tile has been placed onto the map, when dealing Units into the
Unit Offer at the start of every Round, you always begin with an elite (gold) Unit and then alternate. You begin
with an elite Unit each Round, even if it's been an odd number of Rounds since a core tile was first placed.1006
S2:—Recruiting Units—In general, if an effect allows you to recruit a Unit but doesn't say that you need
to be at a VILLAGE or KEEP or CITY or MAGE TOWER or inhabited space, then you don't need to be there.1007
Q3:—Recruiting Multiple Units—Can you recruit multiple Units during the same Interaction? E.g., can
you recruit a Unit, and then activate that new Unit to get enough Influence points to recruit a second Unit?
A3: Yes. Yes. You get the things you buy (or acquired by some card effect) immediately—only combat
rewards are delayed (they're received in the End-of-Turn sequence step 5). New Units can be used immediately,
to generate more Influence or Healing points, etc. And unless you're directed otherwise, new Spells and Ad-
vanced Actions go on top of your draw pile—though you might be able to draw them on the same turn that you
acquire them if you play an effect like Motivation that allows you to draw cards. Artifacts, though, are always
gained during the End-of-Turn sequence. See Influence S8.1008
S4:—Disbanding Units—You can only voluntarily disband a Unit (exception: MAGIC FAMILIARS S6)
when you're recruiting a new Unit; and even then, only when you don't have a free command token.1009
S5:—"Any Card"—When a rule or effect refers to "any card" (or generically to "a card"), it's referring to
any non-Wound card currently in your hand (e.g., one not yet played). It's never referring to a Unit "card". An
effect that gives you "+X to the next card you play" does not affect the abilities of Units.1010
S6:—Playing against Fire and Ice Resistance—Any non-Attack/non-Block effect from a Unit ability
that's powered by red or blue mana has no effect against enemies with Fire or Ice Resistance, respectively. This
restricts the abilities of only two Units: DELPHANA MASTERS and AMOTEP FREEZERS.1011
S7:—Unit Ability Icons—The Shield symbol that's printed to the left of some Unit effects indicates that
Block points are provided (if it's solid black), or that other Block-phase effects are provided (if it's hollow).
[Some players also see the Shield symbol as a restriction: see O8 just below.]1012
O8:—Unit Ability Icons—There's a curious statement in the Walkthrough. When discussing the abilities
of Units, it says "Each of these options has an icon showing when you can use them." This rule—if it is a rule—
isn't mentioned anywhere in the rulebook, and the icons themselves are never explicitly defined.1013
By compar-
ison, the gold icons on Deed cards that limit when they can be played are described in detail in the base game
rulebook and are expanded on in Lost Legion. The contrast in the treatment of these two sets of icons is star-
tling. Consider also that the Walkthrough often uses looser language to describe the workings of the game, and
consider further how the designer talks about the Walkthrough booklet itself: "[T]he right use of [the Walk-
through is] 'Read it, play the first scenario, and then never open it again, unless you want to explain the game
to other people.' There is nothing more for you [in it]. All [of] the rules are in [the] Rulebook." 1014
Many play-
ers, therefore, don't see these icons as limiting when Unit abilities can be played: they see the Walkthrough sen-
tence quoted above as merely saying that the icons which precede the Unit abilities are simply visual cues, flags
seen at a glance to show you which Unit abilities might be germane to the current Phase.1015
Consider also the third set of icons used in the game: the icons that appear on Skill tokens. These icons
are not defined either, but are clearly meant to visually summarize the Skill effect so that players can be remind-
ed with a glance what each Skill does. The use of each Skill is fully described on the Skill description card:
aside from the flip icons, the icons on the tokens provide no additional information. There are icons on the Skill
description cards themselves that do limit when these Skills can be used: these icons, however, are the same
gold icons that're used on Deed cards.


[PAGE 116]
p. 116 Silver Units
Accordingly, I don't think the Unit ability icons are meant to restrict when those abilities can be used.
[Some players, however, do believe that the Unit icons limit when Unit abilities can be played. In non-
PvP gameplay, these icons affect only two Units: AMOTEP FREEZERS and SHOCKTROOPS. And what these icons do
is prevent those Units from using their armor-reducing ability before the Block Phase begins, meaning that
those two Units can't affect the armor of enemy tokens during the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase. Based on
their artwork, the Units do appear to lack volley capability, a circumstantial point in favor of this view. Players
who treat the Unit icons as a restriction, nevertheless do allow any Unit ability preceded by a bow- or catapult-
icon to be used either in the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase or in the Attack Phase. See SCOUTS Q2.]
S9:—Wounds and Readiness—A Unit has two independent binary states: (a) unwounded or wounded,
and (b) ready or spent. These states exist independently of each other: a Unit can be unwounded and ready; un-
wounded and spent; wounded and ready; or wounded and spent.
A Unit becomes spent when you use one of its abilities; a Unit may become wounded when you Assign
Damage to it (see Combat S34 and Dueling S3). Assigning Damage to a Unit does not make it spent (although
to spend a Unit, not only must the Unit be ready, it must also be unwounded).
You can assign damage to a Unit only once per turn (regardless of whether the "assigning of damage"
results in Wounds being placed on the Unit). Blocking, for instance, is an ability that spends a Unit; Assigning
Damage is not the use of an ability and so does not "spend" the Unit.1016
S10:—Readying Units and Re-use—If a player has PEASANTS and HERBALIST, he could expend the PEAS-
ANTS for 2 movement points, then (if not in Combat) expend the HERBALIST to re-ready the PEASANTS, then ex-
pend the PEASANTS again for 2 more movement points, and then (if in the Movement Phase) use the 4 movement
points to enter, say, a wasteland space. See Movement S1 and Miscellaneous S9.1017
FORESTERS
# S1:—MAZE / LABYRINTH—When you decide to enter a MAZE, any unspent Movement points disappear. If
you want to use the Move effect of FORESTERS to travel through a MAZE, you can do so—but you can't take any
other Units with you. Your FORESTERS become spent, and unless you refresh them (you're not yet in Combat, so
Healing effects are playable), you'll be able to assign damage to them but not use their Block ability. See Com-
bat Q1.1018
Cross References: Movement O9; Miscellaneous S9; Forward March S2.
GUARDIAN GOLEMS
# Cross References: Veil of Mist S5; Circlet of Proficiency S14.
HERBALIST
# S1:—Readying Units—The ability to Ready a level-1 or level-2 Unit applies only to that player's Units.
And, as usual, this effect cannot be used in Combat.1019
Cross References: Resting Q2; Silver Units S10.
ILLUSIONISTS
# Q1:—SUMMONERS / Arcane Immunity / Multiple Attacks—The ILLUSIONISTS Unit's white-mana ability
reads: "Target unfortified enemy does not attack this combat." Regarding this ability:
(1) Can ILLUSIONISTS prevent Attacks from an enemy that has the Summon Attack ability?
(2) Can ILLUSIONISTS prevent Attacks from an enemy that has Arcane Immunity?
(3) Can ILLUSIONISTS prevent all Attacks from an enemy that has Multiple Attacks?
A1: This ILLUSIONISTS Unit's ability interacts with numerous other rules, some of which involve ques-
tions that have puzzled players on several levels, so be sure to see the related discussions in this Compendium.


[PAGE 117]
Silver Units p. 117
(1) Summon Attack: yes, if played before the summoned MONSTER token is drawn, in which case no
enemy MONSTER will be Summoned. See Combat S23, and also S2 below.1020
(2) Arcane Immunity: no. Thematically this effect is tied to white mana, so it's a magical effect and
therefore negated by Arcane Immunity.1021
So DRAGON SUMMONERS, despite the answer given
immediately above, are immune to the white-mana ability of ILLUSIONISTS. See Arcane Immunity
S1 and Combat Q24.
(3) Multiple Attacks: yes. This Unit ability clearly states the "enemy does not attack".
S2:—Types of Attack—There are 5 types of Attack: Physical, Fire, Ice, Cold Fire, and Summon Attacks.
All of these can be canceled by an ILLUSIONISTS Unit. So when you're facing a SUMMONER, you can use ILLU-
SIONISTS at the very beginning of the Block Phase just before you draw a MONSTER token: at that point you de-
cide either to (a) draw the MONSTER token and face it, or (b) exhaust the ILLUSIONISTS Unit, pay a white mana,
and cancel the summon attack. Once a MONSTER token is drawn, it's too late to cancel its attack: the MONSTER
will attack. See Combat S23 and Banner of Fear Q6.1022
S3:—Demolish—It is permissible to use Demolish to ignore the site fortifications of an enemy token,
and then to have an ILLUSIONISTS cancel the Attack of that enemy token.1023
Cross References: Combat S10, S23, S34 (enemy token); PvP Q1; Call to Arms S7; Earthquake S4.
MAGIC FAMILIARS
# S1:—Recruiting—The first two paragraphs under "MAGIC FAMILIARS" (Lost Legion p. 5) are not contra-
dictory. What they are conveying when taken together is that (a) if you recruit FAMILIARS in a MAGICAL GLADE,
your Reputation does not apply, and (b) you cannot gain FAMILIARS through card effects or combat rewards: you
must recruit them using Influence only. See also DELPHANA MASTERS S1. You are permitted to recruit both FA-
MILIARS Units that come in the game during the same Action Phase if otherwise able to do so: i.e., if they're both
present and you pay the combined Influence points and mana costs.
The "or use them" phrase in the second paragraph applies to using FAMILIARS while they're in the Unit
Offer: see S2 just below.1024
S2:—Gaining MAGIC FAMILIARS by other Means—The parenthetical sentence in the second paragraph
under "MAGIC FAMILIARS" (Lost Legion p. 5) is saying that Call to Arms and Call to Glory have no effect on FA-
MILIARS. FAMILIARS also cannot be claimed as a combat reward, and they cannot be recruited through the strong
effect of the Banner of Command. See S1 above and DELPHANA MASTERS S1.1025
S3:—Gold Mana—MAGIC FAMILIARS can be paid with gold mana, but you must immediately specify
which basic color you're using that gold mana as, and indicate this by placing a basic mana token on them.1026
Q4:—Start of the Round—In paying MAGIC FAMILIARS a crystal, when is "at the start of the Round"?
A4: Immediately before choosing Tactics.1027
And you do have to pay FAMILIARS a crystal each Round,
as clearly stated on the FAMILIARS Unit card.1028
S5:—Unit Abilities—MAGIC FAMILIARS, once recruited, always have exactly one mana token on their
card: no more, no less. You don't pay mana when you use an ability of the FAMILIARS, but if the ability you use
has a mana token of the matching color sitting on the card, then that ability receives the additional bonus speci-
fied. For instance, if there's a blue mana token on the Unit when you activate the Block ability, you'll receive
Block 7. If instead there's a red mana token on the card, you'll receive only Block 4.
When activating FAMILIARS, you may select any of the abilities on the card, regardless of which is cur-
rently enhanced by the presence of a mana token. And the mana on the card is not consumed by this use of its
ability: after using FAMILIARS, if you ready them again by some means, the strengthened ability still applies.


[PAGE 118]
p. 118 Silver Units
The mana on the FAMILIARS card is discarded at the start of the next Round when "you have to pay a
crystal”. When this happens, the old mana is not recovered to your mana token pool or to your crystal inventory,
nor does it remain on the card: it goes back to the bank.1029
S6:—Voluntarily Disbanding—With MAGIC FAMILIARS, you can choose not to pay them a mana crystal
at the start of a Round even if you have crystals in your inventory. If, for whatever reason, you don't pay them a
crystal, the Unit is thrown away (along with any Wounds it has), but there is no other penalty for the player. The
command token immediately becomes available again, just as when a Unit takes a Paralyze wound.1030
S7:—Use Against Resistances—When a MAGIC FAMILIARS unit has a red or a blue crystal on it, the in-
creased Attack points and Block points are considered physical attacks and blocks, and they apply normally
against enemy tokens with Fire Resistance and against Ice Attacks (even if from an Ice Resistant enemy), al-
though the Block points in the latter case will be inefficient against the Ice Attack and be halved as usual.1031

Possess O2 (Enemy Token); Bonds of Loyalty S9.

SCOUTS
# Q1:—Explore Once Only—How many times may you use the "reveal a new tile at a distance" ability?
A1: The wording on the card—"a new tile"—means you can do this once each time you activate the
SCOUTS Unit. And, just the same as always, it costs 2 Movement points to reveal that tile.1032
Q2:—When Resting—The middle SCOUTS ability that lets you look at an enemy token has a Movement
(foot) icon next to it. When Resting, you don't get a Movement Phase, so can you use this ability on a turn dur-
ing which you decide to Rest?
A2: Yes. See Silver Units O8.1033
Cross References: Movement S2, Forward March S2.
SHOCKTROOPS
# S1:—Timing—The first ability (+1 Attack to all other Units) does apply to Ranged Attacks and Siege
Attacks. If activated during the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase, the +1 also applies during the Attack Phase.
You are allowed to play this ability during the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase even if all enemy tokens
are fortified: in this case the "Ranged Attack 1" has no effect (and cannot be delayed for use in the Attack
Phase) but the other part ("+1 to Unit Attacks") does apply. See SORCERERS S2.1034
O2:—Timing—The middle SHOCKTROOPS effect (reduce Armor and one attack) can be played during the
Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase even though the icon to the left of it shows a shield icon. [This is contrary to
how some players play it: see Silver Units O8.]1035
S3:—Minimum Armor—Armor can never drop below 1.1036
S4:—Into the Heat / Call to Arms—Even if Into the Heat or Call to Arms is active, if SHOCKTROOPS re-
duce one enemy Attack by 3, any damage from that Attack must be assigned to those SHOCKTROOPS. Any Unit
wounded while in the Unit Offer still receives a Wound, but since any Unit recruited from the Unit Offer is re-
cruited ready and healthy, when recruited, any Wounds are healed. It's also my opinion that if SHOCKTROOPS are
to be assigned damage, then the play of Into the Heat is not permitted.1037


[PAGE 119]
Silver Units p. 119
S5:—Multiple SHOCKTROOPS—If you have two SHOCKTROOPS and activate them both for the "Range At-
tack 1; all Units get +1 Attack" ability, only the second SHOCKTROOPS will have its Attack increased, and by on-
ly +1. All non-SHOCKTROOPS Units thereafter receive +2 to their Attacks.1038
S6:—Damage Assigned First—If you choose the third ability that says damage from the reduced attack
must be assigned to this Unit, and if the SHOCKTROOPS somehow gets wounded before you can assign that dam-
age, then that damage must be assigned elsewhere, for you can't assign damage to an already wounded Unit.1039
[I also argue that you must assign any damage from that reduced enemy attack to SHOCKTROOPS, and that
you cannot assign other damage to SHOCKTROOPS first.]
S7:—Swift Attack Reduced to Zero—If you use SHOCKTROOPS to reduce an attack of an enemy token to
zero (or less), and that enemy token also has Swiftness, the attack value for purposes of blocking remains zero,
so this attack is still considered to have been successfully blocked.1040
S8:—Affected Attacks—You can reduce physical, ice, fire, and cold-fire Attacks, even from an enemy
with Arcane Immunity: see Arcane Immunity S1. Other enemy token abilities like brutal, swiftness, poison, and
paralyze still apply to the now-reduced attack strength.1041
O9:—SUMMONERS—I allow using the middle ability against SUMMONERS, with both the attack of the
summoned MONSTER and the armor of the SUMMONER being reduced. See Taunt O2.
Cross References: Combat S5; PvP O8; Arcane Immunity S1; Miscellaneous S12; Into the Heat S3;
Shield Bash O3; Call to Arms Q4, S5; Silver Units O8.
THUGS
# S1:—Low Reputation—Even THUGS can't be recruited while your Reputation is on the "X".1042
S2:—Force of Nature—If you use Force of Nature to give THUGS Physical Resistance and want to as-
sign 5 or less damage to them (too little to wound them), you still need to pay them 2 Influence points to allow
that. See Combat S34 and Dueling S3.1043
O3:—Timing of Reputation Adjustments—If you use a THUGS Influence ability before your Action
Phase, your Reputation decreases by one. Does this decrease happen immediately, or at the end of your turn?
A3: Immediately.1044
[Others infer that all Reputation changes are to be delayed until the end of the turn
even if cards don't explicitly say so, but that's not rigorously indicated in the rules.]

UTEM GUARDSMEN
# S1:—Counts Twice—The phrase "counts twice" only means that the Block points are doubled against an
Attack that has Swiftness. It doesn't mean you can use the Block 4 against two different Attacks, such as when
defending against a HEROES enemy token that has both Swiftness and Multiple Attacks.1045

UTEM SWORDSMEN
# S1:—Into the Heat / Call to Arms—If you use Call to Arms or Into the Heat with UTEM SWORDSMEN and
choose its bottom ability, the SWORDSMEN become wounded (though you are not, strictly speaking, "Assigning


[PAGE 120]
p. 120 Silver Units, Gold Units
Damage" to it). Any Unit wounded while in the Unit Offer still receives a Wound, but since Units recruited
from the Unit Offer are recruited ready and healthy, when recruited they are immediately healed.1046
S2:—Paralyze—If you use the lower ability of UTEM SWORDSMEN to Block MEDUSA, the Unit is not
killed due to the paralyze ability: it is only wounded. The Wound the SWORDSMEN receive comes from activat-
ing the Unit during the Block Phase, not from any damage taken from an enemy token during the Assign Dam-
age phase. Similar reasoning applies if you use SWORDSMEN to Block the poisonous MONKS: only one Wound is
assigned to the SWORDSMEN.1047
S3:—Vampiric—Similarly to S2, if you use the lower ability of UTEM SWORDSMEN against an enemy to-
ken with the vampiric ability, the enemy token does not increase its armor value due to the resulting Wound to
the SWORDSMEN.1048

ALTEM MAGES
# Q1:—Affects Units—Does the "make everything a Siege Attack" ability apply to Attacks by Units?
A1: Yes: it makes all Attacks conducted by that player better, even cards played sideways.1049
Q2:—Affects Units and Skills—Does the black-mana ability include Attacks made by your Units too?
And your Skills? And any Attack points that were played before you played the ALTEM MAGES ability?
A2: Yes. Yes. No: retroactive effects rarely apply in MKBG.1050
Q3:—Black Mana—Can you use the top ability of the ALTEM MAGES to create a black mana during the
day, and then take that black mana token into a TOMB/DUNGEON to cast the strong effect of a Spell there?
A3: Yes. In general you can gain black mana tokens during the day—you just can't use them except in
specific situations (such as with Polarization or upon entering TOMBS).1051
O4:—PvP—If you adhere to the alternate interpretation discussed above under Silver Units O8, then the
shield icon before the black mana ability indicates it can be used during PvP when blocking, perhaps allowing
you to use it before the opponent gets a chance to wound the ALTEM MAGES.1052
Q5:—Black Mana during Day—Can black mana enable the bottom ability of ALTEM MAGES during the
day?
A5: In general no, not unless you're standing on the NECROPOLIS or a GRAVEYARD where night rules ap-
ply, or if using the Amulet of Darkness. Remember Units cannot join you in a DUNGEON/TOMB.1053
S6:—Ranged and Siege Attacks—Ranged Attacks and Siege Attacks can be made into Cold Fire Attacks
with the black mana ability. But turning Attacks into Siege Attacks has no effect during the Attack Phase: see
Combat S6.1054
S7:—Plain Attacks—Not only can Ranged Attacks be turned into Siege Attacks during the Ranged-and-
Siege-Attack Phase by the black-mana ability of ALTEM MAGES, but regular Attack points can be too (see Bow of
Starsdawn O5).1055

DELPHANA MASTERS
# S1:—Recruiting—DELPHANA MASTERS have limitations on how they can be recruited (see Call to Arms
S9 and Call to Glory S4). The Unit can, however, be recruited through Bonds of Loyalty, and if it is, then the In-
fluence discount will apply.1058
S2:—Multiple Uses—You have the entire Combat Phase during which you activate the DELPHANA MAS-
TERS to use as many of their abilities as you wish, across multiple sub-phases if you wish. But each ability can
be used only one time each turn.1059
S3:—Multiple Uses—With each ability, you can target the same enemy or a different enemy.1060
S4:—Arcane Immunity—Enemies with Arcane Immunity will not be affected by any of the printed abili-
ties of DELPHANA MASTERS.1061
S5:—Red-Mana Ability—The red-mana effect will destroy an enemy only if that enemy is successfully
Blocked, which means that if it has Multiple Attacks that all of its Attacks must be successfully Blocked.1062
S6:—White-Mana Ability—Arcane Immunity is not a Resistance, so the white mana ability can't cancel
it. See S4 above.1063
And both elements don't need to be present (Fortifications and Resistances) in order to re-
move the single element that is present. See Earthquake S4.1064
S7:—Enemies with Fire/Ice Resistance—Enemy tokens with Ice Resistance are unaffected by the blue-
mana ability; enemy tokens with Fire Resistance are unaffected by the red-mana ability. But if you use the
white mana ability on an enemy token first, it will remove their Resistances and render them susceptible.1065
S8:—Resistance Break—When used with TOVAK'S Resistance Break, you can choose the order. So re-
duce the armor of the chosen enemy token first with Resistance Break, then remove its Resistances with the
white mana ability of DELPHANA MASTERS.1066

SORCERERS
# S1:—Arcane Immunity—Enemies with Arcane Immunity will not lose fortifications. See Expose S1.1070
S2:—Cannot Delay Ranged Attacks—The white-mana ability of SORCERERS also gives a Ranged Attack.
If you use this white-mana ability during the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase (the only phase where it makes
sense to remove fortifications), the Ranged Attack must also be used then. You needn't use the Ranged Attack
against the same enemy token affected by the "loses all fortifications" ability, but if you're going to use the
Ranged Attack, it must be used during the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase.1071
You can also use the white-mana ability during the Attack Phase and gain an Attack (Combat S6), but
stripping fortifications from an enemy during the Attack Phase is unlikely to be of benefit.
The green-mana ability of SORCERERS, (that causes an enemy to lose all Resistances, noting that Arcane
Immunity is not a Resistance), can be helpful both in the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase and in the Attack
Phase. If you activate it during the Attack Phase, you can use the Ranged Attack then, but it's treated as a plain
Attack (Combat S6). But if you activate the green-mana ability during the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase, you
must use the accompanying Ranged Attack in that same sub-phase or forfeit it.
Similar arguments hold for the Spell Expose/Mass Expose. See Combat O46.1072
Cross References: Combat O46; PvP O10; Arcane Immunity S1 (Enemy Token), S2 (Enemy Token);
Expose S1; Mass Expose S1.


[PAGE 123]
Tactic Cards p. 123
Tactic Cards
# S1:—Use Outside of a Regular Turn—There are several situations in which players take actions or even
a whole turn outside of their normal slot (as when Fully Attending a PvP Combat, when joining a Cooperative
Assault on a CITY, or when participating in Cooperative Assaults on VOLKARE or Cooperative Defenses against
VOLKARE), and there are other situations in which players take limited actions (like actions taken during other
players' turns, or when only Partially Attending a PvP Combat, or when Retreating from an Attack by VOL-
KARE). Players may wonder if they can use their Tactic card during these moments.
Several Tactic cards have effects that apply only at the moment that you select the Tactic card: Early
Bird, From the Dusk, Preparation, Rethink, and Great Start. Another Tactic, Long Night, can be used at any
time no matter whose turn it is. And one Tactic—Planning—takes affect during your End-of-Turn Sequence.
There's usually no uncertainty regarding the use of these Tactic cards in the above situations.
Of the remaining Tactics, two of them—Sparing Power and Midnight Meditation—are used "before [the
start of] your turns". The permissible timing for using these Tactics seems very broad: see Turn Start S1 and the
corresponding entries for these two Tactic cards below. You cannot, however, use them during your turn.
The remaining three Tactic cards—Mana Steal, Mana Search, and The Right Moment—have a special
effect that must be taken during your turn. For The Right Moment, see the entries below. With Mana Search,
since you're manipulating mana dice in the SOURCE, it's probably better to permit its use during the first group of
situations listed above but not during the second group of situations. With Mana Steal, you can certainly use it
during the first group of situations, and I would also permit it when only Partially Attending a PvP Combat.1073
Great Start
# S1:—No Discarding—When you select Great Start, you immediately draw two additional cards into
your hand; you do not discard anything. And you do this only once, at the very start of the Round.1074
Long Night
# Q1:—Timing—Can this Tactic be used any time?
A1: Yes. You can use it whenever your deck is empty, even during the middle of drawing cards. And
since the discard pile is usually considered open information, a player has the ability to discover which cards
have been placed into the draw deck: see Miscellaneous S2.1075
Q2:—Timing—Can you use this Tactic during the middle of discarding cards? (E.g., if you want to put
some of your discarded cards into the potential draw stack, but not others?)
A2: No, because the End-of-Turn rules say, "put all cards into your discard pile": it isn't done individ-
ually, one card at a time, so it can't be broken down into sub-parts—it's a single composite action.1076
Mana Search
# S1:—Re-rolling Dice—You must choose how many dice you'll re-roll before you re-roll any dice.1077
S2:—Timing—You have to re-roll the mana dice before you use any dice from the SOURCE, whether the
dice being used are gained through your free once-per-turn mana die or through any other effect or ability. But
this re-rolling of dice is permitted even if you don't plan on using any of them during your turn.
If you roll poorly and end up with no usable dice after the roll, that's too bad: you're stuck with them.1078
Mana Steal
# Q1:—Mana Dice—Does using the mana die from Mana Steal count as using a die from the SOURCE?
A1: No. It's no longer in the SOURCE after you steal it and place it on the Tactic card. So you can use
both it and your free mana die from the SOURCE during the same turn. While the stolen mana die is sitting on
Mana Steal, there will be one fewer die than usual in the SOURCE.1079
Cross References: Who Needs Magic S3.


[PAGE 124]
p. 124 Tactic Cards
Midnight Meditation
# S1:—Timing—You can play this tactic after a player announces he is using Mind Read / Mind Steal, but
before he announces which color he is choosing.1080
S2:—Cards—You choose which cards are reshuffled into your deck: they're not chosen randomly.1081
Cross References: Turn Start S1; Mind Read S2.
Planning
# Q1:—Wound Cards—Do Wound cards count toward the "two cards in hand" requirement that must be
met in order to draw as if your hand limit is one higher?
A1: Yes.1082
S2:—Hand Limit—Planning essentially increases your hand limit by one.1083
S3:—End of Round—This Tactic does not increase your card draws during an ensuing Round.1084
S4:—Cumulative Draw Benefit—The Planning card-draw benefit does stack with other card-draw bo-
nuses from KEEPS or CITIES and from other sources.1085
Preparation
# S1:—Wound Cards—You are allowed to select a Wound card.1086
S2:—Timing—Preparation is used only once: the moment you draw it. For the rest of the Round it does
nothing.1087
Rethink
# S1:—Timing—Rethink is triggered the moment you choose it as your Tactic card, so the only cards you
shuffle back into your deck are the three cards you just discarded.1088
The Right Moment
# S1:—Timing—You must make the choice of taking another turn before you begin your End-of-Turn se-
quence. See End of Turn S5.1089
S2:—Cooperative Assaults—If you play The Right Moment while participating in a Cooperative Assault
that has been proposed by another player, the (only) effect will be that your Turn Order token is immediately
flipped back up again, so that when it's once again your chance to take your turn, you'll be able to take a normal
turn instead of just flipping your Turn Order token up at that time.
If you play The Right Moment while participating in a Cooperative Assault that you yourself proposed,
then after all of the players participating in the Assault have finished their assaults, you get another turn.1090
S3:—End of Game—In many scenarios, after you conquer the final CITY, you're allowed one more turn.
If you delay using The Right Moment until that point, you could play it and get two additional turns instead of
just one. See Scenarios O30.1091
S4:—Full Turn—The turn you gain is a full turn. You finish your present turn the same as always, in-
cluding the entire End-of-Turn sequence. Then you take a whole new full turn.1092
Cross References: Mana Suppression S2.


[PAGE 125]
Tactic Cards p. 125
Sparing Power
# S1:—Timing—When you're attacked by another player (whether you're fully attending or not) or when
you're attacked by VOLKARE (whether fully attending or not), you can use Sparing Power to draw the extra cards
that're stored under it. See Turn Start S1. But if you decide to place another card under Sparing Power immedi-
ately before you're attacked by another player or by VOLKARE, and then decide to only partially attend the result-
ing combat, you cannot use Sparing Power (in either mode) before the start of your upcoming full turn.1093
[The
Tactic card could be read to mean "Once immediately before the start of each of your turns . . .". But that read-
ing would limit the Tactic's use to when fully attending a combat, never when only partially attending. See Turn
Start S1.]
S2:—First Use—You choose one of this Tactic's two effects before the start of every turn until it is
flipped, and this includes during your first turn of the Round. Once you flip this card and put all of the Deed
cards that're under it into your hand, you may no longer place any more Deed cards under it.1094
Cross References: Turn Start S1.

CRYPT WORM

CRYSTAL SPRITES
# S1:—Elusive—The CRYSTAL SPRITES token shows the Elusive symbol, then the Ice Resis-
tance symbol overlapping that, then the Defend symbol overlapping that. This doesn't mean that
these latter traits bear any dependency on the Elusive trait. SPRITES have Ice Resistance even if
both of its attacks are fully blocked and its higher Armor value is negated. The same goes for the Defend abili-
ty: it's present whether or not the Elusive trait has been negated. The three abilities—Elusive, Ice Resistance,
Defend—are each independent of the other two.1095
Cross References: Combat S26, S27.

# S1:—Summons Attack—The DRAGON SUMMONERS draws two MONSTER tokens and uses the at-
tack of each MONSTER token once; it doesn't draw one MONSTER token and use it's attack twice.1096

SHROUDED NECROMANCER
# S1:—Summoning a SUMMONER—The SHROUDED NECROMANCER summons an ORC MARAUDER.
That token should, if possible, come from the NECROMANCER'S faction: the DARK CRUSADERS. If there're
none available, then you could end up drawing an ORC SUMMONERS for the NECROMANCER'S attack, which
in turn will draw a summoned MONSTER to resolve its attack.1101
If the above happens, which MONSTER token type should the ORC SUMMONERS draw? You could
argue this either way, but I'd draw the MONSTER from the DARK CRUSADERS faction, if they're available.

[PAGE 128]
p. 128 General Questions
General Questions
• What Products are Available from Wizkids?
o Mage Knight Board Game (2011): the base game, includes four Mage Knights.
o The Lost Legion (2012): one Mage Knight, new components and scenarios, and VOLKARE.
o KRANG Character Expansion (2013): one Mage Knight.
o Shades of TEZLA (2015): one Mage Knight, two boss enemies, and new scenarios.
o Ultimate Edition (2018): combines the content of the four previous entries, plus five new cards.
o The Apocalypse Dragon (2026): one Mage Knight, one boss enemy, new scenarios.
• How can I tell the Manufacturing Date?
Look under the UPC code on the box. For instance, one print run of Shades of TEZLA shows the code
739W050216. The last six digits correspond to May 2, 2016.1102
• Latest Rules - without the Expansions:
Q: If you don't have the latest printing of the base game or of the expansions, or lack some of the expan-
sions altogether, are you playing by the latest rules and with the latest components?
Short answer: If you own Lost Legion (whichever printing of it you have) then you are playing with the
latest components and by the latest rules.
KRANG adds a new Mage Knight. TEZLA adds a new Mage Knight and some additional compo-
nents and rules—but these rules apply only to the new material included in TEZLA. So if you own
Lost Legion but not KRANG or TEZLA, you'll be missing a couple of Mage Knights and some ene-
my tokens and scenarios, but you'll be playing by the latest rules. When you draw an enemy to-
ken or look at a card, you'll be looking at the same enemy token and the same card that players
who own all of the expansions look at, and you'll be playing them according to the same rules.
Long answer: All printings of the Mage Knight expansions are identical to each other except for compo-
nent quality, perhaps different-sized disks for the enemy tokens in early printings, or different color hues
on the backs of some of the cards, etc.
With regards to the Mage Knight base game, the early printings did have some misprints, but those com-
ponents were replaced in Lost Legion. (The rulebook was also updated early on—the newer version hav-
ing a Table of Contents on the first page.)1103
In addition, Lost Legion has a few but important rule
changes that apply to the base game; and some of the base game cards and enemy tokens were rebal-
anced and replaced. So if you don't own Lost Legion, you may be playing with some misprinted compo-
nents, and you will be playing by a few different rules than players who've bought Lost Legion. (Excep-
tion: the latest printing of the MKBG base game now includes improvements from Lost Legion.1104
)
Conclusion: If this bothers you, buy Lost Legion and your worries'll be over.1105
• Latest Rules - without the Ultimate Edition:
Q: If you didn't buy the Ultimate Edition, are you playing with the latest rules and components?
A: If you own Lost Legion, you're good: see the previous entry. There were no component changes in
the Ultimate Edition that affect play, other than the inclusion of five new cards.
The rules were reorganized and you can download them from the Wizkids website, but as for me, I'm
happy with the originals. No text changes were made to the cards.1106
The November 17, 2021 printing of the Ultimate Edition includes larger, more-detailed models for all of
the miniatures (with subjectively superior paint jobs). There are other improvements, too: the colored
enemy dots on the click bases are more decipherable, the card backs and token backs appear very uni-
form in color, there's an improved insert, and there're bigger mana dice.1107


[PAGE 129]
General Questions p. 129
• Playing Correctly:
It's common to get some of the rules wrong during your first plays, but it's difficult knowing which rules
you've messed up. Here're some evaluative questions about your play that might pinpoint play errors. These
questions should all be answered yes except where indicated otherwise.1108
—Are you certain you know the difference between "Throw Away" and "Discard", between "Turn" and
"Round", and between "mana token" and "mana crystal"?
—Are you taking only one mana die (for free) from the SOURCE every turn, and using it only once that turn?
—Are you using only the top half or the bottom half of each Deed card?
—Are you using only one option of your Units, and using each individual Unit only once per Round?
—Are you Blocking each enemy attack separately, losing any surplus Block points?
—Are you shifting excess Attack points to other (groups of) enemy tokens? [You shouldn't be.]
—Are you taking only one Action every turn (e.g., either combat or interacting with the locals), and not
moving or exploring after that Action?
—When you play a mana crystal, do you throw away the crystal after you use it?
—Are you treating black mana as being wild at night? [It isn't.]
—For each attack, are you assigning damage to Units first, and to your Mage Knight last?
—When you assign damage to a Unit with Resistance, are you doing so according to Combat S34 and S35?
—Are you using the Dummy correctly: that is, are you getting only about five or six turns every Round?
—Most Tactic cards are one-use only.
—Many Skills are one-use-a-Round only, and almost all Skills are only usable once during any given turn.
—Are you certain which of your Attacks and which of your Blocks are inefficient, and thus halved?
—At game end, do you have more than 5 Skills or more than about 25 Deed cards? [You likely shouldn't.]
—On your even-row level-ups, are you doing anything other than flipping over the next level token? [You
shouldn't be.]
—Was it difficult moving onto CITY tiles? (That is, were mountains, or lakes, or DRACONUM in the way?) If
not, you may be laying out your map incorrectly. (CITY spaces themselves, contrarily, are always two
movement points to enter.)
—Is there one Site type that you always rush to enter? [If so, you may have the rules for that Site wrong.]
—If you take the Dummy player Skill during a level up, do you restrict yourself to taking only the bottom
Advanced Action card from the Offer?
And remember . . .
—If enemies are in a CITY and those enemies have the fortification ability, they generally cannot be defeated
during the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase even if you use Siege Attacks.
—The white CITY gives +1 armor to all enemy tokens: this bonus is often forgotten.
—In solo play, the two chosen Tactic cards are removed at the end of every Round.
—You cannot claim your own Skills from the Common Skill Offer.
