Copyright
Much of the limited artwork in this file belongs directly or indirectly to WizKids/NECA, LLC, 693 Sweetland Ave,
Hillside, NJ 07205 USA, www.wizkidsgames.com. (Cover & Card Illustrations by J. Lonnee; Additional Illustra-
tions by Milan Vavron; Art/Graphic Design by Chris Raimo; Layout and Typesetting by Filip Murmak).
Fair Use Assessment:
--The artwork used herein is a very small percentage of that used in Mage Knight Board Game, does not con-
stitute the heart of the product, and is lower in quality and in size than the originals. This use should have no
negative impact on the market for the game. The artwork used has previously been published, most of it being
freely available on the WizKids website in a higher resolution, and so already has widespread availability.
--This compilation is no substitute for the full product and is valueless to anybody not having access to the full
product. If anything, this compilation should tend to increase the market for WizKids products by making the
game more accessible to players.
--This compilation is for noncommercial use. In places it quotes from rulebooks that're freely available on
WizKids website, but the quotations are intentionally kept very short.
--This compilation is factual in nature, helping to clear up questions about the rules of mechanic of the game,
much as one might with the rules of chess. Yet this compilation doesn't reproduce existing documents: it adds
something new--an explanation of complex game situations. It is intended to serve as an in-game reference, to
help newcomers learn the game, and to help longtime players handle the complex interactions that might arise.
--All that being said, should WizKids ever request that this compilation be removed for any reason, I ask
BoardGameGeek to promptly do so.
From the U.S. Copyright Office Website (www.copyright.gov):
Fair use is a legal doctrine that promotes freedom of expression by permitting the unlicensed use of copyright-
protected works in certain circumstances. Section 107 of the Copyright Act provides the statutory framework
for determining whether something is a fair use and identifies certain types of uses--such as criticism, com-
ment, news reporting, teaching, scholarship, and research--as examples of activities that may qualify as fair
use. Section 107 calls for consideration of the following four factors in evaluating a question of fair use:
Purpose and character of the use, including whether the use is of a commercial nature or is for nonprofit edu-
cational purposes: Courts look at how the party claiming fair use is using the copyrighted work, and are more
likely to find that nonprofit educational and noncommercial uses are fair. This does not mean, however, that all
nonprofit education and noncommercial uses are fair and all commercial uses are not fair; instead, courts will
balance the purpose and character of the use against the other factors below. Additionally, “transformative”
uses are more likely to be considered fair. Transformative uses are those that add something new, with a fur-
ther purpose or different character, and do not substitute for the original use of the work.
Nature of the copyrighted work: This factor analyzes the degree to which the work that was used relates to
copyright’s purpose of encouraging creative expression. Thus, using a more creative or imaginative work (such
as a novel, movie, or song) is less likely to support a claim of a fair use than using a factual work (such as a
technical article or news item). In addition, use of an unpublished work is less likely to be considered fair.
Amount and substantiality of the portion used in relation to the copyrighted work as a whole: Under this factor,
courts look at both the quantity and quality of the copyrighted material that was used. If the use includes a
large portion of the copyrighted work, fair use is less likely to be found; if the use employs only a small amount
of copyrighted material, fair use is more likely. That said, some courts have found use of an entire work to be
fair under certain circumstances. And in other contexts, using even a small amount of a copyrighted work was
determined not to be fair because the selection was an important part—or the “heart”—of the work.
Effect of the use upon the potential market for or value of the copyrighted work: Here, courts review whether,
and to what extent, the unlicensed use harms the existing or future market for the copyright owner’s original
work. In assessing this factor, courts consider whether the use is hurting the current market for the original
work (for example, by displacing sales of the original) and/or whether the use could cause substantial harm if it
were to become widespread.

A Further Note on Copyright
This Compendium contains some images taken by camera: in I believe all instances these images were either
taken by the author, or the use of these images was granted by the taker of the image.
This Compendium quotes at times from the text of the MKBG Rulebooks or from the cards that come with the
game. This text is freely available for download on the WizKids site or elsewhere on the internet and is deemed
to be both incidental in nature and unable to substitute for the full product. Again, it's standard practice to quote
short rule sections when discussing rule questions pertinent to those quoted rules.
The factual information in this Q&A Compendium was derived in large part from the knowledge shared in rules
questions and discussions held on the relevant game forums on BoardGameGeek.com. As mentioned on p. 2 of
this Compendium, many many players have contributed to these discussions, insights, rule examinations, and
in-play examples, and I 've done my best to provide links to the pertinent forum threads. The information con-
veyed in these threads is primarily factual in nature, that information being posted in a public venue that's freely
viewable by anybody with internet access.1109
Nevertheless this Q&A Compendium is intended to be posted on-
ly on BoardGameGeek—the same internet site where these many, many discussions can still be viewed. I there-
fore ask MKBG enthusiasts to honor this intention: you're welcome to post links elsewhere on the internet to
this Compendium, but please don't post this Compendium elsewhere. Its fit home is BoardGameGeek.
Endnotes
1
This document provides many internet links. But as always when following internet links, beware! Just because a link is given here,
there's no assurance that the link will be free of malware or viruses or whatever. Internet links come and go, and later on might be re-
placed with harmful material. (Also note that sometimes individual posts get deleted from Forums, which can create dead links or can
inject a lot of confusion into the reading of an old Forum thread.)
2
https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/704520/official-rules-faq-thread/page/1. Here are many of the official rulings, 54 pages of them.
A mighty endeavor headed up by Paul Grogan.
3
The BGG MKBG player community has much experience playing this game. In situations where there doesn't seem to be a clear
answer in the rulebook, my advice is to see what the consensus opinion on BGG is (if there is one), and then either accept it or adjust
it. For a list of major questions over which there is still debate, see https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/3108641/debated-rules.
See also https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/2978813/article/41237485#41237485 and
https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/2674502/article/37886385#37886385.
4
In many cases a careful re-reading of the pertinent rules and of the card text, combined with some logical thought and an apprecia-
tion of the writing style and the conventions used throughout the rulebook, along with the official clarifications given in other threads,
can lead you to a single defendable answer. But when you're in the middle of a game, or when you've been chasing your tail trying to
ferret out an answer, or when two different players are arguing oppositely, a single defendable answer can be difficult to establish—so
a summary of these Q&A threads is helpful.
For other questions that've been asked there is no clear answer. Yet even then, many times there's a consensus among BGG players
as to which answer is the most reasonable. At other times, though, the consensus opinion is questionable or there simply isn't a con-
sensus. (If you read many of the referenced BGG threads, you'll see that varying opinions are common.)
So how should players determine the best answer in these cases?
I recommend some practical guidelines: (a) try to follow the rules as written without extending them, (b) whenever several effects
interact and a literal reading feels contrary to the perceived intent or "sense" of the game, then resort to commonsense pragmatism, (c)
don't require players to be expert grammarians in order to play their cards correctly, and (d) prefer interpretations that're more restric-
tive for the player, for this makes the game more challenging and consequently more fun.
This still leaves many gray areas that need answers. Toward that end, this Q&A Compendium takes a stance on every question that
it covers—even those questions over which there's some dispute, all while endeavoring to apply a systematic interpretation of the rules
and a consistent approach to the game. So now, if you're called on to make decisions in the heat of the moment, you at least can see
the decisions that one other player makes after having weighed the evidence, decisions that're impartial with respect to your current
game. In any event, significant differences of opinion are pointed out inside square orange "[ ]" brackets or in "O#:" entries. If you
disagree with any of the judgments that've been made, simply cross them out and insert your own.
The "reasoned" in "reasoned answers" is broadly defined: it encompasses a number of elements, including the reasoned answers that
are to be found in the linked threads and it offers honor to the contribution of all of the posters (as expounded upon in the next end-
note); it also encompasses the idea of a systematic interpretation of the rules as discussed in the previous paragraph of this endnote.


[PAGE 138]
p. 138 Endnotes
5
BoardGameGeek is a special locale where posters contribute in a collaborative effort to help new players and other enthusiasts share
in the enjoyment of boardgames. If I were to attempt to document the contribution of every BGG poster who in any way contributed to
the knowledgebase behind MKBG play—whether through posing thoughtful questions, or framing cogent explanations, or furnishing
useful examples, or compiling pertinent data, or citing relevant rule passages, or contributing to the often far-ranging discussions of
underlying principles, or evaluating the competing interpretations and thumbing the view that seems most reasonable to them—the
lists and citations would be longer than this document itself. And even then, it'd be impossible to untangle the credit that each person
is deserving of. So instead of that, I've tried to reference in these endnotes the chief discussion threads for each topic, where you can
read for yourself what various posters have written. In addition, in the next two endnotes I list many of the standout contributors, al-
though in doing so I'm sure to have missed several names that should've been included. To each of you—both the named and the un-
named—I say thank you, for you've increased my personal enjoyment of the boardgaming hobby and you've become my online
friends.

67
Why is the combat sequence for MKBG asymmetrical?
You attack the enemy at long range.
The enemy attacks you: you can block, but all unblocked damage gets assigned to you.
You attack the enemy at short range.
Imagine designing a game that'll include combat between two opposing groups, group A and group B. And imagine you want to
include a volley phase and a hand-to-hand phase: how would you do it?
You have at least three options:
1) A and B exchange long-range attacks simultaneously and remove damage simultaneously; then A and B conduct hand-
to-hand combat simultaneously and remove damage simultaneously. (The drawback to this approach is having to remember the dam-
age A does to B while figuring out the damage B does to A, and then applying all of that damage afterwards.)
2) Alternatively, you could let A choose one solider to make its long-range attack and apply the damage to B. You then let
one soldier in B make its long-range attack and apply the damage to A. Back and forth you go until everybody has finished all of their
long-range attacks. You then use a similar process for hand-to-hand combat. (But this will take time to work through all of the back
and forth, and you also need a fair way to determine the order of attacks.)
3) Finally, you could let A do all of its long-range attacks and assign the damage to B.
Then let B do all of its long-range attacks and assign the damage to A.
Then let B do all of its hand-to-hand attacks and assign the damage to A.
Then let A do all of its hand-to-hand attacks and assign the damage to B.
(Swapping execution order like this tends to even things out a bit.)
This last option is kind of what MKBG does, but with an added efficiency that speed things up. Abstractly, B's long-range attacks
and hand-to-hand attacks are combined into one (presumably larger) attack:
A does its long-range attacks against B.
B does all of its attacks—both long-range and hand-to-hand—against A.
A does its hand-to-hand attacks against B.
But why do players get to block attacks but enemies don't? Well, enemy tokens do get to block attacks. During the player's long-
range attacks, enemy tokens can use Fortifications to prevent Ranged Attacks, and double Fortifications to prevent Siege Attacks.


[PAGE 141]
Endnotes p. 141
And, notionally, the armor value of enemy tokens could have been increased to account for the impact that separate blocking would
have had.

group. https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/1961477/fighting-multiple-enemies. https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/3198195/mana-

Some players do allow Mage Knights to get knocked out a second time: they specify a number of additional wound cards that it
takes—just one more, or maybe your hand size limit again, etc. Some players even play that the Mage Knight dies if he gets knocked
out twice in the same turn, or if at any time he has more than some pre-specified number of wounds—say if more than half of the
cards in his Deed deck are wound cards.

[PAGE 143]
Endnotes p. 143
Early in the development of the game, the Assign Damage Phase apparently worked differently. The defender's armor reduced the
damage, and only if there were any damage remaining would a wound be given. If the defender also had a thick hide under his armor
(i.e., physical resistance), the attack had to penetrate both "armors" before a wound could be given. This approach was logical, but it
was hard to write a set of rules for selecting which defender—the Mage Knight, or his strongest-attack Unit, or his highest-armor Unit,
or his costliest Unit, etc.—would be assigned damage first. Any algorithm would be complex, yet still open to exploitation by the
player. The solution the designer came to, however, was genius itself: instead of a complex set of rules, he let the player choose
whichever order of assigning damage he wanted to, whichever order was to his advantage. To balance this great flexibility, there
would be a price: the process would—in essence—be reversed. Instead of the armor reducing the attack and then the Unit being
wounded by any remaining damage, now a wound always occurs and only after that is the attack reduced. (The minor exception being
for Unit resistances, where the outer armor has to be penetrated before any damage is assigned.)
https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/697673/article/7445792#7445792. Vlaada Chvátil's discussion on this topic is here. See also
https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/2708948/assigning-damage-multiple-units. https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/3141444/does-
assigning-damage-unit-spend-unit.

141
Some players, though, argue that if you use Sacrifice to generate Ranged or Siege Attack points, that you can't assign them to, say,
a doubly Fortified enemy, and then later use the converted crystals from Sacrifice to play an effect that removes that Fortification. But,
rather, that you must play the two effects in the opposite order, even though that might reduce the number of Attack points created via
Sacrifice. This situation was also discussed in January 2024 on Discord.com. See the relevant rule on p. 8 of the base game rulebook.
Note that the stricter interpretation doesn't necessarily contradict the fourth paragraph under Combat S28.

154
One way out of this quandary would be to treat Mage Knights in PvP combat kind of as if they had Arcane Immunity: you cannot
reduce their armor or cancel their Attacks, but you can reduce their Attacks. And that players shouldn't have to quibble over minor
phrasing variations like "reduce one attack of chosen enemy" vs. "reduce one enemy attack".
Another way out is to simply prohibit any effects (or portions of effects) that mention the word "enemy" from being applied against
Mage Knights, period. With this interpretation, these kinds of effects can only be applied to unwounded Units. (This is the simpler
approach and may sidestep some of the thornier complications.)

187
The Ultimate Edition rulebook, p. 13, covers the specific steps. Step 6b suggests players may play Special Effects and Healing Ef-
fects before moving into the CITY. Step 6c, at first blush, seems to contradict this, but the phrase "no other movement and actions are
allowed" is, I think, simply reiterating the point that you must move into the CITY —that you can't move around willy-nilly, or stay put
and choose a different action. The word "action" here isn't referring to action cards and other effects, but to the action portion of a
player turn.
See p. 5 of the base game rulebook: "Playing a Regular turn has two voluntary parts: movement, and action." (Step 8a is referring
only to the end of the turn and therefore isn't relevant.) The PvP Combat rules aren't of much help because they're vague regarding this
issue (see PvP Combat Q1), but generally players permit the one who is attacked in PvP to play Special Effects before the attack be-
gins (but not Healing Effects—see footnote ‡ in PvP Combat Q1). Consequently, I allow players in Cooperative Assaults to play both
Special Effects and Healing Effects before expending movement points to enter the CITY.
https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/1509501/article/22304147#22304147. https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/2896089/cooperative-
city-assault-can-healing-cards-be-play. https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/3135832/may-knight-play-tranquility-draw-cards-joining-
coo. See also the Turn Sequence diagram on the last page of the Lost Legion rulebook.

201
In defense of this view, see Lost Legion: page 9 says you apply your Reputation "immediately once you start the interaction" and p.
5 says "your Reputation . . . counts in reverse for the entire interaction."
202
An alternate approach—not sanctioned by the rules but which avoids most of the headaches—is to ignore the HEROES or THUGS
modification to the Reputation altogether. The player simply takes her normal Reputation adjustment (whether positive or negative) at
the beginning of her Interaction with the Locals, same as always. But then she modifies the Influence cost of the first HEROES she buys
in a turn by subtracting her Reputation from its cost (a negative Reputation thereby increasing the cost of the HEROES), or of the first
THUGS she buys in a turn by adding twice her Reputation to its cost (a negative Reputation thereby decreasing the cost of the THUGS).
The rule about not buying both HEROES and THUGS during the same Interaction still applies.

https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/3103726/article/42488657#42488657. One could of course come up with a house rule that says
you simply need to remove at least one Action card from your hand every turn. But then you need additional rulings: what if that card
is a wound card—does it still count if removed by a MAGICAL GLADE, or by a Healing Skill? And soon you might have a house rule
that's every bit as complicated as the rulebook rule.
220
Rulebook p. 5, point 4.a. https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/868019/article/10275839#10275839.
https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/1103295/about-end-turn.
I view the "Minimum Turn" rule as saying this: a player has to play a card from her hand every turn, and if she fails to do that
then there's a penalty—she's got to discard a card from her hand. (But remember, in MKBG you can't double use a discard: for in-
stance, if you discard a card to enable the play of Improvisation, you can't also count the same discard for use with the basic effect of
Magic Talent. So it follows that if a Skill causes you to discard a card, you can't count that discard as also meeting the requirements of
the Minimum Turn rule: you didn't "play it".) Additionally—as discussed in Silver Units S5—a wound card, in general, does not meet
the requirement of "play a card/play any card" or "discard a card/discard any card" unless the rules or effect explicitly allow otherwise
(such as with Slow Recovery). For more nuances on Resting and playing cards, see the Resting section just above.

226
https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/2008313/rolling-gold-combat-reward-crystals. An alternate way of achieving a rigorously cor-
rect result is to roll all of the dice at once, in a stream if you can, and then process those dice one at a time starting with the die that
lands closest to you. Whenever you encounter a gold die, choose its color and then reroll all of the dice that landed farther away. (If
there are two dice equidistant from you, begin with the die that's closer to your dominant hand.) Using this approach, three-quarters of
the time you can get all of the dice rolling done in one roll. I suspect the rule was written this way because the base game came with a
limited number of mana dice. In my games I roll for all of the crystals at the same time. This does give me slightly greater control
(since I don't choose the color of any gold outcomes before rolling for the remaining crystals), but usually I'd rather just roll all of the
dice at once and get on with the game.

258
A player plays the basic effect of Refreshing Walk (Move 2 and Heal 1). He then activates the Move 2 ability of FORESTERS, which
also reduces the costs of forests, hills, and swamps by 1). [At this point he has 4 Movement pool points and 1 Healing pool point.] It is
night, so he moves into a desert space for 3 movement points. [Now he has 1 Movement pool point and 1 Healing pool point remain-
ing.] He then plays the strong effect of Regeneration: Heal 2, and Ready a Level I, II, or III Unit. He has only one spent Unit, a
wounded FIRE MAGES Unit (level III): he now both readies it and heals it. [This reduces him to 1 Movement pool point and 0 Healing
pool points.] He now plays the Skill Forward March, giving him an additional 1 movement point. [Now he has 2 Movement pool
points.] With these 2 Movement pool points, he could move into a hills space (reduced to 2 due to the FORESTERS) or reveal a new
map tile. (See also Silver Units S10.)

Lost Legion p. 7 says of combat with VOLKARE: If VOLKARE is not defeated, the attacking player returns to the space he is attack-
ing from for free. If that space is not safe, he is subject to forced withdrawal. Faction Leaders are very similar to VOLKARE, so the


[PAGE 149]
Endnotes p. 149
default guess is that combat versus them is similar to combat versus VOLKARE in regards to what happens if a combat fails to kill
them. Additionally, TEZLA p. 5 talks above cooperative attacks against Faction Leaders and talks above players having to first move
onto their space, the idea seeming to be that Mage Knights cannot coexist in the same space with Faction Leaders in between turns, so
the "being kicked back to the space they moved from" idea seems to be assumed here.

What is written in the referenced paragraph is the consensus opinion and it is how I play, but it goes against common experience:
e.g., if you're out at night, you can see into lit-up spaces just fine, so IMO you ought to be able to see into lit-up RUINS and KEEPS! So
apparently GRAVEYARD night is more like octopus inkiness than it is like any ordinary nighttime absence-of-light. In any event, when
you move out of the GRAVEYARD during daylight, or when the next player starts her turn, then normal daylight rules resume and apply
to the revealing of tokens on RUINS and KEEPS and MAGE TOWERS. https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/2243398/realm-dead-

If you continue your last turn into the next Round, you'll miss out on the 30-point Round bonus for that Round, but gain points for a

chance of scoring more points. (Note that, playing solo or cooperatively, you should be able to see the end of the Round coming and

317
In the Ultimate Edition rulebook, the CAMP tile is drawn as solid green in the scenario description—which is wrong on two counts:
the CAMP tile is a core tile which should be indicated by the color brown, and the CAMP tile should be face-up not facedown when you
start the scenario.
You can tell that the CAMP tile orientation is incorrect on the older VOLKARE'S Quest scenario cards because tiles are always oriented
according to the icons on their corners, so there's only one correct way to place the CAMP tile. If you physically build the map and
follow the tile-placement rules, the CAMP tile will have to be placed with the Lake hex rotated to the lower left, not straight to the left
as is incorrectly shown on the scenario card.
https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/912248/image-mismatch-between-volkares-quest-card-and-sce#.

387
See the base game rulebook, p. 5. In general, you could use the gained mana die to power another deed card or Unit even if you
don't gain any measurable advantage from doing so, such as playing the strong effect of Tranquility to draw two cards when your draw
deck is empty, just so long as you respect the turn sequence—e.g., by not playing an Attack or Block effect unless you're actually in
combat at the time, etc. https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/3055114/mana-pull-question.
388
The rule that you cannot use a SOURCE die if you do not use its mana is there to prevent players from simply messing with the
mana dice in the SOURCE. And although when a player uses the strong effect of Mana Draw, that might be all that the player is doing
—changing the mana dice in the SOURCE—the rule about the player having to use the mana die does not apply here because this is
something different: this is a card effect, and this card effect says nothing about the player having to use the two mana tokens that are
gained. https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/704520/article/7887239#7887239.

427
The intent of Ambush is to give only a certain fixed amount of additional Block points (2 or 4, for the basic or strong effect respec-
tively). Ambush is a base-game card whose text was set before the expansion card Shield of the Fallen Kings even came out, and so it
wasn't worded to handle minor unforeseeable ambiguities like this. Think of Ambush as adding an additional X worth of Block points
to the "entirety" of a card, not separately to each sub-element of a card.
https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/963066/article/12673668#12673668: from the designer.
https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/963066/faqs-lost-legion/page/1.

636
Justification: refer to the text of the white-mana ILLUSIONISTS ability which takes the enemy token's fortification status into account
and then notice how the accompanying shield icon indicates that this ability can be played during the Block Phase, thereby indicating
that an enemy token's fortification status persists at least through the Block Phase. (See Silver Units O8.) [It's my view that an enemy
token's fortification status persists through the entire Combat Phase: once the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase is over, fortifications
simply lose their ability to prevent certain kinds of attacks.] https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/3242248/question-about-fortified-
enemies.

To use Exploding Shield to destroy an enemy, you need red mana plus black mana plus for it to be night plus for the enemy not to
have Fire Resistance or Arcane Immunity. Given all that, allowing the Spell Exploding Shield to destroy an enemy with Multiple At-
tacks by blocking only one of its attacks doesn't seem especially overpowered or open to abuse.
648
The effect says "all fortifications" not just "site fortifications", so it would be affecting the enemy token, not just the site around the
enemy token. For comparison see Demolish S3. Why don't site fortifications get removed by themselves? Because the "loses all forti-
fications" effect of Expose is a single effect, not two effects—and Arcane Immunity renders it an ineffective effect. See
https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/3339242/article/44613820#44613820.
649
https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/1692530/arcane-resistance-expose.
650
The rules don't appear to allow you to create attacks that target nothing: Attack points have to directed at a specific target, unlike
Influence or Movement points which can be generated without being used.

Lost Legions p. 9 seems to argue yes, despite the fact that the similar skill Puppet Master (see Puppet Master S3) lets a player use
multiple attacks against different targets. But with Puppet Master, the effect text itself seems to explicitly permit using these multiple
attacks against different enemy tokens, whereas Possess has no similar wording. I therefore think that multiple attacks gained with
Possess must be combined and used together. https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/1980461/know-your-prey-against-blue-city.

treatment-possess-and-puppet-master-q. Puppet Master came out with the KRANG expansion, which included a FAQ that explicitly
allows multiple attacks to be used against different enemy tokens.

turns "even if they immediately follow each other". https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/734525/article/9264950#9264950.

703
Playing Maximal Effect to reiteratively use Magic Talent to cast Time Bending from the Spell Offer more than once would only
gain you one extra turn, no matter how many times you cast it, because (by Lost Legion p. 9) Maximal Effect is "evaluated as playing a
single effect". And according to the designer, contriving to cast Time Bending twice in a row would break the laws of the universe and
make the universe implode. https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/734525/article/9264950#9264950. Tardigrade Matsuri pointed out this
issue to me.

The preceding sentence in Wings of Wind uses the word "move" in referring specifically to the movement provided by this card
effect, so it makes sense that the succeeding sentence (quoted above) would also use "move" in the same sense. Also see p. 6 of the
rulebook, point 7.c. Accordingly, you may move "over inaccessible spaces", but you have to end that move on a safe space.

728
If you answer yes to question (a)—arguing that new enemy tokens do get revealed as you move because there is no specific rule
specifying otherwise, then you have to answer the remaining questions too.
Question (b): no, you can't change the number of move points you decided to spend (because new information has since been re-
vealed, so you can't change previous decisions).
Question (c): yes, you can change which future spaces you'll move into (because you can change your decisions, back to the point at
which new information was revealed, and nothing on the card insists that you must predesignate your entire movement path).
Question (d): yes, you may move back to a previously traversed space (there's no rule prohibiting this).

Remember to do what the card tells you to do, not what you think should happen. With the Amulets, you may be tempted to think
that day becomes night and night becomes day (like when entering a DUNGEON/TOMB) and that Night Rules apply: but that's not what
the cards say. What happens is that it remains day or night but some new effects apply: you can use gold/black mana; the movement
costs of forests/deserts take on their lower value; and you might reveal garrisons/RUINS. In all other ways the normal rules still apply.

760
Why? Because the base game rulebook, p. 6, in point 7a says that, with regard to Movement effects, you get to apply these in any
order, and I read that to mean that every time you spend Movement points, you can apply them in any order. (E.g., if you have two
Movement effects—A and B—in play, and if you apply them in order A-B for entry into a given space, and if you then play Move-
ment effect C and then move into a second space, I believe you could choose to apply the Movement effects in a different order, may-
be B-C-A if you wish.) And, absent a direct statement either way regarding Attack points, I prefer to permit the same level of freedom
with Attack points that you have with Movement points. https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/3122840/bow-starsdawn-and-altem-
mages.
761
Ordinarily you cannot play Ranged Attack points against Fortified targets during the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase, per p. 8 of
the base game rulebook. (See Combat S13.) However I treat as being permissible those combinations that will become legal when you
do look ahead, such as knowing that you will immediately be turning the interim Ranged Attack points into Siege Attack points. This
is somewhat similar to how Ghostly Elixir works (Ghostly Elixir S1), turning unplayable plain Attack points into Ranged Attack
points—although there I require the enabling power to be played first, as the rule-breaking is more severe: see the endnote to Ghostly
Elixir S1. (Alternate views on this ruling are discussed here: https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/3122840/bow-starsdawn-and-altem-
mages.) Ultimately, though, this is a tricky situation to untangle rule-wise and no argument is entirely convincing. The circumstances
under which this combination would occur, however, are very rare and I favor the fun factor that'll be created if this combination is
permitted.

767
For one thing, Bonds of Loyalty isn't a once-a-turn Skill—it's a one-time-use Skill, so at best you would treat it like a once-a-round
Skill and use it only once. If you were to use it with the Circlet, the player would add two regular Units to the Units Offer. The player
could then, I suppose, recruit a Unit for five fewer Influence points if it uses the Bonds of Loyalty as its command token—but the
player would only be able to keep that Unit through the rest of his turn: the Unit would then have to be discarded during the end-of-
turn sequence for that's when the Circlet's effect ends and the player loses use of the Bonds of Loyalty. As for the two Units that were
added to the Unit Offer, whether they should then be discarded or not is another unknown that must be addressed.
https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/1201817/article/16308039#16308039.
https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/1298299/article/17950437#17950437.

919
See the rule on p. 6 of Lost Legion that allows an attack from an enemy with Arcane Immunity to be stripped of its Swiftness. Since
Cumbersome and Swiftness are both enemy token "offensive abilities" (and are abilities related to the inertia and speed and dodge-
ableness of an attack), and since the rules allow effects to strip Swiftness from the attack of an enemy with Arcane Immunity, other
effects should be able to add Cumbersome to the attack of an enemy with Arcane Immunity.

KRANG FAQ. https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/1234700/puppet-master-summoners-assigning-attack-targets.
Despite the consensus stance discussed in this paragraph, one might argue that a player holding Puppet Master must also designate the
target of any summons attack (naming a single enemy token, or a single group of enemy tokens) before drawing any Summoned MON-
STERS, (a player using a DRAGON SUMMONER having to name up to two targets). And that if a HYDRA is summoned, that the HYDRA
must then use all of its attacks against its predesignated target. And that although a player may afterwards tack additional attacks onto
the summoned attack, he cannot redirect the summoned attack itself. The argument being that if you pull the trigger to see what type
of bullet is coming out of the barrel, that it's then too late to swivel the barrel to point it at a different target. But this argument is ably
countered by pointing out that, according to the rules, players are permitted to delay the picking of which targets will be the focus of


[PAGE 168]
p. 168 Endnotes
enemy SUMMONER attacks (i.e., choosing from among his Unit(s) and his Mage Knight) in both the Block Phase and the Assign Dam-
age Phase until after the MONSTER token is drawn.

The puppet isn't like a Unit. It's not reusable, nor can it be assigned damage. If it helps to envision what's going on, think of the
puppet as a one-time addition to your Attack or Block, with the enemy token representing the value of that onetime addition. There's
nothing there to be killed, and there's nothing there to which you can assign damage.)

945
In MKBG you choose which effects to play and the order in which to play them, and these effects apply to the state of the game as
it exists at the moment that you play them. Once you've played an effect, and then play another effect, this latter effect functions
against the new state of the game, not the previous state of the game.
https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/941467/article/12852864#12852864.

The Lost Legion rulebook, p. 6, last paragraph under "COOPERATIVE SKILLS", when talking about back-to-back turns, says

968
Lost Legion p. 6 says that if an effect reduces an enemy Attack or affects it in any other way, it can be used against the Attack of an
enemy with Arcane Immunity. (If it helps, think of this Skill as a "magically assisted" taunt.) One way to give the Skill Taunt a little
more punch would be to assume the opposite: that it's an entirely non-magical Skill and hence fully effective against enemy tokens
with Arcane Immunity.
969
https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/922723/wolfhawks-taunt-skill-versus-summoners. Read also the endnotes to Combat S23.
Whichever way one decides this question, I think the same answer should also apply to Dueling, Howl of the Pack, Taunt, and SHOCK-
TROOPS. Personally, I believe the prohibition—"as long as the summoned MONSTER is present, no effect can target the enemy that
summoned it"—is primarily meant to prohibit cards like Shield Bash, Ice Shield, and Exploding Shield from affecting SUMMONERS.
970
https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/1789311/circlet-proficiency-timing-using-once-turn-skill-t. As Skills go, Taunt is relatively
weak. A possible enhancement would be to allow it to be used in three different ways—during the Block Phase to (a) reduce one
enemy attack of any enemy by one; (b) reduce the armor of one enemy by one; or (c) increase one attack of an enemy by one while
also decreasing the armor of the same enemy by two.
971
One might argue that I'm being inconsistent: here, I don't allow another effect (in this case Taunt) to affect an enemy token that's
being used with Possess, but with CITY bonuses (see Possess O2), I say the bonus should apply. But I believe my approach is fair be-
cause when Possess is used on an enemy token inside a CITY, the CITY bonus is still active, but the Taunt bonus IMO ends when the
enemy token doesn't attack. See also the orange text in Circlet of Proficiency S14.

988
Influence points and Move points, when accrued, first go into a pool of points that can be spent freely throughout the remainder of
the turn, and so lose their association with any given Action card. (See Miscellaneous S9.) So the converted Attack points / Block
points are not coming "from a single Action card"; they're coming from an Action card (Diplomacy or Agility) and from their respec-
tive Movement / Influence point pools. https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/2245044/using-fire-gem-ice-shard-diplomacy.
989
https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/2965982/ice-shardfire-gem-concentration-combo.
https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/1717282/concentration-shapeshift-braevalars-skill.

https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/2692772/can-i-use-ice-shard-item-turn-converted-ranged-att.
991
https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/2434325/article/35259468#35259468.
992
Elixir needs to be played before the Attack Points are played because, otherwise, standard Attack points cannot be played during
the Ranged-and-Siege-Attack Phase (see also Vial of Toxin S2). This same restriction also applies to the black-mana ability of ALTEM
MAGES (see Bow of Starsdawn O5). In this Compendium, though, we suggest allowing the sequence to be more forgiving with effects
that are milder in their rule-breaking, as when allowing the play of Ranged Attack points/Siege Attack points during the Ranged-and-
Siege-Attack Phase before there are any valid targets for those attacks: see Combat S13 and S49, and Bow of Starsdawn O5. Read also
the endnotes to Bow of Starsdawn O5. https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/1542906/article/22110587#22110587.
https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/3122840/bow-starsdawn-and-altem-mages.

https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/2434325/can-i-use-ghostly-elixir-card-sideways-attack-1-ge.

1106
Actually this is a simplification. The site cards are single-sided; they were double-sided before. And in the Ultimate Edition two
sets of Skills description cards are provided for each of the first five Mage Knights, one with the cooperative Skill listed and one with
the competitive Skill listed. Previously there was just one Skills description card for these five Mage Knights, with a sixth Skills de-
scription card that explained the cooperative Skills for all of these five Mage Knights combined.
Also, the map tiles and many of the other tokens were printed reversed on the countersheets, so when they were punched by the
cutting die, the sharp edge of the cut is on the other side of these tiles/tokens. Some players find this a (very minor) issue:
https://boardgamegeek.com/thread/2982729/comparsion-material-between-old-mage-knight-and-ma.
Furthermore, I've noticed three changes in the Basic Action cards: Threaten still says "Reputation -1", but without the preceding
text "At the end of your turn". Similarly Noble Manners and Intimidate have had the same text removed. These changes, generally, do
not affect play: see Noble Manners S3 and Threaten S1.
